Run on CPU: the three seeded-failure commands, the dense toy and the two-process replica check pass, and every step of every capture carries the loss. `moe_dogfood.py` needs CUDA and was not run; its captured arm now includes one scalar copy per step, so the next overhead reading is not like for like with +0.70%. Router-only captures still carry no gradient norm: it comes from the per-parameter norms, which only full capture records.
  - *TrainCheck's figure.* The [OSDI '25 abstract](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi25/presentation/jiang) confirms 18 of 20 real silent errors detected within one iteration. It gives no number for older methods, so the focused plan's "at most 3" is not verified. *Settled Nov 17, 2026 from the paper (arXiv 2506.14813, p. 11):* the signal-based detectors (spike, trend and anomaly detection on loss, accuracy and gradient norm) "collectively only detect 2 errors", and PyTea/NeuRI detect 1. The figure for spike-and-trend monitoring is 2.
- [x] **One Rust copy, first step: `tensorquill-server detect` (decided and built Nov 16, 2026).** Python reaches the Rust detectors through the server binary, not a compiled wheel. This settles the open decision in "Focused plan: eight decisions", item 6.
  - *Why the binary.* It needs no new dependency, and the SDK wheel stays pure Python for the release. The cost is that offline detection needs the binary on `PATH` (or `TENSORQUILL_SERVER_BIN`) and `pyarrow`. A compiled wheel (PyO3) would make `pip install` enough, but ends the pure-Python wheel and needs a build per platform; the Rust entry point built here can be wrapped that way later.
  - *Built in Rust.* `detect::judge` reads one rank's rows from memory as `replay` reads stored parts, cutting batches where the attempt changes. `detect::report` builds the report for both the server's `incidents.json` and the new subcommand, so the two cannot drift. `tensorquill-server detect [FILE]` reads an Arrow IPC stream (standard input by default) and prints the report as one line of JSON. Rows of a second rank or run, attempts out of order and a stream with no rows are refused.
  - *Built in Python.* `tensorquill.server_detect.server_report(records)` sends the records and returns the report. `build_evidence(..., detectors="rust")` and `python -m tensorquill FILE --detectors rust` take the incidents of the 25 per-rank kinds and the `not_checked` entries from it. The default stays `"python"`. A binary that applies other thresholds, or lacks one of the 25 kinds, is refused.
  - *One rule ported.* A step written twice within one attempt is now `repeated_steps` in Rust too. The server's own store can never hold such rows, so the port had left it out, but a local file can.
  - *Tested.* Both paths give the same evidence on 57 record sets and the seven Gate 1 fixtures (`sdk/tests/test_server_detect.py`, 80 tests, in CI's server job). The record sets plant every one of the 25 kinds and every kind of `not_checked` entry. Four Rust unit tests cover the entry point. Run by hand through `build_evidence`, the two paths also agree on the seven GPU captures of Nov 15, 2026 and the six CPU toy captures made the same day. Five deliberate breakages each failed a test.
  - *Two differences, pinned by tests.* For a step written twice within one attempt, Rust attaches the scalars of its second writing and Python those of its first. Attempts out of order in the input are read oldest first, as a store holds them. A logged NaN is `null` in the report, so the summary reads "not finite" where Python's reads "nan".
  - *Not done: no Python detector is deleted.* Four things come first, in this order.
    1. The Rust rules take no options. `build_evidence` takes five (`overload_threshold`, `drift_tolerance`, `starve_window`, `starve_ratio`, `top_k`), and the Rust path refuses any that is not the default. Either Rust takes them or the options go. *Done the same day: Rust takes them ("Thresholds into Rust").*
    2. The per-step MoE `samples` carry the MoE rule's own verdict (overloaded, dead and starved experts per step) and are made in Python. The Rerun export, the expert-load table and the Gate 1 fixtures read them. Rust has to emit them before Python's MoE rule can go.
    3. The parity tests use Python as the reference. Its outputs must be frozen as golden files first. *Done the same day ("Golden files").*
    4. `--watch` on a local file and `--rank all` do not use the Rust path yet, and `duplicate_batches`, the cross-rank kind, still has a Python copy beside the watchdog's.
  - *Not measured:* the release binary's time (the debug binary took about as long as Python on 400 to 650 steps, most of it converting records to Arrow), and any run over a few thousand steps.
- [x] **Thresholds into Rust (decided and built Nov 16, 2026).** The five options of `build_evidence` move into the Rust detectors, as one JSON argument on `detect` and on the server, with today's values as defaults and unknown keys rejected. This removes the first of the four things that block deleting Python's detectors, and is R1 in "What is left to build".
  - *The argument.* `--thresholds '{"overload_threshold": 0.5, "top_k": 2}'`, accepted before or after `detect`. Keys: `overload_threshold` (0.35), `drift_tolerance` (1e-6), `starve_window` (50), `starve_ratio` (0.1) and `top_k` (`null` for what the run recorded, a number for every router, or an object of router name to number). A key left out keeps its default.
  - *Refused, with exit status 2 before the server listens or `detect` reads its input:* an unknown key, anything that is not a JSON object (an array would otherwise be read by position), a value of the wrong type, a negative number, and a `starve_window` or `top_k` below 1.
  - *In Rust.* `detect::Thresholds` holds the five. `RankDetectors::new` takes it, so `replay`, `judge`, `detect_stream` and the ingest configuration all pass one value, and no constructor is left without it. A `top_k` override stands against the value recorded on every row, not only when a router is first seen.
  - *Every report says what was applied.* `thresholds` in `incidents.json` and in `detect`'s output carries the five values, `top_k` among them (a new key), and a `replica_drift` incident carries the tolerance it was judged by.
  - *In Python.* `build_evidence(..., detectors="rust")` no longer refuses the options and sends them; `server_report(records, thresholds=...)` takes the object. A value the Rust rules refuse raises `ValueError` with the server's reason. `python -m tensorquill FILE --detectors rust --top-k 2 --overload-threshold 0.5` works.
  - *A reader that asks for the same five values now finds a server's report current.* `read_server_report` compares `top_k` with what the server applied. Before, any `top_k` given to a reader read `other_thresholds` and made it recompute in Python. Floats are compared to 1e-12 relative. That is a precaution against a value changing in its last place on the way through JSON text; no run here showed one change.
  - *Two gaps in that, found by hand and left open (R4).* The Python command line has flags for two of the five, so a server started with another `drift_tolerance`, `starve_window` or `starve_ratio` cannot be matched from it, and `--watch` recomputes in Python at the defaults. `--rank all` refuses `--top-k`, so every rank of a server started with a `top_k` reads `other_thresholds` there. The choice to make: the reader gives the values to match, or it takes whatever the server applied unless told otherwise.
  - *Tested.* `sdk/tests/test_server_detect.py` has 111 tests (80 before): 17 option values and one combination of all five, each on the eight record sets with MoE counts or a replica spread, give the same evidence on both paths, and each must change at least one incident in Python; a per-router `top_k` on five record sets; a live server started with thresholds, across three appended stretches and a restart, read by a watcher asking for the same values and by one asking for the defaults; the refusals; the command lines. Three Rust unit tests cover parsing, echoing and refusing (61 unit tests). The 80 earlier tests passed unchanged before the Python side was touched, which shows the defaults are today's values. Eleven deliberate breakages each failed a test.
  - *Left as it was.* `--rank all` still refuses `--top-k` (R4). Only these five are options; the other rule constants are listed in a report but cannot be set. The Python command line has flags for two of the five (`--overload-threshold`, `--top-k`); the other three are set through the API.
  - *Not measured:* the 64-rank detector benchmark has not been rerun with the options in place.
- [x] **Golden files (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session, merged the same day).** The Python detectors' outputs are frozen in `sdk/tests/golden/`, so they stay the reference after the Python code is deleted. This removes the third of the four things that block that deletion, and is R3 in "What is left to build".
  - *What is frozen.* One file for each of the 57 parity record sets and the seven Gate 1 fixtures: `incidents`, `not_checked`, `run_state` and the per-step MoE `samples`, with a hash of the input records so a changed generator is noticed. 64 files, 1.5 MB. The samples are there because R2 moves them to Rust and will be checked against these files.
  - *How it is held.* `sdk/tests/test_golden.py` (133 tests, in CI's server job) checks that the Python path still gives each file, and that the Rust path gives the same to 1e-12. `pytest tests/test_golden.py --write-golden` rewrites the files on purpose.
  - *Format.* Strict JSON with sorted keys; a NaN or infinity is written as a tagged value. Numbers are compared to 1e-12 and the record hash rounds to nine digits, because the files were written on macOS and another platform's `log` and `exp` may differ in the last bit.
  - *Checked at the merge.* The golden tests and the 111 differential tests pass together on `main` with the thresholds work in place. Changing one step number in one file failed both its Python and its Rust test.
  - *Not frozen:* the two cases where the paths differ on purpose (a step written twice in one attempt, attempts out of order), and nothing at thresholds other than the defaults.
  - *Not run:* Linux. The files were made and checked on macOS only, so CI's first run is the first test of the last-bit allowance.
  - *Found on the way.* `experiments/moe-bakeoff/artifacts/` is git-ignored, and `test_server_detect.py` read it, so that file would have failed at collection on a clean checkout. It now builds the fixtures from the generator (`6dabd75`).
- [x] **Compact evidence and the trigger (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session, merged the same day).** Focused plan pieces 2 and 3, in `sdk/src/tensorquill/compact.py` and `trigger.py`, with small calls from `watch` and the command line. A2 and A3 in "What is left to build".
  - *Compact evidence.* `--compact` writes `<input>.evidence.compact.json` instead of the full file: every key as it is, and of `samples` only `--compact-window` recorded steps (5) each side of a MoE incident. It is written instead of the full file because the 86 MB write is what is being avoided and the full file can be rebuilt from the records, and under its own name so a full file is never replaced.
  - *Size, as the side session measured it* on the benchmark's Granite-shaped run at 10,000 steps with two MoE incidents planted: 86.5 MB full, 195 KB compact, 22 KB at a window of 0, 3.9 KB with no samples at all. The proposed line of 1 MB is met up to a window of 28 steps, and stands.
  - *Trigger.* `--watch --on-incident COMMAND` and `--webhook URL` fire once for each new incident, keyed by rank, kind and step and kept in `<evidence>.fired.json`. Invariants fire by default, `--fire-anomalies` adds anomalies, and `--fire-kind` adds one kind by name. A change of run state never fires. *(Changed Nov 17, 2026: a stall is keyed by its run, and the trigger turns on the compact evidence; see Decisions: "The trigger and the watchdog: one firing for one stall ...".)* *(Changed Nov 17, 2026 by the author: one firing per stall episode, not per run; see Decisions: "One firing per stall episode".)*
  - *How it fires.* The command is started and not waited for, gets the incident on standard input and `TENSORQUILL_*` in its environment, and is never stopped by the watcher. The webhook is one POST with the standard library; redirects are not followed and the URL is never printed or stored. A failure is retried on later looks, 4 tries in all, and never stops the watcher. Nothing is started or sent unless the user gave the command or the URL.
  - *Decided in the side session, open to change.*
    1. `job_stalled` and `rank_silent` are observations, so a hung job fires only when named with `--fire-kind`. *Changed by the author Nov 16, 2026 ("Four open choices settled", item 4): a stall at `critical` fires by default.*
    2. In the all-ranks view an incident the ranks share fires once for those ranks, not once per rank.
    3. A kind this version does not know does not fire.
    4. The trigger does not turn on `--compact`.
    5. An incident counts as fired once its command has started, so a command that fails while the watcher is down is not retried.
  - *Verified.* 22 tests (`test_compact.py`, `test_trigger.py`; the trigger file is in CI's server job). The side session also checked 1,546 growing prefixes of the 57 parity record sets: no incident changed its step, so none would fire twice.
  - *Not verified:* a real agent as the command, an outside webhook, https, a proxy, Linux, Windows, and two watchers sharing one evidence path (the state file is not locked).
- [x] **MCP server and agent instructions (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session, merged the same day).** Focused plan pieces 4 and 5: `sdk/src/tensorquill/mcp_server.py` and `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md`. A4 and A5 in "What is left to build".
  - *The server.* `python -m tensorquill.mcp_server PATH` over stdio, where `PATH` is an `evidence.json`, a `.jsonl` capture or a run folder. Four tools, all read-only: `list_incidents`, `get_incident`, `get_series` and `get_all_ranks_status`. A test holds the tool list to exactly these four.
  - *No new dependency.* The `mcp` package is not installed, so the part of the protocol the server needs (revision 2025-06-18: `initialize`, `notifications/initialized`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`) is written out by hand.
  - *Bounds.* No reply is longer than 50 KB. A series is thinned by a stride that grows until the reply fits; anything else too long loses the tail of its largest part, with a note saying where.
  - *The instructions* tell the agent which tool to call first, how to name the step, the layer or parameter and the kind, what the three classes and "not checked" mean, and that it writes its diagnosis beside the evidence and takes no action on the run. The agent's other tools, such as a shell, are bound only by the instructions.
  - *Decided in the side session, to confirm.*
    1. The diagnosis goes to `x.diagnosis.md` beside the evidence; for a run folder, into the agent's working directory and never the store.
    2. A fourth series kind, `scalar`, for logged scalars and the run-level gradient norm, learning rate, data wait and step time.
    3. Layer and parameter values are read from the `.jsonl` beside an evidence file, or from `--records`.
    4. `get_all_ranks_status` on a one-rank path is an error, so it can never be read as "all ranks fine".
    5. Incident ids are `<kind>@<step>`, so an id survives a list that grows on a live run.
  - *Verified.* 29 tests: a scripted client using only the four tools recovers the kind, the first step and the layer, parameter or module of all seven Gate 1 fixtures. This is the plan's done test for piece 4 without a live agent.
  - *Not verified:* no agent session has used the server, so the done test with a fresh agent is still open. Only Python 3.12 was used.
  - *Left for R4.* `mcp_server.all_ranks_look` composes five private helpers of `evidence.py`, because no public call returns the all-ranks view without writing a file.
- [x] **MoE samples from Rust (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session).** The per-step MoE `samples` are made by the Rust detectors when asked, so no MoE rule runs in Python on the Rust path. This removes the second of the four things that block deleting Python's detectors, and is R2 in "What is left to build".
  - *The option.* `tensorquill-server detect --samples` adds one key, `samples`, to the report: one entry for every record and router, with the fields `build_evidence` gives (`counts`, `diversity`, `overloaded`, `dead`, `overload_rule`, `starved`, `starved_shares`, and `missing` for a router a step did not record). Every other key of the report is unchanged, and without the option so is the report.
  - *Not in the server's `incidents.json`.* Measured on 10,000 steps of 24 routers of 32 experts, top-8, from the bake-off's generator: 2,134 bytes without samples, 96.9 MB with them (240,000 samples). The server also keeps a rank's detectors for the whole run, so it would hold every sample in memory for every rank. The file keeps `moe_flagged` (how many samples were flagged, and the last step).
  - *In Rust.* Python lists a sample for every router the run ever recorded, sized by the most experts it ever had. A stream knows both only at its end, so the judged samples are kept while the rows are read and the list is completed as it is written. It is written straight to the output, never held as one document. `RankDetectors::with_samples` turns the keeping on; `RankDetectors::new`, `report`, `judge` and `detect_stream` are as they were.
  - *In Python.* `server_report(records, samples=True)` passes the option. `build_evidence(..., detectors="rust")` takes the samples from the report and skips its own sample loop and the starved check. A binary that gives no samples, or samples for other routers than the records have, is refused.
  - *Time, same run, release binary, a laptop under load.* The binary alone: 131 ms without samples, 324 ms with. Through `server_report`, with the records turned into an Arrow stream (1.8 s) and the output parsed: 2.2 s and 3.5 s. `build_evidence`: 5.6 s with the Python detectors, 3.5 s with `detectors="rust"`.
  - *Equal with no tolerance.* On every record set with MoE counts (the two of the 57 that have them and the five MoE fixtures), at the 18 threshold option sets and with a `top_k` for one router, every field of every sample is the same value of the same type as Python's. The golden files hold the Rust samples to 1e-12, their own rule, because a file may be written on another platform.
  - *One fix on the way: the JSON parser.* The first comparison failed on three fixtures: 392 of 840 recorded `diversity` values came back one unit in the last place off. `serde_json` rounds a number's text approximately unless its `float_roundtrip` feature is on. It is on now, for the whole server, so every number read from a JSON column (`moe_stats`, `layers`, `probe`, `data`, `checkpoint`) is the double that was recorded. On the 10,000-step run 110,227 of 240,000 diversities differed without it. Cost, against a build without the feature: 122 to 128 ms on that run, and 89 to 97 ms on 10,000 dense steps (20 modules and 50 parameters a step). No new dependency. The incidents had the same last-place error before; their 1e-12 comparison hid it.
  - *Tested.* `sdk/tests/test_server_detect.py` has 129 tests (111 before): the samples of each MoE record set, from `build_evidence` and straight from the report; that no Python MoE rule runs on the Rust path; eight ways to get one field of one sample wrong, each named by step, router and field; an older binary. Five Rust unit tests cover the output (66 unit tests). Two deliberate breakages in Rust (a token count one too many, `starved_shares` present before the window is full) each failed a unit test.
  - *Two differences in hand-made records, not pinned by a test.* Python applies the last `moe_top_k` a run recorded to every step, and Rust the value known when a step is read; this already held for the incidents. A recorded diversity that is not finite is kept by Python and worked out from the counts by Rust (tried by hand).
  - *Open.*
    - `router_names`, `top_k`, `expert_count` and `layer_count` in the evidence are still read from the records in Python. They are not rules, but R5 has to keep or move them.
    - `Cargo.toml` and one field on `detect` in `main.rs` changed; another lane changes `main.rs` too.
  - *Run by hand after both pieces were in:* eight recorded GPU captures (the seven of Nov 15, 2026 and an older Granite one), five of them with 15,600 real MoE samples each. Both paths give the same incidents, `not_checked`, `kind_checks` and run state, and the same samples with no tolerance.
  - *Not run:* Linux, where the live comparison depends on Python and Rust using the same `log` and `exp`; the 64-rank detector benchmark with the parser change.
- [x] **What was checked (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session).** Every evidence file gives each of the 32 kinds one of three values: `checked`, `not_configured` or `unavailable`. Only `unavailable` makes a run `degraded`, and the summary carries the count. This is focused plan piece 1, decision 2 of "Focused plan: eight decisions", and A1 in "What is left to build".
  - *The key.* `kind_checks` in `evidence.json`: for each kind its `value`, what it `needs`, and for the 25 kinds read from one rank's step records how many `records` carried that input. An unavailable kind also has the `check` and `reason` of the `not_checked` entry that names it. About 3.0 KB in every file.
  - *The rule.* A kind a `not_checked` entry names is `unavailable`. Otherwise it is `checked` when any record carried its input and `not_configured` when none did. Every `not_checked` entry already meant "asked for and could not be judged" and already degraded a run, so the two say the same thing and the server's `watchdog.json`, which reads `not_checked`, gives the same state without a change.
  - *A failed probe is new in `not_checked`.* A probe that raised was skipped in silence. It is now an entry with the check `probe`, the three attention kinds, how many probes failed and the first error. One failed probe is enough, as one failed memory reading is.
  - *The summary.* A line such as `3 of 32 kinds checked, 29 not configured`, after the incidents or "No incidents detected.", with the unavailable kinds by name.
  - *A rule that changed, open to change: a local file can read `healthy`.* Evidence built without a server's `watchdog.json` always read `degraded` (reason `no_status`). Now a `.jsonl` file or a list of records that no server was watching has `job_stalled` and `rank_silent` as `not_configured` and no such reason. A status is still expected, and its absence still degrades, when the source is a directory of the server's parts or one part, or when `watchdog=` names a status that cannot be read. `run_state` takes this as `status_expected` (true by default, so a caller that does not pass it sees the old rule) and `_per_rank_run_state` reads it from the evidence's own `kind_checks`. `--watch` on a store names the store's status file, there or not, so it follows the rule without a change.
  - *Tests that changed because of it,* ten:
    - A local file is `healthy` where it was `degraded`: `test_run_state.py::test_build_evidence_carries_it_and_schema_version_stays_1`, `test_mcp_server.py::test_a_path_that_does_not_exist_is_an_error_until_it_does`, `test_flight_e2e.py::test_a_resumed_run_keeps_its_repeated_steps_and_watch_reads_them`.
    - A store without its status no longer equals the local file it was streamed from; the comparison now names the missing status on the file's side: `test_evidence.py::test_evidence_from_parquet_parts_equals_evidence_from_the_jsonl_file` and `::test_watch_follows_a_growing_jsonl_file_and_new_parquet_parts`, `test_flight_e2e.py::test_a_capture_file_reaches_parquet_unchanged`, `test_two_ranks_e2e.py::test_the_command_reads_the_rank_it_is_asked_for_and_rank_0_by_default` (through `_without_status`), and `test_benchmark_detectors.py::test_a_timed_update_builds_what_watch_builds` (the benchmark's copy of the watcher's update now names the status file as the watcher does).
    - The summary has one more line: `test_compact.py::test_evidence_with_no_incidents_keeps_no_samples_and_reads_the_same`, `test_server_reports.py::test_the_incidents_of_a_current_report_are_python_s_and_the_summary_shows_them`. And `test_compact.py::test_the_cli_writes_the_compact_file_instead_of_the_full_one` bounded a tiny compact file at a tenth of the full one, which the 3 KB of `kind_checks` passes; the bound is a fifth.
  - *In Rust.* `detect::report` adds `kind_checks` for the 25 kinds the server has, so `incidents.json` and `detect`'s output carry it. `checked` and `not_checked` keep their names and shapes. The counts come from columns the detectors read already; no JSON is parsed twice. `build_evidence(..., detectors="rust")` takes the 25 from the report, refuses a binary without the key, and adds the other seven.
  - *Tested.* `sdk/tests/test_kind_checks.py` (17 tests, Python only): a router-only run lists the weight and activation checks as not configured and reads `healthy`, also from a real router-only capture of the toy model; a failed probe reads `degraded`; the status rule; the files; the count. `test_server_detect.py` (139 tests): Python and Rust give the same value, count and reason for every kind on the 57 record sets, the seven Gate 1 fixtures and seven more sets, with every kind reached as `checked` and as `not_configured` and the 11 kinds an entry can name as `unavailable`. Four Rust unit tests (70 unit tests). Making one Rust count wrong failed 41 tests.
  - *Golden files, regenerated on purpose.* `kind_checks` is frozen with the rest, so the Rust path stays held to it after the Python code goes. A script compared every file with its committed version: `incidents`, `samples` and the record hash are identical in all 64; each gains the `kind_checks` line; each `run_state` line loses the `no_status` reason and nothing else, but for three files. Those three (`attention`, `attention edge a`, `attention edge b`) have a failed probe planted, so each gains the `probe` entry in `not_checked` and names it in `run_state`. Ten files go from `degraded` to `healthy`, twelve stay `degraded` and 42 stay `unhealthy`.
  - *For an agent.* `list_incidents` of the MCP server lists the kinds by value under `kinds`, and its description and `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` say that "nothing found" covers the checked kinds only.
  - *Open.*
    - A count is of records that carried the input, not of rules that had enough history: `grad_spike` is `checked` from its first record though it needs ten. Calling such a kind unavailable until it can fire would be more exact and would make every run `degraded` while it warms up. Not built; only the attention kinds say "too few", as before.
    - Evidence the watcher builds from a server's report (`_report_evidence`) and the all-ranks view do not carry `kind_checks`, so their summaries have no count. The report has the key and `other_kind_checks` supplies the other seven; they belong to the lane that owns those functions.
    - The server's `watchdog.json` run state does not read the key. It degrades on `not_checked`, which gives the same state today.
    - A resume in a run that recorded no learning rate reads `unavailable` where "not configured" would fit the definition, because `not_checked` has always listed it.
    - The compact-evidence sizes in `sdk/README.md` were measured before the key; each file is 3.0 KB larger.
  - *Not run:* Linux; a real job; the Gate 1 harness with an agent (its fixtures' evidence and summaries now carry the key and the count line).
- [x] **Watch and all ranks through Rust (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session).** The parts of the evidence output that could not use the Rust detectors now can: `--watch`, `--rank all`, `duplicate_batches` across ranks, and a reader of a server's report that takes the server's thresholds. This is R4 in "What is left to build", and the fourth of the four things that block deleting Python's detectors ("One Rust copy, first step"). The default is still `detectors="python"` and no Python detector is deleted.
  - *`--watch` with `--detectors rust`.* The watcher judges through `tensorquill-server detect` whenever the detectors run here: on every rebuild of a `.jsonl` file, and for a server's store whose report cannot be used. `--compact`, `--on-incident`, `--webhook` and the `fired.json` state are the same code after the evidence is built, and behave the same. A missing binary or `pyarrow` is said before the first look, not at the first rebuild.
  - *Measured, one rebuild.* Medians of five on an Apple M1 Pro shared with other work (load average 45 falling to 4 during the run). On the benchmark's Granite-shaped records at 10,000 steps (285 MB), a rebuild through Python takes 8.1 s. Through Rust with the release binary it takes 8.9 s: 2.8 s converting the records to Arrow, 1.8 s in `detect`, and 4.3 s for what Python still makes (the per-step samples and the other keys, not timed apart). With the debug binary it takes 12.8 s, 5.6 s of it in `detect`. At 1,000 steps: 0.77 s through Python, 0.89 s through Rust (release) and 1.23 s (debug). On light rows (0.8 KB a step) at 10,000 steps: 1.04 s, 1.15 s and 1.52 s. So the Rust path is 10% to 23% slower a rebuild than Python today with the release binary, and 46% to 68% slower with the debug one.
  - *What a poll re-reads.* The file is tailed from an offset, so only new lines are read from disk. But each rebuild converts every record read so far to Arrow again, starts a new `detect` that judges from the first row, and makes the per-step samples again in Python, so a rebuild grows with the run, as Python's does. Not to, in the order of the measured parts at 10,000 Granite-shaped steps: (1) R2, so the samples are not made in Python (part of the 4.3 s); (2) keep the converted Arrow batches between polls and convert only the new records (2.8 s); (3) keep one `detect` process alive (1.8 s) and send it only the new rows, which the Rust side can already do for a plain continuation (`RankDetectors::continues` and `append`, as the server does for a live stream) and must start over for on a new attempt or a step written again.
  - *`--rank all` with `--detectors rust`.* The view takes one source, a run folder in a server's store (a `.jsonl` file and a `rank=N` folder are refused), and runs no detector: each rank's incidents are read from the report its server's Rust detectors wrote. So the option is accepted, changes nothing, and needs no binary. Nothing in the view was running a Python copy of a server kind; what it works out itself is `memory_imbalance` (a Python-only kind) and what the Flight Recorder and TrainCheck files show. `--recompute` with `--rank all` stays refused.
  - *`duplicate_batches` across ranks.* `tensorquill-server detect --run` takes the rows of every rank of one run, in any number of Arrow streams, and groups them by their `rank` column. Each rank is judged as `detect` judges one (`judge`, then `report`), and the ranks' batch-hash windows go to the same `duplicate_batches` function the server calls for a run's status. It prints the run's document: `reports` (each rank's), `duplicate_batches`, `incidents`, and `ranks_compared`, which tells "compared and clean" from "nothing to compare". In Python, `server_detect.server_run_report(records)` returns it and `server_duplicate_batches(records)` gives the incident, sending only each record's keys and `data`. One-rank `detect` is unchanged, and still refuses a second rank.
  - *Why the rank column, and not a file a rank or a store folder.* Every row already carries its rank, and the server's cross-rank code keys its windows by it, so no naming rule is needed and a caller with records in hand sends one stream. PyTorch's Flight Recorder analyzer takes the rank from each dump's file name in one directory, and TrainCheck's online checker takes several trace files into one checker; both come to the same thing, per-rank state compared in one process. A store folder was not chosen because a local capture is `.jsonl` files, not a store. `detect --run` also takes one stream a rank, so that layout works too.
  - *The reader and a server's thresholds, recorded as open to change.* A reader takes the thresholds the server applied, and a report is `other_thresholds` only for a threshold the reader gave itself that differs. So `read_server_report(folder)` with none given is `current` against a server started with any `--thresholds`. When a one-rank watcher has to judge here, it uses the value it was given and the server's values for the rest, so the incidents do not move with who judged them (and the trigger does not fire on a change of thresholds). The all-ranks view lists a refused rank as unknown, as before. The Python command line has a flag for each of the five (`--overload-threshold`, `--drift-tolerance`, `--starve-window`, `--starve-ratio`, `--top-k`), none with a default until typed, and `--rank all` takes `--top-k`.
  - *The evidence says what was applied.* For one rank of a store, `detection.thresholds` holds the five, whether the server or this process judged. In the all-ranks view, `thresholds` holds them, and `thresholds_by_rank` appears with every set and its ranks when the judged ranks differ, which a server restarted with other thresholds leaves until each rank sends again; the summary then says so.
  - *A public all-ranks look.* `evidence.all_ranks_evidence(run_folder)` returns what the all-ranks watcher writes on its first look and writes nothing. The watcher's loop and this call share one body, and `mcp_server.all_ranks_look` calls it in place of the five private helpers.
  - *Tested.* 216 new tests in five files, all needing the server binary: `test_reader_thresholds.py` (24: taking, matching and refusing through the reader, the one-rank watcher, the all-ranks view and the command line), `test_all_ranks_look.py` (6), `test_all_ranks_rust.py` (36: every test of `test_watch_all_ranks.py` again with the option given), `test_watch_rust.py` (77: the 57 parity record sets and the seven Gate 1 fixtures written a quarter at a time under a Python and a Rust watcher, compared at every look; five of the trigger's own tests again with the option given; the compact file; the command line) and `test_run_detect.py` (73: Python's `detect_duplicate_batches` as the reference). Four new Rust unit tests (65). Thirteen deliberate breakages each failed a test, at least one for each new file and one on the Rust side.
  - *Three assertions that pinned what this changes were inverted,* each with a comment: the no-argument reader and the watcher at the defaults in `test_server_detect.py` (they read `other_thresholds`, and now take the server's values), the command line's refusal of `--detectors rust` with `--watch` and `--rank all` in the same file, and the refusal of `--top-k` with `--rank all` in `test_watch_all_ranks.py`.
  - *Chosen here, open to change.*
    1. The threshold rule above.
    2. `top_k=None` is "not given", like the other four, so a reader cannot insist on "what the run recorded" against a server started with a `top_k`.
    3. A one-rank watcher that judges here keeps the server's values for the thresholds it was not given. A watcher that never saw the server's report uses the defaults, and so does one read without `--watch`, which does not look at the report.
    4. Ranks judged by different thresholds are all taken when the reader gives none, and the evidence and the summary say which rank was judged by what. The run state does not change for it.
    5. `detection.source` stays `"python"` when the Rust detectors ran here: it says where the incidents were worked out, and `"detectors": "rust"` names the engine.
    6. How ranks reach `duplicate_batches`: the `rank` column, behind `--run`.
    7. A step written twice within one attempt is compared across ranks by its last writing in Rust and by both in Python. This is a third pinned difference, beside the two of "One Rust copy, first step"; a server's store never holds such rows.
  - *Left for the merge.* The applied thresholds are not a top-level key of the evidence `build_evidence` makes, because a test holds the watcher's file equal to it and `build_evidence` belongs to the detector lane; its four existing keys list all but `drift_tolerance`. One line there would make every evidence carry the same `thresholds` key.
  - *Not verified:* Linux and CI; the release binary in the tests (it was only timed); a watched run longer than 10,000 steps; `detect --run` on more than four ranks, on a real multi-rank capture, or timed; the 64-rank detector benchmark with any of this in place. A `detect` that fails after the first look stops the watcher, as an error in the Python detectors does. The look at the reference repos was a few greps and short reads.
- [x] **R2, A1 and R4 merged (Nov 16, 2026).** The two side branches were merged into main the same day, and the places where they meet were joined here.
  - *`detect` takes both options.* `--samples` (R2) and `--run` (R4) changed the same subcommand. Both are kept; together they are refused, because the per-step samples are one rank's. A test holds the refusal.
  - *Evidence from a server's report says what was checked.* `_report_evidence` now carries `kind_checks`: the server's values for its 25 kinds and the reader's for the other seven, with a status expected, since the source is a store. A report from a server older than the key gives evidence without it, and its summary has no count line. In a look that comes before the server has written its status, the two stall kinds read `unavailable`.
  - *An old binary under `--watch --detectors rust`.* The check before the first look passes, since the binary is there; the first rebuild is refused with the message that says to rebuild it, exit status 2, and no evidence is written. A test holds it.
  - *One test of R4's met A1's rule:* a store judged here with no status has the stall kinds `unavailable`, so it is compared with `build_evidence(records, watchdog=<the store's status file>)`, as A1's own tests are.
  - *Open, left as it was.*
    - The all-ranks view (`--rank all`, `evidence.all_ranks_evidence`) has no `kind_checks`. It needs a rule of its own: a kind checked on some ranks only, ranks that are not judged, and the two cross-rank kinds, which this view does judge.
    - A status from a stopped server still counts the stall kinds as `checked`; the run state reads `unknown` there, so the state is right and the count is generous.
    - `build_evidence` lists four of the five thresholds at the top level (not `drift_tolerance`); the store's evidence lists all five under `detection.thresholds`, and the all-ranks view under `thresholds`. One place for all three is not chosen.
    - R4's table of what a rebuild costs was measured before R2, when Python still made the samples on the Rust path. Measured again on the merged tree with the release binary (medians of five, load average 7, the bake-off generator's records with 24 routers of 32 experts, top-8): 435 ms through Python and 401 ms through Rust at 1,000 steps, 4,500 ms against 3,877 ms at 10,000. So a rebuild through Rust is now 8% to 14% shorter, where R4's table has it 10% to 23% longer. The parts were not timed again.
    - Exact equality of the samples between Python and Rust has only been run on macOS.
    - The Gate 1 fixtures' evidence and summaries now carry `kind_checks` and the count line. If `experiments/gate1/PREREGISTRATION.md` fixes what each arm is shown, that is the author's to amend.
- [x] **Projects, run names and comparing runs (proposed and accepted Nov 16, 2026, for the first release; built and merged Nov 17, 2026).** The author asked for comparison across runs as in W&B, by project and by run name or ID, and accepted this design the same day with the words "i want this with first release". Read here as the whole design, pieces 1 to 4 of "Order and done tests" (C1 to C4 in "What is left to build"), so the first release waits on them; the author can narrow it to piece 1. It reverses two lines of the focused plan: "Not planned: run-comparison dashboards" and "Learn across runs", which that plan starts only after result 5.
  - *Scope.* Compare what only TensorQuill records: what was checked, the incidents, and the per-layer series. Scalar charts, sweeps and a web page of runs stay with W&B and MLflow, as before. The output is a file and a summary for a person or an agent, as `evidence.json` is.
  - *Read first (the sibling repos, code read, nothing run).*
    - W&B (`74bc21f32`): a run is addressed by entity, project and run ID. `wandb.init` takes `project`, `name`, `id`, `group`, `job_type` and `tags`, each also from the environment (`WANDB_PROJECT`, `WANDB_NAME`, `WANDB_RUN_ID`, `WANDB_RUN_GROUP`). The ID is 8 random base-36 characters and is the identity; the name is a label that may repeat. A project name may not exceed 128 characters or hold any of `/ \ # ? % :`, and the default project is `uncategorized`. `Api().runs(path, filters, order)` lists a project's runs, and each run gives `config`, `summary` and `history`.
    - W&B's `run.project`, `run.name`, `run.group` and `run.id` each return a field of the run's local settings (`wandb_run.py`), so reading them once at `init` is not a round trip. `run.step` is, and stays unread (`tracker.py`).
    - MLflow: the project is the experiment; `mlflow.search_runs(experiment_names=[...])` lists its runs.
    - LLM-Training-Debugger (`3f904c9`): `compare_runs` gives config changes and metric regressions; `localize_divergence` gives the first step where two runs stop agreeing. It uses only steps present in both runs and never interpolates, estimates the normal difference from the first 30 common steps, wants 8 times that for 3 steps in a row, needs 40 common steps, leaves out wall-clock metrics, and says so when it cannot answer.
  - *1. Identity.* `tensorquill.init(model, project=None, name=None, group=None, tags=None, config=None)`, beside the existing `run_id=`.
    - `project`: the argument, then `TENSORQUILL_PROJECT`, then the tracker's (W&B's project, MLflow's experiment name), then `uncategorized`. W&B's name rule is applied, since the name becomes part of a path in listings.
    - `name`: the argument, then `TENSORQUILL_RUN_NAME`, then the tracker's run name, then the run ID. A label: it may repeat within a project.
    - `run_id`: resolved as today and unchanged. It stays the identity, and it stays unique in the whole store, not only within a project: the store's folders and the server's dedup are keyed by it (`runs/<run_id>/`), and moving runs under a project folder would break every reader and every stored run.
    - `config`: an optional flat dictionary of hyperparameters, for the config difference. Nothing is read from the tracker's config.
    - *Other ranks.* A tracker's run exists on rank 0 only. The project and the name are run-level facts, so rank 0 alone writes them, and no new collective call is needed. The environment variables reach every rank anyway.
  - *2. Where it is kept.* One small run manifest, not a column on every step record.
    - Local files: `<run_id>.run.json` beside `<run_id>.rank<N>.jsonl`, written by rank 0 at `init`.
    - A server's store: `runs/<run_id>/run.json`. The Flight stream has no run-level message today; each batch carries `app_metadata` with `schema_version`, `run_id`, `rank`, `end_byte_offset` and `world_size`. The manifest would ride as one more optional key on rank 0's batches, and the server would write the file when it first sees it. `BatchMetadata` does not refuse unknown keys, so an older server ignores it.
    - Fields: `schema_version`, `project`, `name`, `run_id`, `group`, `tags`, `created_at`, `world_size`, the SDK and PyTorch versions, where the run ID came from (`explicit`, `mlflow`, `wandb`, `launcher`, `generated`) and `config`.
    - `evidence.json` and the all-ranks view gain a `run` block with the project, name and ID.
  - *3. Listing.* `python -m tensorquill runs <folder or store> [--project P]` and `tensorquill.list_runs(source, project=None)`: one row a run with its name, ID, run state, last step, incidents by class, the first incident (kind and step), kinds checked, and start time. It reads the manifest and the small evidence or report files only, never a Parquet part. A run without a manifest (every run recorded before this) is listed under `uncategorized` with its ID as its name.
  - *4. Comparing.* `python -m tensorquill compare A B [C ...] [--project P]` and `tensorquill.compare_runs(...)`, where each run is given by ID or by name. A name that matches several runs is refused with their IDs. It writes `compare.json` and prints a summary, in four parts:
    1. **What differs in the setup:** project, group, world size, versions and each `config` key that differs.
    2. **What was checked:** `kind_checks` side by side. A kind checked in one run and not configured in the other is said first, so "no incident" in one run is never set against "not looked for" in the other.
    3. **Incidents:** by kind and layer or parameter: in A only, in B only, and in both with each run's first step. It reuses the grouping the all-ranks view already has for one incident on many ranks.
    4. **Where two runs part (two runs only):** the first step, and the layer or parameter, where a series leaves the runs' own early agreement and stays out, on the loss, the gradient norm and the per-layer series. This part is for paired runs: the same job run twice, or a run before and after one change. Runs that never agree in their first steps (another seed, another model) get no answer from it, and the output says why. LLM-Training-Debugger's numbers are its own; a pass line here is set first on a healthy and a seeded run of `seeded_failures.py`.
  - *5. For an agent.* Two more read-only MCP tools, `list_runs` and `compare_runs`, over the same files.
  - *Depends on.*
    - `kind_checks` in the all-ranks view, which is open (Decisions: "R2, A1 and R4 merged"); until then part 2 works on one rank's evidence only.
    - A choice of which rank's records feed a series in a run of many ranks. Proposed: rank 0, named in the output.
    - Where part 4's rule is written. It reads two runs, which no detector does, and no second copy would exist, so one Python implementation does not go against "one Rust copy". If it later runs in the server, it moves then.
  - *Order and done tests.*
    1. Identity and the manifest, with the `run` block. Done when two runs started with the same project and different names are listed under that project by `runs`, from local files and from a server's store, and a two-process run writes one manifest. This is the one piece worth having before the first release: it adds a file and an argument, and adding a project to runs already recorded by outside users is harder later.
    2. Listing and parts 1 to 3 of comparing. Done when a healthy run and each seeded run of `seeded_failures.py` compare as "one incident in B only", with the seeded parameter, and a router-only run against a full-capture run reports the difference in what was checked before any incident.
    3. Part 4. Done when the same pair names the seeded step within one check interval and the seeded parameter, and two healthy runs with different seeds get "no answer" and not a step.
    4. The MCP tools, then (if asked for) two runs side by side in Rerun and the outlier among several runs.
  - *Not in this design:* charts of scalars across runs, sweeps, reports, a hosted project page, artifact and model versions.
  - *Accepted, so the focused plan changes in three places* (made in the author's doc and its copy here later the same day, at the author's word): the "Not planned" line under "After the four weeks", step 4 "Learn across runs", and one more build item with its done test.
  - *Where the pieces meet other work, read from the code Nov 16, 2026, nothing run.*
    - `init` is in `capture.py` and the run ID is resolved in `tracker.py`. The batch metadata is built in one place in `flight.py`, and `BatchMetadata` in `server/src/ingest.rs` has five fields and does not refuse unknown ones.
    - The command line is one parser in `evidence.py` whose first argument is a path. Both `__main__.py` and the `tensorquill` console script (`tensorquill.evidence:main`) call it. `runs` and `compare` are new verbs, so they need a dispatch inside or in front of `main`, and a rule for a file that is itself named `runs` or `compare`.
    - The `run` block has three homes, because evidence is built in three places that each set `run_id` themselves: `build_evidence`, `_report_evidence` (a store's evidence from the server's report) and the all-ranks view. `kind_checks` missed the second one until the merge of Nov 16, 2026. The golden files freeze five keys (`incidents`, `kind_checks`, `not_checked`, `run_state`, `samples`), so the block does not change them.
    - `test_mcp_server.py` holds the tool list to exactly four; C4 makes it six.

  - *C1, identity.* `tensorquill.init(model, project=, name=, group=, tags=, config=)` beside `run_id=`, which stays the identity and the key of every file and folder. `project`: the argument, then `TENSORQUILL_PROJECT`, then the tracker's, then `uncategorized`. `name`: the argument, then `TENSORQUILL_RUN_NAME`, then the tracker's run name, then the run ID. `group` falls back to W&B's group. Rank 0 writes `<run_id>.run.json` in the folder of its `.jsonl` file at `init`, by a temporary file and a rename; other ranks write nothing and make no collective call. Fields: `schema_version`, `project`, `name`, `run_id`, `group`, `tags`, `created_at`, `world_size`, `sdk_version`, `torch_version`, `run_id_source` and `config`. A run started again under its run ID keeps the first `created_at`. A manifest that cannot be written is a warning and the run goes on.
  - *C1, the stream.* The manifest is one more optional key, `run_manifest`, in the batch metadata, on the first batch of each of rank 0's streams (so a restarted server is told again). The server writes `runs/<run_id>/run.json` when the manifest is new for the run or differs from the one it last wrote, after the batch's rows are accepted; one that is not an object, names another run, is over 64 KiB or cannot be written is left out with a line on stderr and never refuses a batch. The folder comes from the stream's descriptor, never from the manifest. The sender sends a manifest over 60,000 bytes without its `config` (marked `config_left_out`, with a warning at `init`), since a large one would otherwise go with every stream and be dropped on arrival.

A run without a manifest reads as `uncategorized` with its run ID as its name.

One row a run: project, name, run ID, run state, last step, incidents by class, the first incident, kinds checked and start time. Local files are read from the manifest, the evidence file beside the rank's `.jsonl` file, and that file's first and last lines; a store from each rank's `incidents.json`, the names of its newest parts and `watchdog.json`. No part is opened and no detector runs, so a local run whose evidence file was never written is listed with its state unknown and a note, and one older than its records says so.
  - *C2, comparing.* `python -m tensorquill compare A B [C ...] [--source FOLDER] [--project P] [--rank N] [-o compare.json]` and `tensorquill.compare_runs(...)`, in `sdk/src/tensorquill/compare.py`. Each run is an ID or a name at `--source` (default `tensorquill-out`), or a path. An exact ID wins; a name that fits several runs is refused with their IDs. The result's parts are in a fixed order: `setup`, `checked`, `incidents`, `divergence`. `checked` lists each kind the runs differ on: checked, checked without enough history, not configured, unavailable, or not said. Each incident entry names the runs that show it, each run's first step and what it names, and for every other run `absent_from` says whether that run looked: `checked`, `not_enough_history`, `not_configured`, `unavailable`, `not_said` or `ended_before_step`. `compare_runs` returns the result and writes nothing; the command line writes `compare.json`.

Local files are judged here by the Python detectors with the default thresholds. A store is read from the rank's `incidents.json` when that report is current; when it is not, the parts are read and judged here with the thresholds that report applied, and the output says so.

This changes what `python -m tensorquill runs` meant for a folder named `runs`.

They read the folder the run at `PATH` is kept in (the `runs/` folder of the store for a run folder), or the one named at start with `--runs FOLDER`. A tool argument is a run ID or a name and never a path. On a store the tool opens no part: a run whose report is not current is refused with the report's state. Replies go through the 50 KB cap. `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` has a section on reading a comparison. `test_mcp_server.py` was changed on purpose: the tool list is held to six, and the instructions test looks for the six names.

    - MLflow's experiment name is read from `MLFLOW_EXPERIMENT_NAME` only. Looking it up from the active run's experiment ID is a call to the tracking server, and nothing at `init` may wait on one. So an MLflow run whose experiment was set with `mlflow.set_experiment` is filed under `uncategorized` unless `project=` or the environment says otherwise.

One change came after that run, a clearer refusal for a capture with no whole record and its test; after it only `test_runs_compare.py` and `test_mcp_server.py` were run again (54 passed).
    - Two runs of one project with different names are told apart from local files and from a real server's store, by one-shot evidence, the watcher, the report path and the all-ranks view; a two-process run writes one manifest; a run with no manifest still reads.

- [x] **Where two runs part (C3; built Nov 17, 2026 in a side session; merged and joined to `compare` the same day).** Part 4 of "Projects, run names and comparing runs". One new module, `sdk/src/tensorquill/divergence.py`, and `sdk/tests/test_divergence.py` (44 tests); no existing source file changed. `first_divergence(records_a, records_b, *, rank=0)` takes two runs' records as `load_records` returns them and gives plain data: `answer` (`diverged`, `no_divergence` or `no_answer`) with a `reason`, the diverging series with its step and both runs' values at the last common step before it and at it, every series that left, a table of what was compared, what was not comparable and why, what was left out, and the thresholds used. `divergence_summary(result)` gives the lines to print.
  - *Read first.* LLM-Training-Debugger's `localize_divergence` (`3f904c9`). Kept from it: only steps in both runs, no interpolation, wall-clock metrics left out, an early window, a multiple of the early difference, a count of records in a row, and "cannot answer" as a result. Not kept: its floor of 1% of the series' level when the early difference is zero (the stalled toy pair differs by 0.003% of the level, so that floor finds nothing), its scale (a median deviation around the median of the difference, then tested against the difference itself), and its lack of a test for runs that are not a pair (they come back as "no divergence").
  - *The rule.*
    - *Series.* Each logged scalar that is not a wall-clock reading, the total gradient norm (and the clipped one), each parameter's gradient norm and weight norm, each captured module's `mean`, `std`, `abs_max` and `zero_frac`, and for each MoE router its largest expert share and the share of its experts with no tokens. Weight norms are not in the design's list; they are compared because nothing else names a frozen parameter. Left out and listed: `ts`, `data_wait`, `step_time`, `system`, and scalars with a clock word as a whole word of their name (`time`, `ms`, `sec`, `throughput` and so on, a list; `val_mse` and `train/beta` are series). Other keys (`lr`, `probe`, `replica_spread`, `data`, `checkpoint`) are listed as not compared.
    - *The unit.* A difference between the runs is measured against the series' own movement: the median change from one record to the next in the early steps. A level-relative unit cannot work: two toy runs with different seeds differ in loss by 0.1% to 1.4%, less than two GPU runs of one job differ in a block's `std` (1%) or a parameter's gradient norm (3% to 5%).
    - *Early steps.* The first 30 common steps. A series' line is 12 times its largest early difference, and never below 1% of its own movement; that floor is the whole line when the runs were identical early.
    - *Left.* The first step from which the series is above its line on 3 common records in a row and on at least half of all its common records from there to the end. A stretch that comes back is counted (`came_back`) and is not a departure. Fewer than 3 records out when the runs end are reported as too short to call.
    - *A pair or not.* On each series that moves, the typical (median) early difference over the series' own movement; the runs are a pair when the median of that over the series is below 0.2. Otherwise `no_answer`, with the number. Series that never move (`zero_frac` at 0) are the same in any two runs and do not vote. A single series above 0.2 in a pair is listed as not comparable.
    - *Series recorded less often.* Weight norms come every 50 steps, so none falls in the early steps. When every series of the two runs is identical there, a series' first two samples stand in. When the runs differ at all there, a series needs samples on half of the early steps or it is not compared.
    - *The name.* The earliest step wins. At one step, a block's output comes before the loss, the loss before a parameter's gradient, that before the total, and among blocks the first in the capture's order leads, as `_first_in_forward_pass_leads` already does for one run. The others at that step are listed.
    - *Also said.* The first step at which any compared value differs at all (`first_exact_difference`): in the stalled pair that is step 101, on 36 series, by rounding, and it is not the answer.
    - *Not finite.* A value not finite in one run only is a departure, written `"nan"`, `"inf"` or `"-inf"` so the result stays strict JSON; the same non-finite value in both runs is agreement; one in the early steps makes that series not comparable.
    - *No answer.* No records of the rank, no common step, fewer than 33 common steps (30 and 3), no series in both runs, or not a pair. Each has its sentence. One rank is read (0 unless asked) and named.
  - *How the numbers were chosen.* Eleven pairs, each with a known answer, and one number moved at a time with the rest held.
    - *Toy pairs (CPU, `seeded_failures.py --toy`, 400 steps).* A healthy run against: itself run again (no divergence), `--case grad_spike` (step 250, the seeded k_proj's gradient norm), `--case stalled_weights` (step 149, the first weight check after the freeze at 100, the seeded k_proj's weight norm), two other model seeds and another data seed (no answer). The script has no healthy case and no seed option, so the healthy runs come from a helper that calls the script's own model, optimizer groups, batches and capture; it is in the test file.
    - *GPU pairs (recorded captures in `~/Downloads/runs`, read only).* `trainer-check` against `trainer-check-block`: one SmolLM2 job twice, with different faults seeded in `model.layers.16` from step 250, so the answer is known: step 250, `model.layers.16`, with 196 more series at that step; cut to the 250 steps before the faults, no divergence. Three pairs among the four Granite captures of `moe_dogfood.py` (650 steps): one job under different capture settings, each with the same router collapse seeded at step 450, so no divergence is the answer expected; that was read from the script, not checked on the GPU. `granite-full` and `granite` differ by about 15% in gradient norms from step 0, and why was not found. `dense-layer8` against `trainer-check` (another job on the same model): no answer. No pair was held back: the GPU pairs shaped the rule (below) and sit in every band.
    - *What the pairs measured.* Two CPU runs of one job are identical, value for value, until the fault. GPU runs of one job differ from step 1 to 4 on. The pair measure is 0, 0, 0, 0.003, 0.020, 0.035 and 0.037 on the seven pairs of one job, and 1.0, 1.0, 1.0 and 7.8 on the four that are not.
    - *The bands in which all eleven come out right, with the other numbers at their final values.* Early steps: 12 to 100 (wrong at 10; at 120 the freeze at step 100 is inside them); set to 30. Factor: 8 and up (wrong at 7: a Granite block's `std` drifts out late in the run); set to 12, since the faults at hand are too large to show what a higher factor costs. That lower edge, and the pair line's (0.04), come from the Granite pairs, so they rest on those being one job. Records in a row: 2 to 6 (1 names noise; 7 is more weight checks than a 400-step run has after the freeze); set to 3. Share of later records out: 0.2 to 1.0 (wrong at 0.15; real departures are at 1.0); set to 0.5. Floor: 0.002 to 0.05 of own movement; set to 0.01. Pair line: 0.04 to 0.9; set to 0.2. The floor and the pair line are the geometric middle of their bands.
    - *What the GPU pairs changed.* The first draft had neither the share of later records nor the rule for series recorded less often, and passed every toy pair. On the SmolLM2 pair it named step 92 (a block's `zero_frac` out for 3 records) and then weight norms at step 149 that drift apart by one float32 step; on a Granite pair, a router's share out for 3 records at step 141.
  - *Tested.* 44 tests: 39 on made-up records, 5 on the toy Llama (skipped without `transformers`). Each number has a pair just inside it and one just outside. Fourteen deliberate breakages were applied to the module one at a time, and each failed at least one test: the factor halved, records in a row dropped to 1, the floor halved, the share halved and dropped, the pair line doubled, the pair test removed, the early steps halved, interpolation of a run's missing steps, wall-clock keys and wall-clock scalars compared, a sparse series judged in a noisy pair, the largest change leading at a tie, and non-finite values dropped. The whole `sdk` suite: 714 passed, 554 skipped, none failed; every skip names the server binary, which was not built in that worktree.
  - *Open.*
    - The floor rests on one pair. In the stalled toy pair the frozen weight norm is 0.14 of its own movement off, and rounding differences elsewhere reach 0.002; the floor sits between. A freeze that moves a norm less, or a longer run in which rounding grows further, is not measured.
    - Two GPU runs of one job drift apart for good, and no fixed factor holds for ever: at 650 steps a factor of 7 was already crossed once. Runs longer than 650 steps are not measured. The result gives the size of the departure (times the line, and in the series' own movement) so a small one can be read as such.
    - A pair that is identical in its first 30 steps and noisy later (a GPU pair whose first differing value comes after step 30) would be judged on the floor alone. None was at hand.
    - In a pair that differs early, a series needs samples on 15 of the 30 early steps. Between 2 samples (wrong on the SmolLM2 pair) and 30, that number is not measured. So weight norms are not compared in any GPU pair, and a frozen parameter is named only when the two runs are identical early.
    - A fault inside the first 30 common steps is not found: it raises the line or fails the pair test. A change that lasts under half of the rest of the run is counted as a stretch that came back, not named.
    - A scalar is taken as a clock by a word of its name, so a clock with another name is compared (and listed as not agreeing early), and a series with a clock word in its name is left out. `lr` is not compared as a series.
    - The result of the SmolLM2 pair is 550 KB of JSON, most of it the per-series table (711 series); whether `compare.json` carries the table whole is for the merge. Computing it took 0.3 s.
    - A run before and after one change that alters step 0 (another learning rate) is "not a pair" by this rule when most series differ early, and the reason says so; whether `compare` should say more there is open.
  - *Not run.* One machine (macOS, CPU, PyTorch 2.15): CI's Linux jobs and its PyTorch 2.6, 2.10 and 2.14 were not run, and the stalled toy pair depends on rounding differences staying under the floor there too (5 times under it here). No GPU. No two real runs with a project and a name. No Parquet store: every pair was read from `.jsonl`. No run of many ranks beyond made-up records. No pair with a seeded fault on a real model other than the SmolLM2 one. nanoMoE with its balance loss on and off (named in "Trial jobs") was not run.

- [x] **A checkpointed hooked block under compile on PyTorch 2.6 (found by CI and fixed Nov 17, 2026; PyTorch 2.6 stays supported, the author's decision).** The first Linux run of the three lanes below (run 44204018813 on `bd173f5`) passed every job except the two on PyTorch 2.6, which failed the same 8 tests of `sdk/tests/test_review_fixes.py`, all with the `aot_eager` backend: six raised "one of the variables needed for gradient computation has been modified by an inplace operation" at `backward()`, and two recorded a step's router counts twice. The author first said to drop 2.6 if it failed, then to keep it and fix this.
  - *Why it failed on 2.6 only.* Dynamo marks every node of a block wrapped in `torch.utils.checkpoint.checkpoint` as "must recompute", the hook's read and write of its buffer among them. PyTorch 2.6's partitioner saves every graph input such a node reads for the backward pass, so the compiled forward saved the hook's buffer, though nothing in backward reads it. Read from the tensors a compiled forward saved: seven on 2.6, the last two the router's counts and its expert numbers; five on 2.9.1. The next write to the buffer (a second forward before the backward, or the zeroing at an optimizer step) then failed the saved tensor's version check. PyTorch 2.9 and later do not save an input backward does not use.
  - *The fix (`capture._NotRecomputed`, `capture._row_max_compiled`).* While AOTAutograd traces a hook's read or write of its buffer, the "recompute" mark is taken out of `torch.fx.traceback`'s current metadata, so those nodes are ordinary forward nodes and the buffer is not saved. The layer hook's one read of its buffer (the largest magnitude so far) moved into a helper of its own for this. Private API, so best effort: where the metadata cannot be reached the hook is traced as before.
  - *Checked.* PyTorch 2.6.0 in a throwaway environment on macOS (downloaded with the author's leave): the 8 tests pass, and the whole SDK suite gives 731 passed, 116 skipped (no numpy, pyarrow or server there). PyTorch 2.15: 902 passed, 575 skipped without a server binary; experiments 92 passed. On 2.15 and 2.9.1 the AOT forward and backward graphs, Dynamo's graph-break and frame counts and the records are identical before and after the fix, for a checkpointed and a plain block, with routers and with full capture. On 2.6 a plain block's graphs are identical and a checkpointed block's differ only in that the buffer is no longer returned for backward.
  - *Not run.* The real-server tests (no server build here; CI runs them), Inductor on 2.6, PyTorch 2.7 and 2.8 (CI does not run them), and a GPU.
  - *PyTorch 2.15's breaking changes, checked at the author's word.* The author noted that 2.15 changed distributed checkpoint metadata (2.15 checkpoints are unreadable on 2.14 and earlier) and blocks `importlib.reload(torch)`. Nothing in `sdk/`, `server/` or `experiments/` calls `importlib.reload` or uses `torch.distributed.checkpoint`; TensorQuill's own checkpoint manifest records what the user's `torch.save` dict holds and reads no PyTorch checkpoint. The release notes themselves were not read here.

- [x] **Claims corrected after the papers review (Nov 17, 2026).** A side session read the 22 papers in `researchpapers/` and the two SageMaker Debugger clones and wrote `Research papers and SageMaker Debugger review.md` (untracked). Its proposals 1 and 4 are claims this project made that the papers do not support. Each was checked here against the cited pages before the text was changed.
  1. *Silent data corruption is only partly covered by `replica_drift`.* The failure list said "same detector as entry 1". A corrupted gradient is reduced and returned to every rank, so the replicas stay equal: "after one wrong contribution has been incorporated into the result and returned to every rank, the corrupted value no longer identifies its source" (SCOUT, arXiv 2608.11034, p. 1; also p. 3). The "parameters drifted" result the list quotes is drift against a separate healthy node's run (Ma et al., arXiv 2502.12340, p. 7). What the check does see is a replica left different: corruption in the optimizer step or in memory, the BLOOM bug, a broken synchronisation hook. Changed: failure-list entry 2 and its summary row, and one clause in `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md`.
  2. *The reason given for router-only capture overstated its paper.* The README said the paper "proposes router-level monitors such as routing entropy, which router-only capture records". The paper measures per-token entropy of the routing distribution before top-k (eq. 9, p. 5) and says it can change "while the selected expert set and aggregate assignment counts remain unchanged" (p. 5), and that "aggregate load balance does not exclude this state" (p. 6). Router-only capture records load. Changed: the README's capture-mode paragraph.
  3. *Router weight similarity is not one of that paper's signals.* Its version 3 argues against it: similarity can rise with routing unchanged, and healthy checkpoints range from about 0 to 0.51 (p. 3, Table 1). Changed: the competitor table row, and the paper's title, which is "Mechanism-Aligned Observability for Training Instability of Large Language Models" (v3, Oct 18, 2026).
  4. *`abs_max / std` is not kurtosis.* The FP8 paper's kurtosis is per token, not centred, averaged over tokens (p. 5), and its early sign was on the query, key and value tensors (p. 6: about 3B tokens, against 12B for the gradient norm and 15B for the loss, in one run). Changed: the failure-list entry on activation outliers, which also said a fourth moment was "as cheap as" today's statistics while saying it would need a second pass, and the same sentence in the README's detector list.
  5. *TrainCheck's baseline is 2 of 20, not "at most 3".* The signal-based detectors "collectively only detect 2 errors"; PyTea/NeuRI detect 1 (arXiv 2506.14813, p. 11). The 18 of 20 comes from invariants inferred from reference runs at the level of API calls and variable updates. It supports checking invariants; it is not a measurement of TensorQuill's own 12. Changed: the note under "TrainCheck's figure", and the focused plan.
  6. *Two tools were missing from the alternatives.* SCOUT's `lm-resiliency` and SageMaker Debugger are added to the competitor table here and to the focused plan's table.
  - *Not changed, and open.* `kind_checks` does not say that equal replicas do not rule out corruption: that text is in the evidence of every run, so changing it moves the golden files and the Gate 1 fixtures. The review's other findings are untouched: `activation_drift` on a from-scratch run (predicted to fire, not shown), single-step gradient spikes, the `1e-6` tolerance, and split weights under FlashOptim. Its proposals 3 and 5 to 10 are not started.
  - *Not verified here.* ByteRobust p. 5, LLM-PRISM p. 9 and Altenbernd et al. p. 9 are quoted from the review, not read here. SDCHunter was not re-read. The SageMaker Debugger row was checked in its clone for the 500-step default (`save_config.py`), the one-worker default (`hook.py`, `docs/api.md`), the PyTorch versions and the last commit (Oct 31, 2023); the rule counts are the review's.

- [x] **Seven choices settled by the author (decided Nov 17, 2026).** Each was logged as open. The code for 2 to 6 was built in side sessions and merged the same day (entry "Six lanes merged").
  1. *MLflow's project name stays the environment variable only.* `project` is read from `MLFLOW_EXPERIMENT_NAME` and the experiment is never looked up by the run's ID. This departs from the accepted design ("W&B's project or MLflow's experiment"). Why: the lookup is a call to the tracking server during `init`, which can stall or fail a training start, and nothing in TensorQuill may do that. Anyone whose experiment is set another way passes `project=` or sets `TENSORQUILL_PROJECT`.
  2. *One refused router keeps the run `degraded`.* The MoE kinds stay `unavailable` even with a counted router beside it. Why: the user asked for that router and it is not being counted, so a collapse there would go unseen, and a good router beside it does not cover that. The reason must name the router and the two fixes; built the same day (entry on the router reason).
  3. *One stall is one firing.* The trigger keys a stall by run, so a silent rank that becomes a stalled job is one event, not two.
  4. *A killed job still fires, labelled as a lost connection.* An out-of-memory kill is worth waking an agent for. The label is there so the agent does not call it a hang. This narrows the earlier open item "any stall at `critical` fires, a killed job included": the firing stays, the wording changes.
  5. *`--compact` is on whenever the trigger is set,* so the agent that is woken always has the compact evidence.
  6. *A rank restored after a server restart gets a fresh start window.* This changes "the restart keeps the warning's clock" (entry "Enough history, the stall trigger and the startup bound merged"): a restored rank could come back `critical` and fire before its client had any chance to reconnect. The cost, a hang that spans a restart being called later, is accepted.
  7. *Gate 1 is judged on the evidence arm against stock Rerun with the same evidence.* Entered as an amendment in `experiments/gate1/PREREGISTRATION.md` the same day. Pass line: arm C's accuracy within 5 points of arm R's or better (at most 4 of 96 scored items behind), and C's median agent tool calls to a correct answer no more than R's. Every arm shown evidence gets the same `run` block and the same "without enough history" clause; a test already holds C and R byte-identical. The model and the time limit per trial are fixed before the first trial and are not entered yet.

A lone file of its own name that may hold several ranks is one whose first and last records name two ranks, or whose manifest's `world_size` is above 1; the trigger does not depend only on which rank wrote the last line, which changes from one listing to the next. The evidence file beside such a file is one rank's: the command line judges one rank of it (`--rank N`), and the file does not say which rank. So the row is `ranks_unknown` with `evidence.rank` null. Its incidents are listed, the run state is `unhealthy` if it has any incident and `unknown` otherwise, and what was checked is not counted for the run.

No detector is run and no file is read whole. A test fails if `build_evidence`, `load_records` or `compare._read_local` is called.
