- [x] **A refused router leaves no entry (decided and built Nov 17, 2026).** Closes "the router guard leaves a refused router with zero counts each step". Before, a refused router kept its slot, so every record carried `moe`, `moe_top_k` and `moe_stats` for it: eight zeros, `routed_tokens: 0`, `diversity: 0.0`. Both detectors already read a zero-sum router as a `missing` sample, and `input_counts` counted no MoE record for it. So no false incident and no history came of it. But the zeros read as data in the records, the MCP series and the dashboards. A run whose only router was refused also read `not_configured`, which is not the same as "asked for and could not be judged".
  - *What was built.* `_decode` in `capture.py` leaves a refused router out of all three keys. A record whose routers were all refused has no MoE keys. The refusal sticks for the session: the guard reads shapes, so a short batch (8 counts on 4 tokens) would pass a step that full size refuses, and that step's counts would be the same misread. A router with holes in its series would be worse than none. The one-time warning is unchanged. The first record written after the refusal carries `moe_refused: {router: reason}`, once per router per session. Records are what the evidence and the server read; the run manifest is written at `init`, before any forward pass. The key travels in `extra_json`, so the Flight schema is unchanged. `refused_routers_not_checked` in `evidence.py`, and the same entry in `detect.rs`, add a `not_checked` entry (`check: "router"`, the routers, the reason, the step). `kinds: [moe_imbalance, starved_experts]` makes those kinds `unavailable`, as a failed probe does for attention. A model with no refused router writes byte-identical records (a two-router run, routers-only and full capture, compared before and after with `attempt` and `ts` removed). The golden files are unchanged and were not regenerated.
  - *Taken from the reference repos.* moewatch's watcher (`_watcher.py`) analyses nothing for a step with no layer stats and writes no zeros for it. TrainCheck's checker (`checker.py`) counts and logs the invariants that were "not triggered" apart from the ones that passed. That is the line drawn here between `unavailable` and `checked`. Megatron's MoE tracker, W&B and MLflow were not read for this.
  - *What a reader now sees.* With a refused router, the MoE kinds read `unavailable` even when a router beside it was counted. This follows the failed-probe precedent. The run is then `degraded` (reason `not_checked`). The counted router's records still count as `records` toward history. A refused router no longer takes a `layer` number, so the routers after it are numbered one lower than before.
  - *Tests.* `sdk/tests/test_router_guard.py`, now 8 tests:
    - Only router refused: no MoE keys, `moe_refused` once, no samples, `input_counts["moe"] == 0`, both kinds `unavailable`.
    - A refused router beside a counted one, over two blocks: the counted one at 240 tokens on every step, history and samples its own, `layer` 1.
    - Under `torch.compile`, and a model with no refusal: nothing written.
    - Streamed to the real server with one router refused, then with all refused: stored equals written, and the Rust path's `not_checked`, `kind_checks` and samples equal Python's.
  - *Rust.* One unit test, `a_refused_router_makes_the_moe_kinds_unavailable_and_says_why`.
  - *Run, macOS.* Rust: 85 unit and 31 integration tests, clippy `--no-default-features`, fmt. Python: 1,321 passed and 15 skipped in one full run with `PYTHONPATH=src`, which the tests that start `python -m tensorquill` or a rank as a subprocess need when the package is not installed. Every skip is an optional package that is not installed (`accelerate`, `boto3`, `rerun`); none is a golden, kind-checks parity or server-detect test. The command line was run on a run with a refused router: a local file and a server store, with the Python and the Rust detectors and with `--rank all`. Each printed the MoE kinds `unavailable` and the run `degraded (not checked: router)`. The all-ranks summary names the check but, like any per-rank `not_checked` entry, does not print its reason. The experiment tests were not run: they need `rerun`, which is not installed here.
  - *Open.*
    - Steps already written before the refusal are not withdrawn. The refusal applies to every block queued after the hook found it, including steps of that block that ran before it.
    - A router given an `indices=` function is still not checked. *Closed Nov 17, 2026: see "An `indices=` router is checked, and the reason names the fixes".*
    - nanoMoE itself was not run.
    - CI on Linux was not run.
    - `compare` (`divergence.py`) lists keys it does not know under `not_compared`, so `moe_refused` will show there. Left to the sessions on `compare.py`; there is a note in the shared folder.
- [x] **An `indices=` router is checked, and the reason names the fixes (decided and built Nov 17, 2026).** Closes "a router given an `indices=` function is still not checked".
  - *The user's decision.* One refused router keeps the run `degraded` and `moe_imbalance` and `starved_experts` `unavailable`, even with a counted router beside it. The user asked for that router, and a collapse there would go unseen. What changed is the reason, which must name the router and the two fixes. Before, the warning named the fixes but the `not_checked` reason read only "router NAME is not counted: WHY". For a router that already had `indices=`, "pass `indices=`" was no fix.
  - *What is checked, by how the router is named.*
    - *Default.* An integer tensor in the output is refused when its size is not a whole multiple of the tokens (as before); its values are not checked. Without one, the top-`top_k` of the first float tensor whose last dimension is `n_experts` is used; those indices are in range by construction.
    - *`indices=`, new.* The function is refused if it raises (caught, never raised into training, under `torch.compile` too), or returns a non-tensor, a float, bool or complex tensor, a tensor on another device than the counts (which used to raise into training), the wrong size for the tokens, or any value outside `0..n_experts-1` on a step.
    - *A named inner module.* It is read as the default is.
    - *Still not checked.* Logits whose last dimension is not `n_experts` (that router records zero counts on every step and is not refused), whether a multiple of the tokens matches `top_k`, and the size check when the router's first argument has fewer than two dimensions.
  - *No new sync.* The value-range check is device work only. An `indices=` router's counts buffer has one extra slot, which adds the indices that match no expert (`numel - hits.sum()`). `flush` reads that slot from the counts it already copies to the CPU, and `_decode` drops it. Default routers keep the buffer and the device work they had; `_count_width` gives the width in all three places. Records are unchanged, so a router counted in earlier blocks is refused from the block in which a value left the range. That state is new, and it is tested in Python and through the real server. The warning is also said from `flush`, so a refusal found at `close` is not left unsaid. Not checked on a GPU that no sync was added; on CPU, three ways of naming a router (default, `indices=`, an inner module) wrote byte-identical records before and after, with `attempt`, `ts`, `system` and the timing keys removed.

Its expert loads are not recorded.") and the value of `moe_refused`. `refused_routers_not_checked` and `detect.rs` keep their wrapper "router NAME is not counted: TEXT" unchanged, so Python and Rust cannot drift and older records read as before. It holds no "; ", the join between routers. What each place printed on a refused-router run stored by the real server:

The Rust report gives the same.

It reads indices with `.cpu()`/`.item()` (a sync) and takes `n_experts` from the largest index, which hides out-of-range values. Neither is taken: here the range is judged on the device and refused.

The fix named is the one that holds: an `indices=` function that returns the indices on the counts' device. The inner module is not offered, since its logits would be on that device too. An `indices=` router on another device was refused already; its text is unchanged.
    - Logits whose last dimension is not `n_experts`, or a 0-dimensional first floating-point tensor, with no integer tensor (a block that returns its hidden states first): zeros on every step. Under `torch.compile` there is no `try` in the hook, since an op that fails fails the trace first; the checks above are what keep it from raising there, and a type they do not foresee could still raise under compile.
    - Warnings turned into errors (`-W error`): the refusal's `RuntimeWarning` raises out of the next `optimizer.step()`. That was so for every refusal before; it is new for wrong-width logits, which trained on with zeros. Not changed.
    - `torch.export` of a hooked model, strict and not: nothing raised and nothing refused, before or now.
    - No tensor in the output (`None`, `()`), a module called with keyword arguments only, a module called under `torch.inference_mode()`: none raised, before or now. - [x] **Evaluation forwards are left out of the records (found Nov 17, 2026 by the papers review; built the same day in a side session).** The capture hooks fired on every forward and their buffers were emptied only when an optimizer step was committed, so an evaluation between two steps went into the next step's record. `session.phase("eval")` only told the heartbeat.
  - *Reproduced, CPU, eager and compiled.* Three evaluation batches of 10 tokens between steps 1 and 2 made step 2's router counts `[0, 9, 15, 16]` (40 tokens) against `[0, 3, 3, 4]` on the others, and its `abs_max` that of the evaluation's inputs (207 against 2.18). The same with the phase set, without it, and under `torch.inference_mode()`. Inflated or skewed loads on the step after each evaluation can raise or hide `moe_imbalance` and `starved_experts`.
  - *What leaked, statistic by statistic.*
    - Router counts (`moe`, `moe_stats`, the evidence's samples): leaked, summed in.
    - Layer `abs_max`: leaked (a maximum over the step's forwards). Layer `mean`, `std`, `zero_frac` and the seen flag: leaked when the evaluation came after the step's last training forward (between `backward` and `optimizer.step`), since they are the last forward's, and a layer that runs only in evaluation got an entry of its own.
    - Forwards before the first step (Lightning's validation sanity check): went into step 0.
    - The probe: no leak. It runs in eval mode without gradients and the session zeroed the buffers after it; it now also passes the new rule. Its own hooks run only while it is active, and the attention statistics come from it alone.
    - Gradient norms, clipping, weights, the loss logged through Accelerate's `backward`: no leak. An evaluation has no backward and no optimizer step.
    - System readings: not this leak. The allocator's peak is the process's, read once a flush and never reset, so an evaluation's memory is in it by design.
    - Timestamps: an evaluation's time sits between two steps' `ts`, which is what the heartbeat's phase explains. Not changed.
    - `data_wait`: already counted only in the `train` phase, so without `session.phase("eval")` a hand-written loop's evaluation loader wait is still added to the next step. `session.data` and `watch_loader` on an evaluation loader add its batches to the next step's data record. Neither is a forward and the phase is the only signal there. Reported, not changed.
    - Adapters: the Lightning callback (validation and its sanity check), the Hugging Face callback (`eval_strategy="steps"`) and an Accelerate loop that evaluates with `no_grad` all leaked through the hooks, as any loop does. Checked through each.
  - *The rule (`capture._evaluating`).* A forward is left out when the hooked module is in eval mode and gradients are off; under `session.phase("eval")`, when either is. The two signals together are how Lightning (`model.eval()` and `no_grad` or `inference_mode` around its loop), the Hugging Face `Trainer` (`model.eval()` and `no_grad` in `prediction_step`) and most hand-written loops evaluate, so it works without the phase. Neither alone is safe: reentrant activation checkpointing runs its training forward with gradients off (checked: `torch.is_grad_enabled()` is False there in train mode; the Hugging Face default is non-reentrant), and some training keeps modules in eval mode (frozen blocks, dropout off). The phase alone never drops a forward: a phase left behind by a callback that missed its end would drop a training step.
  - *Taken from the reference repos.* SageMaker Debugger has TRAIN, EVAL and PREDICT modes, each with its own step count and save settings, but the user sets them (`hook.set_mode`); the PyTorch hook does not read `module.training`. That is what `session.phase` is here, and its separate evaluation records are not taken: for this change evaluation is dropped, with no new record type or incident kind. `wandb.watch` counts forwards with no notion of mode, so it has the same leak in its histograms. Lightning's evaluation loop sets eval mode and `no_grad`/`inference_mode`, which is the default signal taken.
  - *Unchanged for training.* A training-only run writes byte-identical records, with `attempt`, `ts`, `system` and the timing keys removed: the ten router configurations of "A router the counting cannot read …" (each eager and compiled) and seven more (gradient accumulation of three, reentrant and non-reentrant checkpointing in eager mode, a hooked block kept in eval mode with `capture="full"`, `capture="full"`, a probe every two steps, and a forward without gradients in train mode before each step, which is still counted), against `ff969f7`. No device work is added, so no sync.
  - *Dynamo counts.* Each value a hook reads becomes a guard; Dynamo always guards on grad mode but on a module's `training` only when something reads it. So with gradients on the hook reads the phase flag first and `training` only under the eval phase, and with gradients off `training` first. The training graph gains one guard, on the flag (a bool set on its own object from the start: a first version that kept it on the class compiled the training graph a third time at the first `set_phase`). Train, evaluate, train, evaluate, train, `torch.compile(backend="eager")`, unique graphs without TensorQuill and with it (two routers, `capture="full"`), no graph break in any:

    | Evaluation | No Dropout: unhooked | hooked | hooked, `ff969f7` | With Dropout: unhooked | hooked |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | eval mode, `no_grad` | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 |
    | eval mode, `inference_mode` | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 |
    | phase, eval mode, `no_grad` | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 |
    | phase, eval mode, gradients on | 1 | **2** | 1 | 2 | 2 |
    | phase, train mode, `no_grad` | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 |
    | eval mode, gradients on, no phase | 1 | 1 | 1 | not run | not run |
    | train mode, `no_grad`, no phase | 2 | 2 | 2 | not run | not run |

    The one extra graph is the cost of the phase covering an evaluation with gradients on, in a model where nothing else depends on the mode: that evaluation must compile without the hooks' work. A first version, which read `training` on every forward, added a graph whenever `model.eval()` was called with gradients on; the measurement caught it. Only the `eager` backend was run here.
  - *Tests.* `sdk/tests/test_eval_forwards.py`, 24, each comparing a run with an evaluation to the same run without it: the reproduction three ways (eager and compiled); an evaluation inside a step and before the first step (eager and compiled; covers the mean, std, zero fraction and a layer seen only in evaluation); gradient accumulation still summed; one signal enough under the phase; one signal alone without the phase still counted (phase alone, eval mode alone, `no_grad` alone); a hooked module kept in eval mode during training still recorded; an evaluation batch cannot refuse a router; Lightning validation with its sanity check; the Hugging Face `Trainer` with `eval_strategy="steps"`; an Accelerate loop. Against `ff969f7`, 20 of them fail; the four that pass are the edges that are meant to be unchanged.
  - *Run, macOS.* Python with the worktree's release server and pyarrow, no skips: 1,446 passed (run again after the guard order was changed; the same count before). The router-guard and evaluation files with `pyarrow` made unimportable: 79 passed, the 4 server tests skipped. Experiments: 92 passed. No Rust change, so `cargo test` was not run again. `git status` shows no change under `experiments` or the golden files after the runs.
  - *Open.*
    - Evaluation statistics are not recorded: left out, not kept apart. A record of their own, or an incident kind for them, is not built.
    - An evaluation in eval mode with gradients on (no `no_grad`), or in train mode without gradients, outside `session.phase("eval")`, still reaches the next step. So does `session.data` on an evaluation loader, and `data_wait` without the phase.
    - A hooked module in eval mode inside reentrant activation checkpointing is left out of training (both signals off).
    - A `session.phase("eval")` left set (a callback that missed its end) drops a hooked module kept in eval mode on the next training forward with gradients on, until `_commit` clears the phase; a router in that state writes zeros for that step.
    - Found on the way, not changed: activation checkpointing of a block holding a hooked module, inside `torch.compile(backend="eager")`, raises into training ("one of the variables needed for gradient computation has been modified by an inplace operation", the counts buffer). Seen on main (`3e63842`) and on this branch, reentrant and non-reentrant, with routers and with `capture="full"`; not with `aot_eager` (the path Inductor takes) and not without TensorQuill. Inductor itself was not run. Only eager-mode checkpointing is tested (`test_review_fixes.py`). *Closed Nov 17, 2026: see "A checkpointed block under `torch.compile(backend="eager")` no longer fails `backward()`".*
    - No GPU run, no Linux run.

With the new firing rule, that fires the trigger. *(Changed Nov 17, 2026: a restored rank is now judged from the restart, with a fresh window; see Decisions: "The trigger and the watchdog: one firing for one stall ...".)*

During the start limit (1800 s by default) the server cannot yet call a rank that has shown no step a stall, so for that stretch the two kinds say more than is true. The wait is in time, not records, and is the server's.

- [x] **Enough history (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session; merged Nov 17, 2026).** Item 2 of "Four open choices settled": a kind stays `checked` from its first record, a rule that is warming up does not degrade the run, and each kind now says whether its rule has had enough history. Too few probes is no longer a `not_checked` entry.
  - *The fields.* In `kind_checks`, in `evidence.json` and in the server's report, each of the 25 kinds read from step records gains `records_needed` beside `records`, and `enough_history`, which is `records >= records_needed` whatever the value. The other seven kinds have no count: their source is whole once it is read, so `enough_history` is true exactly when the kind is `checked`.
  - *The definition of "needed", one for every kind.* For each rule of the kind, take the fewest records it can report an incident on. The kind needs the largest of those, so from then on every one of its rules could have fired. A kind with one rule and no warm-up needs 1. A reader can check a number by hand against the rule's constants; `RECORDS_NEEDED` in `evidence.py` lists each kind's rules beside the table, and `detect.rs` has the same table.
  - *The numbers.*
    - `moe_imbalance` 11: the diversity drop of a top-k router (k of 3 or more) needs 10 earlier samples and the one judged. The share and collapse rules need 1.
    - `starved_experts` 50: a full window, and it is the `starve_window` the run was judged with, not the default.
    - `grad_spike` 13: one parameter needs 10 earlier norms and then 3 in a row. The total needs 11.
    - `stalled_weights` 3. `loss_scale_trouble` 10 (skips in a row; a scale below 1 needs 1). `data_stalled` 4 (the same batch: the batch and 3 repeats; no batch needs 3). `epoch_order_repeated` 4 (a pass of two different batches, and the next pass matching both).
    - `activation_spike` 11. `activation_drift` and `dead_activations` 60 (the first 50 records as the baseline and 10 more).
    - The three attention kinds 4: two probes as the baseline, then two in a row.
    - `memory_growth` 6. `hardware_errors` 2 (an ECC count above the attempt's first reading; an Xid needs 1). `input_starvation` 21 (the rise: the first full window as the baseline and one more step; the share needs 20).
    - 1 for the other nine: `non_finite`, `replica_drift`, `memory_pressure`, `allocator_retries` and `gpu_throttled` read one record, and `repeated_steps` and the three resume kinds compare with what earlier attempts wrote, which the count of records leaves out once a later attempt has replaced them.
  - *Why the slowest rule and not the fastest.* The first design took the fewest records on which a kind can report at all. It gave `moe_imbalance` 1 though the rule that judges a Granite router needs 11, and `loss_scale_trouble` 1 though the run of skips needs 10. A false "not yet" costs nothing; a false "enough" is what this item exists to stop. The price: a kind can report through a faster rule before it has enough history. The summary and the listing then mark it ("but reported by a faster rule", `reported`), and the incident stands. Five golden files show this (four Gate 1 fixtures of 4 to 9 records, and `edge low scale`).
  - *Confirmed by the author the same day: the slowest rule stays.* "Enough history" has to mean every rule of the kind could have fired, or the field gives the false all-clear it was built to remove. An incident is positive evidence and stands on its own, so a redundant caveat beside it is much cheaper than a wrong all-clear.
  - *Entered in the benchmark's amendments.* The five MoE fixtures of Gate 1 are 4 to 31 records long, so their summaries now carry the clause, and four show `moe_imbalance` as reported by a faster rule. `experiments/gate1/PREREGISTRATION.md` has it under "Amendments", with each fixture's line. No question, key or score changes.
  - *It is a floor, said as one.* `records` counts every record that carried the input, and a rule may count fewer: a parameter, module or router seen on fewer records has less history of its own; a total that is not finite or belongs to a skipped step is left out; the memory, hardware and data-wait rules start over at each attempt; an epoch must end before the next can be compared with it; a starved expert also needs enough tokens in the window. So false means the kind's slowest rule cannot have fired, and true means the count has reached what that rule needs. The attention kinds used the most probes any one module was seen on; they now use the count of probes that recorded attention, which differs only when probes do not name the same modules.
  - *Too few probes.* The `attention` entry of `not_checked` is no longer written, in Python or in Rust. A capture with probes but fewer than four reads `healthy`, with the three kinds `checked` and `enough_history` false. A probe that failed is still the `probe` entry, the kinds are `unavailable` and the run is `degraded`. The server's `watchdog.json` follows without a change to `watchdog.rs`: its run state degrades on the report's `not_checked`, and the entry is gone at its source.
  - *The 4,000th step, checked.* The capture probes on every `probe_every`-th step it commits, counting from 1 (`_cadence` in `capture.py`). A module's first two probes fill its baseline, the third is the first one judged and the fourth confirms it. So with `probe_every` 1,000 nothing can be reported before the 4,000th step of a run's first attempt, and the incident is then dated at the third probe, the 3,000th. A test runs a real capture of the toy model with `probe_every` 3 and finds probes on the 3rd, 6th, 9th and 12th steps, no attention incident possible at 11 steps and one at 12. Read and not run: after a resume the new session counts its committed steps from zero again, so the probes fall on its own 1,000th, 2,000th steps, while the rules keep the history of the probes that still count.
  - *Where a reader sees it.*
    - The summary: `6 of 32 kinds checked, 1 of them without enough history yet (grad_spike 3 of 13 records), 26 not configured`. The clause is there only when a checked kind is warming up.
    - `list_incidents` of the MCP server: `kinds.checked` holds only the kinds with enough history, and `kinds.not_enough_history` the rest, each with `records` and `records_needed`. The tool's description says "nothing found" is no verdict for those.
    - `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` has "checked, not enough history yet" as its own case, and says `healthy` does not mean every checked kind had enough history.
  - *Older files and binaries.* An entry without `enough_history` is never read as enough: `kinds_by_value` lists such kinds under `history_not_stated` and the summary says so. `build_evidence(..., detectors="rust")` refuses a binary that does not give the field. A report an older server wrote can still hold the `attention` entry, and is read as before.
  - *Golden files, regenerated on purpose.* A script (`kind-history-golden-diff-check.py` in the side sessions' notes) compared all 64 with the commit before: `case`, the record count and hash, `incidents` and `samples` are identical in every file. Every kind gains `enough_history` and the 25 gain `records_needed`. One file, `attention, the first probes`, loses the `attention` entry: its three attention kinds go from `unavailable` to `checked` and its run state from `degraded` to `healthy`. No other `not_checked` or `run_state` moved (11 stay `degraded`, 10 `healthy`, 42 `unhealthy`). In 19 files a checked kind is without enough history, 50 kinds in all.
  - *Tested.*
    - Every kind that needs more than one record (16 of the 25) has a record set of exactly that many records on which its slowest rule reports, and does not with one record fewer, either without the first record or without the last. `enough_history` turns true with that record. The 16 sets run through Python (`test_kind_checks.py`, 39 tests, 17 before) and through Rust (`test_server_detect.py`, 155 tests), where both paths must also give the same evidence.
    - The numbers are written by hand in a Python test and in a Rust unit test, and the Python test reads the Rust list from the source, so a number that is wrong in both tables fails too.
    - Python and Rust give the same `records_needed` and `enough_history` for every kind on the 57 record sets, the seven Gate 1 fixtures and seven more sets. Every kind is seen with enough history, and every kind that needs more than one record without.
    - Too few probes reads `healthy` in Python, in the Rust report and in a running server's `watchdog.json`, and a failed probe reads `degraded` in all three (`test_run_state.py` starts a server for each).
    - Two Rust unit tests are new (76 unit tests, 28 integration tests, 1 ignored as before).
    - Tests that pinned the old behaviour and changed: `test_attention.py::test_too_few_probes_are_listed_as_not_checked` (now "checked without enough history and not listed") and the server comparison in the same file, which compared the `attention` entry after every stretch and now compares the attention kinds' `kind_checks`; `test_kind_checks.py::test_too_few_probes_to_judge_are_unavailable_under_their_own_entry` (now healthy); `test_run_state.py::test_what_a_rank_could_not_check_makes_the_run_degraded_in_both`, whose cause was two probes and is now a failed probe, with a new twin for the healthy case; in `test_server_detect.py`, the set "probes, too few" reads `healthy` and the cases must not list an `attention` check; `test_mcp_server.py::test_a_path_that_does_not_exist_is_an_error_until_it_does`, where a 30-step file has activation drift under `not_enough_history`.
  - *Breakages tried, each restored after.* Eight, and each failed tests.
    - In Rust: `grad_spike` needing 11 (2 unit tests, 125 parity tests, the Rust half of all 64 golden files); a kind ready one record early (1 unit test, 126 parity tests, 64 golden files); `starved_experts` taking the default window in place of the one applied (1 unit test and 6 parity tests, no golden file, since the golden files use the default window).
    - In Python: `activation_drift` needing 50 (3 kind-check tests, 126 parity tests, the Python half of all 64 golden files); enough history from the first record (21 kind-check tests, 72 parity tests, 20 golden files); the listing putting a warming-up kind under `checked` (17 kind-check tests, 16 parity tests, 1 MCP test); the activation spike rule waiting two records longer than the table says, which changes no table (the boundary test in Python and in the parity file, and nothing else); too few probes as a `not_checked` entry again (5 kind-check tests, 8 parity tests, 1 golden file, 2 attention tests).
  - *Run by hand.* The eight recorded GPU captures (the seven of Nov 15, 2026 and an older Granite one), through both paths. Whole, the two give the same `kind_checks`, `not_checked` and run state; cut to their first 5, 12, 40, 59 and 60 records, the same `kind_checks` (only that key was compared on the cut ones). A full Granite capture cut to 5 records reads `healthy` with "9 of 32 kinds checked, 6 of them without enough history yet (moe_imbalance 5 of 11 records, starved_experts 5 of 50, grad_spike 5 of 13, activation_spike 5 of 11, activation_drift 5 of 60, dead_activations 5 of 60)", and from 60 records on the clause is gone.
  - *Open.*
    - The run state has no reason for a rule that is warming up, so `Run state: healthy` on a run of five steps says nothing about it. The summary's count line and the `kinds` listing do. A reason with the state `healthy`, as observations have, would need the same in `watchdog.rs`.
    - `enough_history` holds a count against a fixed number. It does not follow each parameter's, module's or router's own history, or a rule that started over at a new attempt.
    - `kind_checks` is 4.2 KB as compact JSON in every evidence file and report, where it was 3.0 KB (4,239 bytes against 3,023 on a router-only run). The compact-evidence sizes in `sdk/README.md` were not measured again.
    - The all-ranks view still does not carry `kind_checks` (A6). The title of "Four open choices settled" and the build table still say item 2 is not built; this entry changes no other.
  - *Not run:* Linux; a GPU or a real job; the Gate 1 harness with an agent; the 64-rank detector benchmark; a server older than the field against this reader, beyond a report with the field taken out in a test.
- [x] **What was checked in the all-ranks view (built Nov 17, 2026 in a side session; merged the same day).** Row A6 of "What is left to build", and the open item of "R2, A1 and R4 merged". The all-ranks evidence (`--rank all`, `evidence.all_ranks_evidence`, the MCP tool `get_all_ranks_status`) now carries a `kind_checks` for the whole run, its summary has the count line, and an agent can tell "nothing found" from "not looked for" on a run of many ranks. It is made from the ranks' reports alone: no detector runs and no part is opened, as before. One rank's `kind_checks`, its count line and the golden files are unchanged. Branch `claude/competent-albattani-27b3f1`.
  - *The rule* (`run_kind_checks` in `sdk/src/tensorquill/evidence.py`).
    1. Only judged ranks count. A rank whose report is not current is in no value and no list, and the count line names it as not counted.
    2. A kind read from one rank's step records (the server's 25) takes its worst judged rank's value: `unavailable` if any judged rank is, else `not_configured` if any is, else `checked`. So `checked` means every judged rank checked it, and "nothing found" for it is said of every judged rank.
    3. When the judged ranks differ, the entry lists them by value under `ranks`. A kind that reads `not_configured` or `unavailable` for the run can so be checked, and even reported, on the ranks named there. The count line marks it: "1 of them checked on some ranks only (stalled_weights on rank 0)", with "without enough history yet" when those ranks are still warming up.
    4. History the same way. `enough_history` is false when any judged rank is short, with the ranks under `not_enough_history_ranks`; it is left out when none is short and some rank's report does not say, which `kind_history` reads as `not_stated` and never as enough. `records` is the fewest any judged rank has and `records_needed` the most any needs.
    5. `job_stalled`, `rank_silent` and the three file kinds belong to the run: `other_kind_checks` of the run's status, which is expected because the source is a store, and of the files given.
    6. The two cross-rank kinds follow rule 2 over what each judged rank brings to the comparison, and need two ranks. `memory_imbalance` is `checked` when every judged rank's report has a peak memory reading and there are at least two. `duplicate_batches` is `checked` when every judged rank recorded `data`, there are at least two, and a status was read; without the status it is `unavailable`.
    7. With no judged rank, or with a judged rank whose report has no usable `kind_checks` (a server older than the key wrote it), there is no run-level object and no count. `kind_checks_not_stated` gives the reason and the ranks, and the summary says "What was checked is not stated". A count over only the ranks that do say would read as the run's.
    8. `unavailable` on one rank makes the kind `unavailable` for the run, and the run is `degraded`. `run_state` was not changed: a judged rank's `not_checked` entry already degraded the all-ranks state, and a missing status already gave `no_status`. The two now say the same thing.
  - *Why the worst rank and not a fourth value.* The dangerous reading is "checked, nothing found, so clean" for a rank that never looked. Taking the worst rank cannot give it. The cost is that a kind can read `not_configured` for the run while one rank checks it; the `ranks` list, the count line and `differs_by_rank` say so each time. Keeping three values lets `checked_counts`, `kinds_by_value` and `kind_history` read the run's object as they read one rank's, which the run comparison (C2) needs.
  - *The shape.* `kind_checks` has all 32 kinds with `value`, `enough_history` and `needs` as before; the 25 also have `records` and `records_needed`. Added only where they apply: `ranks` (the judged ranks by value, when they differ), `not_enough_history_ranks`, `history_not_stated_ranks`, `check` and `reason` (the first unavailable rank's), and a `note` on `duplicate_batches`. Each judged rank's entry has `kind_counts`, the count of its own report's 25 kinds, so the rank that differs can be seen. `view_version` stays 1: keys were added and none changed.
  - *The count line.* "6 of 32 kinds checked on the 3 judged ranks of 4 (rank 3 not judged, so not counted), 1 of them without enough history yet (grad_spike 5 of 13 records on rank 2), 26 not configured, 1 of them checked on some ranks only (stalled_weights on rank 0)". A rank is named only where the judged ranks differ, at most six kinds are named in one part, and rank lists are cut to four ranges.
  - *For an agent.* `get_all_ranks_status` and `list_incidents` on a run folder give `kinds`: the listing by value, plus `differs_by_rank`, `judged_ranks` and `ranks_not_counted`; or `kinds_not_stated` in its place. Both tool descriptions and `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` (section "A run folder") say how to read it.
  - *Tests.* 15 new tests in `sdk/tests/test_kind_checks.py` on stores written by hand, each read three ways (`all_ranks_evidence`, the watcher's file, `get_all_ranks_status`): weights on one rank, a rank that is not judged, no judged rank, a rank short of history, one rank unavailable, a kind checked only on ranks that are still warming up, a report without `kind_checks`, a report without `enough_history`, no status, both cross-rank kinds. One new test with the real server's reports in `test_watch_all_ranks.py` (run again by `test_all_ranks_rust.py`) and one over the MCP protocol.
  - *Tests that changed,* ten, all because the all-ranks summary has one more line: nine in `test_watch_all_ranks.py` and `test_the_all_ranks_status_of_a_two_rank_run_folder` in `test_mcp_server.py`, whose hand-written reports have no `kind_checks` and so now assert "not stated". `test_all_ranks_look.py::test_the_public_look_is_what_the_watcher_writes_first` gained assertions and lost none.
  - *Deliberate breakages,* eleven, each failing at least one test of `test_kind_checks.py`: take the best rank's value; count a rank that is not judged; ignore a short rank's history; read "not stated" as enough; count despite a report without `kind_checks`; put `not_configured` above `unavailable`; let one rank count as a comparison; call `duplicate_batches` checked without a status; leave the unknown ranks out of the line; leave out that a partly checked kind is still warming up; drop the `ranks` lists.
  - *Open.*
    - `duplicate_batches` reads `checked` from what the ranks recorded, not from what the server compared. The server writes the comparison into its status only when it finds a duplicate (`with_data` in `server/src/ingest.rs`), and compares only the ranks whose stores its detectors have read since it started. After a restart, "compared, none found" and "not compared" look the same from the status. The entry carries a `note` that says so. Closing it needs a Rust change, the status carrying `ranks_compared` always; it was not made, because the task expected none and this affects one entry.
    - The count behind that entry is of records with a `data` mapping, not of records with a `batch_hash`.
    - A status that is old, or from a stopped server, still counts the stall kinds as `checked` while the run state reads `unknown`. This is the same as for one rank ("R2, A1 and R4 merged", open).
    - When ranks were judged with different `starve_window` values, `records` and `records_needed` of `starved_experts` can come from different ranks.
    - The row A6 in "What is left to build" and the title of "R2, A1 and R4 merged" still say this is open; this entry changes no other.
    - Another branch adds a `run` key to the same view, and C2 reads this object for a run of many ranks; neither was tried together with this.
  - *Run, macOS, this worktree's debug binary.* Python, whole suite in `sdk/`: 1,241 passed and 1 failed, none skipped (9 min 33 s). The failure was `test_mcp_server.py::test_the_agent_instructions_name_the_four_tools_and_offer_no_action_on_the_run`: a line this work added to `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` named `duplicate_batches` without its class, which that test forbids. One word was added to the line, and `test_mcp_server.py` then passes (30 tests); the whole suite was not run again after that one-word change. `pytest experiments`: 81 passed. The trigger's tests (`test_trigger.py`, `test_starting_bound.py`, `test_compact.py`) pass unchanged. By hand: `python -m tensorquill <run folder> --rank all` on a three-rank store from the real server prints the count line; that all-ranks file is 7.2 KB.
  - *Not run:* `cargo test`, clippy and fmt (no Rust file changed; the binary was built from this tree with `cargo build --offline`); Linux and CI; the release binary; a real multi-rank job or a GPU; more than 64 ranks; the Gate 1 harness with an agent; the eleven breakages against any file but `test_kind_checks.py`.
- [x] **A stall at critical fires the trigger, and one line says when the thresholds are not the defaults (built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session; merged Nov 17, 2026).** Items 4 and 1 of "Four open choices settled". Python and documentation only: `sdk/src/tensorquill/trigger.py`, three small functions and two calls in `evidence.py`, the README and the agent instructions. No Rust change was needed, and none was made.
  - *The firing rule.* A `job_stalled` or `rank_silent` incident whose `severity` is `critical` fires with no option. One at `warning` fires only when named with `--fire-kind`, as before. The rule is in `Trigger._fires`, which now reads the incident and not only its kind. `INCIDENT_CLASSES`, the run state and the golden files are untouched: the two kinds stay observations, and the command and the webhook get `observation` as the class.
  - *Once.* The key is still rank, kind and step. A stall named with `--fire-kind` fires at `warning` and not again at `critical`. One not named fires once, at `critical`. A restarted watcher reads `fired.json` and fires neither again. Retries and the state file are the same code as before. *(Changed Nov 17, 2026: a stall is keyed by its run; see Decisions: "The trigger and the watchdog: one firing for one stall ...".)* *(Changed Nov 17, 2026 by the author: one firing per stall episode, not per run; see Decisions: "One firing per stall episode".)*
  - *A declared pause cannot fire.* The server gives a severity only to a rank that is silent, unresponsive or lost (`run_status` in `watchdog.rs`, whose own test holds a paused rank's severity null), and an incident's severity is taken from its stopped ranks alone, in Rust (`progress_incidents`) and in Python (`_progress_incidents`). A test hands the Python reader a status that calls a paused rank critical, which the server never writes, and the incident stays a warning. So nothing was added to the trigger for it.
  - *How `critical` reaches the watcher of a job that sends nothing.* It was not known whether the server rewrites `watchdog.json` while no rank sends. It does. The server looks at each run every `--watchdog-seconds` on a timer of its own (`spawn_watchdog` in `ingest.rs`, which does not wait for anything to arrive), and writes the file when the status changed, each rank's severity being part of what it compares (`write_statuses` in `ingest.rs`), and at least every third look. The watcher reads the status on every look and writes the evidence again when a rank's severity changed (`_status_stamp`), then hands it to the trigger. With the defaults that is a 10 s look and a 2 s poll after the rank passes 900 s.
  - *Proved with a real server, twice.* One test starts `tensorquill-server` with `--silent-seconds 2 --critical-seconds 10`, a real training process that takes 300 steps and then stops stepping while its process, its stream and its heartbeats live, and two watchers through the command line with `--on-incident` and no `--fire-kind`: one rank's, and `--rank all`. While both evidence files list the stall at `warning`, nothing has started and `fired.json` is empty. The status is seen written three more times while no step arrives (the rank's heartbeats do, every 0.5 s). Each watcher's command then starts once, with `severity: critical`, `suspected_hang`, and `stuck_at` naming the function the rank is stuck in. More rewrites of the status and a restart of each watcher start nothing more. A second test holds two streams open on which nothing arrives at all, not a step and not a heartbeat (senders inside the test, `--silent-seconds 1 --critical-seconds 4`): the server's timer alone takes the stall from `warning` to `critical`, the all-ranks watcher writes each, and its command starts once, after the look that wrote `critical`.
  - *The thresholds line.* `evidence.thresholds_line(evidence)` returns one line that names each applied threshold that is not the default, with its value and the default, or None when all five are the defaults: `Thresholds that are not the defaults: the server applied overload_threshold 0.5 (default: 0.35), top_k 2 (default: what the run recorded).` `incident_summary` ends with it, for one rank's evidence and for the all-ranks view, so the command line prints it for every look it reports. It reads what the evidence already lists as applied: `detection.thresholds`, and `thresholds` or every set of `thresholds_by_rank` with its ranks. When the detectors ran here it says so in place of "the server applied". Values are compared as the reader compares them (`_same_threshold`), so a default that came back from JSON one unit in the last place apart prints nothing.
  - *At the defaults nothing changed.* No golden file holds summary text, and the Gate 1 test compares `summary.txt` with `incident_summary` computed in the same run. A test holds the summary at the defaults equal, line for line, to the summary with one threshold changed less its last line.
  - *Read plainly, open to narrowing.*
    1. Any stall at `critical` fires, whatever its `assessment`. A job that was killed without closing its streams is `lost_connection`, critical 900 s later, and fires.
    2. A stall whose kind or step changes is another incident and fires again, both for one hang: a silent rank at `critical` whose job then stops altogether (`rank_silent`, then `job_stalled`, already critical on its first look), and a second rank that falls silent at an earlier step, which moves the step of `rank_silent`. A test pins both. The earlier hand-over excused the moving step because the kind fired only by name; that no longer holds.
    3. Ranks that wait in `train` while another rank runs a long declared evaluation are silent, not paused, and fire at `critical`. The rank in the pause does not. Read in `watchdog.rs`, pinned in Python with a made status, not run with real ranks.
    4. A pause the server stops believing (no sign of work for `--pause-seconds`, 1800 by default, or a work counter that stopped) makes the rank silent with its quiet time counted from its last training step, so it is `critical` at once, with no warning first. Read in `watchdog.rs`, not run.
    5. A `watchdog.json` left at `critical` by a server that was killed fires once for a watcher started later. The evidence calls the status old; the incident is listed all the same. A test pins it.
    6. The line is printed only for evidence from a server's store, which is short of "whenever": a non-default option typed for a local `.jsonl` file, or for a read of a store without `--watch`, prints no line. The reader gave that option itself, so nothing is silent there. That evidence has no `detection.thresholds`; it holds three of the five as keys of their own (`overload_threshold`, `starve_window`, `starve_ratio`), `top_k` only as what each router resolved to, and no `drift_tolerance`, so a line from it would be silent on two. Naming all five there needs a `thresholds` key in `build_evidence`, which the golden files freeze.
    7. In the all-ranks view with `thresholds_by_rank`, a set at the defaults is not named in the line. The block above it still lists every set.
  - *Found on the way, not changed.* A rank that hangs before its first batch is stored is `starting`, with no severity, for as long as its heartbeats go on or if it never sent one, so it is never a stall and never fires. (If its heartbeats stop it is `unresponsive`, which has a severity.) The first try of the real-server test showed it: the job stopped after 40 steps with the default `every=50`, so no step had left the device, and 58 s later the server still read `starting` with a floor of 2 s. So a job that hangs in its first `every` steps, or before its first step, with its process alive, is not caught by the watchdog today. That is the server's rule (`Progress::state`, and its test holds it at 1,500 s), and changing it is a Rust change and the author's call. *Changed the same day, at the author's word: "A rank that never stores a batch is no longer `starting` without limit".*
  - *Tested.* `tests/test_trigger.py` has 23 tests where it had 13. Eight are new under "A stall": a stall fires with no option at `critical` and not at `warning`, for each kind, through the watcher on a file that has stopped growing; a named stall does not fire again at `critical`, for each kind; only a stopped rank makes a stall critical; a change of kind or step fires again; a status that is no longer written; a failing command and an unreachable webhook are retried as for any incident. The other two are the real-server tests above. `tests/test_thresholds_line.py` is new, 12 tests, 3 of them through real servers started with `--thresholds` and at the defaults. Two assertions that pinned the old rule were changed in `test_trigger.py`: the line the command line prints at start (`firing: invariants, a stall at critical`), and the all-ranks test's run-level stall, which now does not fire at `warning` and fires once at `critical`. Thirteen deliberate breakages each failed at least one test: a stall fires at `warning` (5 failed), the old rule (8), any severity fires (5), three ways of firing twice (12, 3 and 6), a paused rank's severity counted (1), the line printed at the defaults (11), the defaults compared with `==` (1), the line never printed (11), left out of each of the two summaries (9 and 9), only the first set of `thresholds_by_rank` read (2). The whole suite, `pytest` in `sdk/` on macOS with Python 3.12 and the server built from this branch: 1175 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed (503 s), at `8d7f768`, the last commit that changes code or tests (the breakages were run one commit to code earlier, at `f17709d`, before the last test was added). The two files from a copy of the commit with no server binary, as CI's base jobs run them: 29 passed, 6 skipped, and the same with `pyarrow` hidden as well. The experiments' tests that CI's extras job runs: 81 passed, run before the last two commits to code and tests.
  - *Not run.* Linux and CI. The server's default timings (300 s and 900 s): the real-server tests use 2 s and 10 s, and 1 s and 4 s. A stall of real processes that leaves the other ranks stepping (`rank_silent`), which is tested from made statuses only. A real stuck collective on GPUs. A real agent as the command, and an outside webhook. Rust's tests were not run, since no Rust file changed; the server was built with `cargo build --offline` from this branch. The 64-rank benchmark.
  - *For the merge.* `tests/test_thresholds_line.py` is a new file that needs the server binary for 3 of its 12 tests (3 of `test_trigger.py`'s 23 need it too, and that file is in the list already), and is not in the server job's list in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (not edited here). The header of "Four open choices settled" still says items 1 and 4 are not built, and the focused plan's line "A hang fires only when its kind is named" is out of date; neither is edited here.
- [x] **A rank that never stores a batch is no longer `starting` without limit (decided by the author and built Nov 16, 2026 in a side session; merged Nov 17, 2026).** Found while building "a stall at critical fires": the capture holds `every` steps (50) before it writes any, so a job that hung in its first steps, or before its first step, stayed `starting` for ever, with no severity, no stall and no trigger. Branch `claude/heuristic-hertz-ef0bda`, on `main` at `9db7b81`.
    - **The rule the author chose** (the work counter, with a time bound for a rank that shows no work; critical at the start limit plus the critical time, not three times the limit):
        1. Once a rank's heartbeats show its work counter rising while it says `train`, it has taken a training step, and until its first stored batch the counter is its progress. It is `silent` when the counter has stood still past its threshold: `--silent-seconds`, or `--silent-factor` times its own time per step, never under three heartbeat intervals. It is `critical` at the larger of `--critical-seconds` and three thresholds, counted from the counter's last movement. Defaults: warning 300 s after the last step, critical at 900 s.
        2. A rank that has shown no training step (compilation, a data load, its first collective, or no heartbeat at all) is `starting` for `--start-seconds` (new flag, default 1800) since its last sign of work (the stream's opening, a tick, a change of phase). Then it is `silent` at `warning`, and `critical` `--critical-seconds` later: 2700 s by default.
        3. A warning given before the first batch keeps its clock over a server restart: the restored rank is counted from its counter's last movement or its last sign of work, not from when it was first seen, so it does not come back `critical`. Found in review after the first commit, and fixed with a test that failed before. *(Changed Nov 17, 2026: a restored rank is now judged from the restart, with a fresh window; see Decisions: "The trigger and the watchdog: one firing for one stall ...".)*
        4. A first report already above zero counts as a step taken. A tick in another phase is work and not a step. A declared pause after the first steps is `paused` under `--pause-seconds`, as later in a run. Stopped heartbeats are `unresponsive`, as before.
    - **What a slow start gets.** Steps of 30 s or 60 s keep the counter moving, so a first batch that takes 25 or 50 minutes raises nothing. A step slower than the floor sets a long threshold from the time per step. A first step that takes 40 minutes gets a warning at 30 that the step ends; one that takes longer than 45 minutes is called critical, and `--start-seconds` is the option to raise.
    - **What moved.**
        - `server/src/watchdog.rs`: `Progress::state`, `threshold`, the severity clock (`quiet_seconds`, `critical_after`), and `heartbeat`, which now keeps `stepped` and the last eight times per step. `main.rs` and `ingest.rs` carry `--start-seconds`.
        - `watchdog.json`: a rank can be `silent` with `last_step: null`, so a run can be `stalled` or `degraded` before any batch. Each rank entry has `work`; the top has `start_seconds`; `restart` has `stepped` and `step_seconds`, both read as absent from an older file. No new state name. The incident's step is 0.
        - Python: `_progress_incidents` and `run_state` are unchanged. `_describe_stream` says "Job stalled before its first batch was stored" and "has had no step stored yet" where it would have printed "after step 0" and "since step None". `sdk/README.md` says the rule.
        - Not moved: the golden files (they hold no status), `server/src/detect.rs`, the run state (a stall before the first batch reads `unknown`, as any stall does), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
    - **Tests.**
        - Rust: `before_the_first_batch_a_rank_is_starting_not_stopped` is replaced by `before_its_first_step_a_rank_is_starting_for_the_start_limit_then_silent`, with eight new tests beside it. `cargo test`: 82 unit tests and 28 server tests passed, 1 ignored as before. `cargo fmt --check` and CI's `cargo clippy` line are clean. Eleven deliberate breakages of the rule each failed at least one unit test.
        - Real server and real training processes, `every` at its default, in the new `sdk/tests/test_starting_bound.py` (3 tests, about 45 s): 40 steps then none goes `starting`, `warning`, `critical` with `last_step` null and `work` 40; a rank that never steps is given the start limit and is critical after it; a rank 6 s to its first step with a 3 s limit is warned and cleared, and its 15 s of steps to the first batch raise nothing with a critical time of 8 s.
        - The wording, with no server: `test_a_stall_before_the_first_stored_batch_is_said_as_that` in `sdk/tests/test_watchdog.py`, from made statuses (`job_stalled` and `rank_silent`).
        - Python suite on macOS with Python 3.12, this worktree's package and its debug server, `TENSORQUILL_SERVER_BIN` set: 1157 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (496 s). Without the variable one test, `test_each_of_the_five_flags_reaches_the_detectors_on_a_local_file`, fails for want of the binary.
    - **Open.**
        - `sdk/tests/test_starting_bound.py` is not in the server job's list in `ci.yml`, so CI skips it until it is added at the merge.
        - All defaults are uncalibrated, `--start-seconds` most of all: no real job's time to its first step was measured.
        - A job whose first steps are all taken within one heartbeat of a first report of zero is seen at the next beat; a rank with heartbeats off (`heartbeat_seconds=None`) has only the start limit, so its hang after 40 steps is seen after 1800 s.
        - A rank waiting in `train` on another rank's long startup has no step of its own and is under the same start limit.
        - The first time per step includes the whole startup. With slow steps (a rise of 1 at the first movement seen), a hang within the first two movements is warned of only after `--silent-factor` times the startup time.
        - A restored rank whose sender does not come back within `--silent-seconds` is `lost` with the quiet time it had, as any restored rank is, so a rank 1,900 s into its start can read `critical` then. *(Changed Nov 17, 2026: a restored rank is now judged from the restart, with a fresh window; see Decisions: "The trigger and the watchdog: one firing for one stall ...".)*
        - The step time is the counter's rise over the time since it last moved, so ticks mixed with steps make it look faster. The floor covers the usual case.
    - **Not run.**
        - The trigger firing for this case: that code is on the unmerged `claude/stall-trigger-b4dc5e`. Here the tests stop at `critical` in `watchdog.json` and in the incident Python reads from it.
        - The server's default timings (300, 900 and 1800 s); the real-server tests use 1 to 8 s.
        - Linux, CI and Windows; a real compilation or data load; a GPU job; two or more real ranks with one stuck before its first step (`rank_silent`), which is covered from made statuses only.
- [x] **Four open choices settled (decided by the author Nov 16, 2026; items 1, 2 and 4 built the same day and merged Nov 17, 2026; item 3 timed again Nov 17, 2026).** The side sessions had left four choices open. Each claim about the code below was read the same day; nothing was run.
  - *1. The reader takes the server's thresholds: kept, with one addition.* Refusing every server started with other values would make its report useless. Added: one printed line whenever the applied thresholds differ from the defaults, so it is never silent.
  - *2. A kind is `checked` from its first record: kept, with a field.* A run is not degraded while it warms up. But `checked` after one record, when the rule needs more, lets an agent read "checked, no incidents" as healthy, which is what "What was checked" set out to stop. Added: each kind says whether its rule has enough history yet, such as records seen against records needed. The attention kinds get the same treatment: today "too few probes" puts them in `not_checked`, which reads `unavailable` and makes the run `degraded` until a module's fourth probe (`ATTN_MIN_HISTORY` 2 and `ATTN_SUSTAIN` 2; with `probe_every` 1,000, about the 4,000th step, to be checked). A probe that failed stays `unavailable`.
    - This moves `run_state` in Python, in Rust and in the server's `watchdog.json`, and changes `kind_checks` in all 64 golden files, which are regenerated on purpose.
    - Open for the build: what "records needed" means for a rule that judges each parameter or module on its own history.
  - *3. The server parses numbers exactly: kept.* The parity and golden tests depend on it, and 5% to 9% of detector time is small. To do before the release: time the 64-rank benchmark again, since the parser is also on the live path, where one acknowledgement already reached 620 ms ("Re-timed with twenty-five kinds"). The author's remark, not a build order: moving the attention maps out of `probe_json` (G1) would win back far more than this costs.
  - *4. A hang fires the trigger only when named: changed.* A stall that has reached `critical` fires by default; a `warning` stall still fires only with `--fire-kind`. This reverses item 1 under "Compact evidence and the trigger". The author's reasons:
    - The watchdog already separates the cases. A rank in a declared evaluation or checkpoint phase is `paused` and raises nothing, and `critical` comes only after 900 seconds (`DEFAULT_CRITICAL`), or three of the rank's own silence thresholds if that is longer.
    - The costs are lopsided: a false wake is one agent run, since the trigger fires once per incident, and a missed hang is hours of idle multi-GPU time, which is what the product promises to catch.
    - The agent can tell them apart: the incident carries the stuck frame and how long it has not changed.
    - Not changed: `job_stalled` and `rank_silent` stay observations, so the run state and the golden files do not move for this.
    - Read plainly: any stall at `critical` fires, whatever its assessment (a suspected hang, a lost connection). Open to narrowing.
    - The README is to say plainly that a local run with no server cannot detect a hang at all.
  - *How it is built.* Two side sessions at the same time: one for item 2 (it owns `kind_checks`, `not_checked`, `detect.rs` and the golden files), one for items 1 and 4 (Python only: `trigger.py`, the printed line, the README). The re-timing of item 3 runs after both are merged, so that two builds do not spoil the numbers and the timed code is the code to release.
  - *What it moves.* A6 (what was checked in the all-ranks view) waited on item 2, which changed the shape of `kind_checks`; item 2 is merged, so A6 is free to start. The focused plan's line "A hang fires only when its kind is named" (build piece 3) was out of date; the author's doc and its copy here were brought up to date later the same day, at the author's word, with build piece 6 (comparing runs), the Rune decision as settled, the two trial jobs and the re-timing.
