DET-01: a `Trigger` given `state_path` empties its saved deliveries on the first tick (the command line passes none). CLI-05: the MCP server opened on the compact evidence the trigger gives an agent cannot find the capture beside it.
  - *Decided: fix the confirmed ones, in six lanes at once, at the author's word.* Split by file so no two lanes edit the same function, each in its own session and branch, each with a test that fails before its fix, and none to push or merge: R1 `_stats_hook`, `_record_weights`, `_read_updates` and `distributed.py` (SDK-08, SDK-10, SDK-05); R2 the scaler, optimizer-hook, commit, checkpoint and flush code in `capture.py`, and `lightning.py` (SDK-07, SDK-03, SDK-06); R3 `tracker.py`, `accelerator.py` and the clip wrapper (SDK-01, SDK-02, SDK-04); R4 `probe.py`, `trigger.py` and `mcp_server.py` (SDK-09, DET-01, CLI-05, CLI-06); R5 `compare.py`, `divergence.py` and the report wait in both watchers (CLI-01 to CLI-03, DET-04, DET-05, and CLI-04 only if it reproduces); R6 `server/` (RUST-01 both halves, RUST-04, RUST-03). A lane stops and reports before changing a detector rule, the golden files, the Parquet schema or the protocol number. Each adds its own entry here. R1, R2 and R3 all edit `capture.py`, in different functions.
  - *Three questions answered with a recommendation, not yet the author's decision.*
    - K2, the cross-rank compare: the same inputs on every rank, using the probe batch, rather than a forward against its recomputation, which works only where the user turns on activation checkpointing (TensorQuill's hooks skip recomputation by design). Its limits: it sees only every `probe_every` steps (1,000 by default), only the forward and only the probe batch, so it catches a GPU whose arithmetic stays wrong and not a passing fault or one in the backward or the optimizer; an exact compare assumes the forward is bit for bit the same on every rank, which is unmeasured on GPUs; it applies across data-parallel replicas only; and naming the odd rank by majority carries Elsen et al.'s caveat that a majority is not proof. The probe records a histogram per block (`lo`, `hi`, `counts`, `nonfinite`), not the mean and spread an outside summary assumed; K2 needs new cross-rank code.
    - Gate 1's fixtures: keep them without `run_end`, as left at the merge of lanes 1 to 4. The line is an artefact of how the fixtures are written, answers none of the scored questions, and would change preregistered materials. Accepted cost: Gate 1 does not show the "Run end" line a real capture now carries.
    - The desync wording: PyTorch's analyzer names as "Culprit" any rank that differs from the rank it compares against, for any number of ranks; its own source says so (`flight_recorder/components/utils.py`: "it's possible that the current rank is the culprit, then users will see lots of normal ranks reported as culprit"). Since `3124fd2` the reference is the lowest rank in the dumps, so that rank is never named. Recommended: keep PyTorch's text unchanged in `mismatch`; in the summary, say "rank N issued X where rank R, the rank compared against, issued Y"; with three or more ranks, count from the dumps which collective each rank issued at that sequence number and name the minority only when it is clearly apart (the rule of `stopped_stacks`: one rank, or at most a quarter); with two ranks, say that nothing shows which is at fault. An outside summary had proposed the note for two ranks only.
  - *Not done.* No fix is merged; the lanes report to the top session, and merging waits for the author's word. The medium and low findings are not checked. The focused plan is updated the same day from this entry.

- [x] **R6: three server findings of the outside review (Nov 18, 2026, lane R6, branch `claude/lane-r6-server-920397`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes R6, R4, R5, R3 and R1 merged").** These are RUST-01, RUST-03 and RUST-04 of the review written at `78f8f7e`, as the top session checked them on `3124fd2`. Under `server/`, `src/ingest.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `src/watchdog.rs` and `tests/ingest_test.rs` changed. Outside it, one assertion in `sdk/tests/test_trigger.py` changed (see RUST-03). The Parquet schema, the detectors, `detect`'s output, the golden files and the protocol number (2) are unchanged. RUST-02 and RUST-05 were judged not to hold, or to be narrow, and are not touched.
  - *RUST-01, the test half: reproduced and fixed.*
    - The fault. `exceeding_the_128_mib_quota_returns_resource_exhausted` waited 2 × 300 ms, then asserted that 100 MiB was buffered.
    - Reproduced. At background priority (`taskpolicy -b`, six runs at once) it failed 12 of 12 at `tests/ingest_test.rs:436`, the line lane B saw fail. At normal priority, 12 single runs and 6 runs of the whole file passed.
    - The fix. The test now polls `buffered_bytes()` through a new `until_within` (up to 120 s; `until` keeps its 5 s). It then checks that the third rank is refused while the first two still have no acknowledgement. It passed 12 of 12 under the same throttling.
    - Two other fixed waits asserted a state the server reaches in its own time, and now poll:
      - `buffered_ranks` waited for the first buffered byte, then 100 ms, before 32 parts were asserted. It now waits until the status names all 32 ranks' batches, then sleeps the 100 ms that ages the buffers past their 50 ms interval.
      - `sigterm_flushes_buffered_rows_before_exit` sent SIGTERM 500 ms after the batch. It now waits until the binary's `watchdog.json` names step 49.
    - Left as they are. The other `no_ack_within` calls assert that nothing arrived, which slowness cannot make fail. The remaining sleeps are the test's input: pacing ranks, outages, refresh and abandon times, file-time resolution. Under the same throttling, `ranks_are_written_concurrently_so_a_slow_store_does_not_serialise_them` misses its 1.5 s bound (47 s). That is a timing claim, not a wait, and is not changed.
  - *RUST-01, the h2 half: does not reproduce, and the code is left as it was.*
    - The test. The new test `a_refused_stream_is_told_its_status_while_more_batches_are_in_flight` sends 200 streams through a tonic client on a four-thread runtime, eight at a time. Each stream is refused, half for the quota and half as a bad batch, with eight 1 MiB batches queued behind the refused one. So DATA is still in flight when the server stops reading.
    - The result. Every client read `ResourceExhausted: ingest buffer full` or its `InvalidArgument`. Over 24 runs (debug and release, normal and background priority), that is 4,800 streams with none `Unknown`. With the error send removed as a check, the test fails, reading `not a status: None` on all 200.
    - Why, read in the sources:
      - Dropping the request body sends no reset while the response is open (h2 0.4.20, `RecvStream::drop`).
      - The reset that follows the trailers is `NO_ERROR` (`maybe_cancel`). hyper 1.12 reads that as the end of a body.
      - h2 keeps a received end of stream after a reset (`ErrorAfterEndStream`).
  - *Lane E's h2 errors: not explained by the drop order.*
    - What `Unknown` means. tonic reports `Unknown: h2 protocol error: …` only for an h2 error whose reason it does not map. Examples are an error with no reason at all (an I/O error on the connection, or one of h2's own user errors) or a `STREAM_CLOSED` reset. The reset a dropped stream would cause is `NO_ERROR` or `CANCEL`, which tonic does map.
    - The experiment. A tonic client whose server process is killed under a waiting stream reads exactly `Unknown: h2 protocol error: error reading a body from connection`, caused by "stream closed because of a broken pipe". This was a throwaway experiment and is not kept.
    - The conclusion. Those ranks most likely lost their connection. A refusal would have reached them with its own code, as 139 quota refusals did in lane E's first 1,024-rank run. What broke the connections is not known.
    - Follow-ups. Make `live_streams.rs` print a status's source chain, and have the server log a refused stream (it logs none now).
  - *RUST-04: reproduced and fixed.*
    - The fault. `main.rs` aborted the flusher and the status writer, wrote the stopped status, and only then flushed.
    - The fix. `main.rs` now calls a new `Ingest::stop`, which does this in order:
      1. It refuses batches.
      2. It signals both tasks through a `watch` channel, which they check between rounds. A round already started skips the ranks it has not begun.
      3. It waits for the two tasks to end.
      4. It flushes and judges what is buffered (`shutdown`).
      5. It writes the stopped status, even when the flush failed, and returns that error afterwards.
    - Tests:
      - `sigterm_flushes_buffered_rows_before_exit` runs the binary with rows buffered at SIGTERM. It now also checks that `incidents.json` reaches step 49, and that the last `watchdog.json` says `server_stopped` with rank 0 judged. On the old binary it failed: `no_steps` and `ranks_unknown: [0]`.
      - New: `a_stop_lets_a_flush_in_progress_finish_and_writes_the_stopped_status_last`. It runs in process with every store request taking 300 ms, and stops as soon as the timed flush's part is acknowledged. With the old order moved into the library first, `incidents.json` was never written.
    - The cost. A SIGTERM that arrives during a write to a failing store waits for that write's retry time before the shutdown flush starts. A status write in progress is also waited for, so no live status can land after the stopped one. The `--store-retry-seconds` help now says it bounds each of these waits, not the whole stop.
  - *RUST-03: reproduced and fixed.*
    - The fix. `Watchdog::batch` now makes a new attempt's step the rank's `last_step` at that attempt's first stored batch. A new attempt is one other than the rank's, or one seen in heartbeats that has stored nothing yet. Within an attempt, `last_step` is the running maximum, as before. `accept` now reports the last attempt that kept rows, with that attempt's own last step; it used to take the maximum across the attempts in one batch.
    - Unchanged:
      - Rows of an earlier attempt sent late, while the new attempt has stored nothing, raise `last_step` without counting as progress.
      - There is one `batch` call per batch, so the gaps, states and severities are as they were. A new unit test checks this on the stall clock: silent at 60 s, critical at 180 s, gaps `[10, 10, 10, 10]`.
      - A lower attempt, once the newer one has stored rows, is still taken as a job started again (a host whose clock is behind). Its step is now the rank's too.
    - Tests changed. Two existing unit tests changed only their `last_step` expectations: 99 → 29, and 120 → 29 then 59.
    - New tests, all failing before the fix:
      - `last_step_is_the_newest_attempts_once_it_stores_rows_and_stall_timing_is_unchanged`, a unit test.
      - `the_status_follows_the_newest_attempts_step_once_it_stores_rows`, on a real stream. It sees 149, then 109 for the resumed attempt, then 59 for one batch that ends attempt 200 and starts attempt 300.
    - The trigger. Its stall episodes compare a rank's attempt and step, and a new attempt already ends an episode, so a lower step after it changes nothing there. That was read in `trigger.py`, and the real-server test bears it out: `test_a_new_attempt_that_hangs_before_it_passes_the_old_step_fires_again` still fires once for each stall, twice in all.
    - **An SDK test pinned the old value; its expectation is changed.** That same test asserted `rank["last_step"] == 99  # the highest stored step, of the old attempt`, and the status now says 29, the step the new attempt stored, so it failed on every run. Asked whether to change it, the author left it to this lane. The assertion now expects 29, with a comment on the old value. Nothing else in the test or in `sdk/` changed. Lanes R2 and R4 edit other lines of the same file (1243 to 1467, and an insertion at 782), so the merge should not conflict. The `last_step == 99` at line 1827 holds while the new attempt has stored nothing, which this change leaves as it was.
  - *Run.* macOS, offline, with lanes R1 to R5 building on the same machine (load averages 20 to 50).
    - Rust:
      - `cargo build --release`.
      - `cargo test` and `cargo test --release`: 98 unit tests, 34 integration tests and 1 ignored, and 1 protocol test.
      - `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, with and without default features.
      - `cargo fmt --check`.
      - At background priority, six runs at a time: the three new stop and attempt tests passed 12 of 12.
    - Python: the 27 files of the CI server job, with this branch's debug binary: 1,098 passed and 2 failed, in 16.5 minutes at load averages of 30 to 50.
      - `test_a_new_attempt_that_hangs_before_it_passes_the_old_step_fires_again` failed on the pinned value above, before its expectation was changed.
      - `test_a_job_started_again_that_hangs_before_its_first_stored_step_fires_after_its_start_window[all]` failed once. Its message was not kept. Its new attempt stores no step, so `last_step` does not reach it, and it asserts timings (the new attempt seen within 4 s, nothing fired inside the window). It then passed 5 of 5 alone, both variants. In a run of `test_trigger.py` alone at load 11 to 24 it passed again: 41 passed, and the only failure was the pinned test. So it was load; the same test on the old binary under the same load was not run.
      - With the expectation changed: `test_trigger.py` alone gave 42 passed, and the 27 files run again gave 1,100 passed and none failed (14.7 minutes; load average about 5 at the end).
  - *Not run.* Linux. S3 or GCS: the stop order is the same code, but a store's retry time at SIGTERM was not exercised. No test covers a timed-flush round skipping the ranks it has not started once the stop is set. Lane E's 64- and 1,024-rank runs, again.

- [x] **Review fixes, lane R4: the probe, the trigger and the MCP server (Nov 18, 2026).** Four findings of the outside review (`codebase-review-findings-78f8f7e.md`, checked on `main` at `3124fd2`) are fixed in `probe.py`, `trigger.py` and `mcp_server.py`, each with a test that failed before the fix and passes after. No detector rule, golden file, server file or protocol number changed.
  - *SDK-09, the attention statistics under autocast.* `_attention_stats` runs inside the user's forward and cast the query and key to float32, but under autocast its `matmul` ran in the lower precision again: under FP16, logits near 37,000 gave a `max_logit` of 13,456 and 10 rows counted invalid; under BF16 they were rounded. It now runs with autocast off (`_autocast_off`), as the head's numbers (K4) already did; the body is `_attention_stats_fp32`. The probe's other work inside the forward, the histograms (`isfinite`, `aminmax`, `histc`), calls no op that autocast casts down on the CPU, CUDA or MPS (none of them is in `autocast_mode.cpp` or `autocast_mode.h`, read at 2.6.0 and at the reference checkout), so it is unchanged. What changes for a user: a run probed under autocast now records float32 `max_logit` and `entropy`, which `attention_logit_growth` reads; earlier records were rounded or overflowed. The rule is unchanged. Tests: `test_attention_stats_are_float32_under_autocast` (FP16 and BF16) and `test_a_probe_run_under_bf16_autocast_reads_the_attention_in_float32` in `test_probe.py`. This closes the open item "Lane 4 suspects the attention statistics have the exposure to autocast" of "Lanes 1 to 4 merged".
  - *DET-01, a state file given by name.* Both watchers call `tick()` before their first `look()`, and a `Trigger(state_path=...)` saved its empty state at that tick before it read the file, so a restarted watcher fired again for every incident and stall episode it listed. The file is now read when the trigger is made (a file that cannot be read is said and nothing counts as fired, as before), and `_save` writes nothing before the first look. The command line passes no `state_path` (it is taken from the evidence at the first look) and behaves as before; the test holds that too. Test: `test_a_state_file_given_by_name_is_read_before_the_first_tick_can_write_it` in `test_trigger.py` (two watches with the same file: two firings before the fix became four).
  - *CLI-05, the capture beside a compact file.* `Source._records_beside` found `x.jsonl` for `x.evidence.json` and not for `x.evidence.compact.json`, the file `--compact` writes and the trigger names in `TENSORQUILL_EVIDENCE`, so an agent started on it had no layer or parameter series. It now strips either ending. The other names `mcp_server.py` derives were checked: none else comes from `.evidence.json` (the `--records` help now names both). Test: `test_a_compact_evidence_file_reads_the_capture_beside_it` in `test_mcp_server.py`.
  - *CLI-06, a router's series from compact evidence.* With step records held (`--records`, or the capture beside a compact file once CLI-05 is fixed), `get_series` read a router's series from the compact file's `samples`, which cover only a window around each MoE incident. When the file left samples out, the series is now read from the records, every step (`_rows`), and the detectors' flags (`overloaded`, `dead`, `first_flagged_step`) come from the file's own samples at the steps it keeps, not from a rebuild, since the file may have been written with other thresholds or the Rust detectors; a `compact` key in the answer says so. With the compact file alone the series is still its window, and the answer, and the error for a range outside it, say that it is the window's and what the run's extent is. Full evidence, and a compact file that left nothing out, answer as before. One sentence each in the tool's description and in `AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` ("How to read a series") says what `compact` means; the instructions name no new kind. Test: `test_a_routers_series_from_compact_evidence_is_the_whole_run_when_the_records_are_held` in `test_mcp_server.py` (it fails before the fix through `--records`, so it does not rest on CLI-05).
  - *Run* (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15, no server binary, `pyarrow` installed). The SDK suite: 1,069 passed and 619 skipped (the six new tests among the passed); the three edited test files and `test_head_probe.py` again on the final tree (two docstrings changed after the suite started): 93 passed, 12 skipped. Before these changes the same suite gave 1,063 passed and 619 skipped (that run overlapped the first edits, so it is a rough baseline). `experiments/moe-bakeoff/test_fixtures.py` and `experiments/gate1/test_gate1.py`: 92 passed. The review's `sdk09.py` now prints the same numbers with and without autocast. For CLI-06, the router series read from the records was compared with the one the capture itself gives, on a copy of the recorded Granite run (`granite-router-only.jsonl`, 650 steps, its compact file written with window 0 beside it) and on the Gate 1 `seeded` capture: `diversity`, `largest_share` and `experts_with_no_tokens` are the same at every step, with no empty value, and the flags are there at the kept steps only. One `get_series` call through the server's stdio on a compact file beside its capture gave strict JSON with the `compact` key.
  - *Not run, or open.* Nothing ran on a GPU or on PyTorch 2.6 itself; the 2.6 sources were read for CPU FP16 autocast and `is_autocast_enabled(device_type)`, which the tests use. CUDA autocast was read, not run. `runs.py` still finds a run's evidence only as `<records>.evidence.json` (`_read_evidence_file` and the shared-file row), so `list_runs` shows a run watched with `--compact` as having no evidence; `runs.py` is not this lane's file. `get_incident`'s `previous_sample` for an MoE incident in a compact file with `--compact-window 0` is not there (the earlier step is left out); not changed. Nothing is pushed or merged.
- [x] **Compare and the watchers' report wait: six findings of the outside review fixed (Nov 18, 2026, lane R5, branch `claude/lane-r5-compare-watch-17ee93`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes R6, R4, R5, R3 and R1 merged").** CLI-01 to CLI-04, DET-04 and DET-05 of the review of `78f8f7e`, checked against `3124fd2`. Each one reproduced on CPU before any change, and each has a test that fails on `3124fd2` and passes with the fix. No detector rule, golden file, Gate 1 fixture, file under `server/` or protocol number changed. `evidence._incident_identity` is untouched: the CLI-01 fix is in `compare.py`.
  - *CLI-01: the run's own incidents in `compare`.* Reproduced: two runs with the same memory imbalance on rank 1, at other byte readings, were two entries; the same stall read at two moments had two identities. `compare.incident_identity` now gives the seven run-level kinds an identity of what stays the same whenever the run is read. Which ranks are named is each run's own and is listed in `where`, as which ranks show a per-rank incident already is, and as the MoE kinds list each run's experts.
    - `job_stalled` and `rank_silent` are identified by how the job stopped: a hang or a lost connection (or ranks never connected). Suspected and confirmed hangs count as one, since "confirmed" only says that Flight Recorder dumps were given beside the status. Times, seconds since progress, severity (which turns critical as time passes), steps, stacks and the other ranks are left out.
    - `collective_hang` and `collective_desync` are identified by the collective and its process group (id, description, size). The sequence number, the dump buffer's start, which ranks were dumped and the mismatch text are left out.
    - `traincheck_violation` is identified by the names of the invariants violated, not when, how often or in which file. Only the ten invariants the incident lists are seen, so beyond ten the names can differ between runs with the same violations.
    - `duplicate_batches` and `memory_imbalance` are identified by the kind alone. Counts and byte readings are left out.
    - `incident_where` and the printed summary gain `ranks`, `missing_ranks` (never connected, or not in the stuck collective), `assessment` for a stall, `collective` and `group`, and `violations`. Without these, two entries that the identity keeps apart would print the same line.
  - *CLI-02: the lowest rank with nothing whole yet.* Reproduced: rank 0's file had only the start of a line while rank 1 had records, and `compare` refused the run. A local run of several files now goes to the all-ranks path before it looks for records in the lowest file. That rank is unknown, with why, and a run where no file holds a whole record is still refused. `read_from.path` names the folder whenever there are several files; before, it named the one file that held records, which here was the empty one.
  - *CLI-03: part 4 on a live file.* Reproduced: a half-written last line made part 4 answer `no_records`, for one rank and for every rank. Part 4 now reads as `load_records` does and, on a `.jsonl` file that has a line that is not whole, falls back to the whole lines. It uses the watcher's reader, with the same rank and attempt resolution. `unfinished_lines_left_out` names the runs and ranks, and `detail` (what the summary prints) ends by saying a line was left out. The tests compare the answer with the one for the same file without the torn line, including a resumed rank whose rolled-back records would change the answer if they were not resolved away.
  - *CLI-04: sparse series in runs identical early. Reproduced, so fixed.*
    - Before the fix, weight norms (every 50 steps, at steps 49, 99 and so on) went wrong when every series was the same up to step 29 and a parameter froze between step 30 and the second check. On made-up records, a freeze at 10 to 75 gave `no_divergence` (the series was "not comparable"), and a freeze at 85 or 90 named step 299 or 199. On the toy Llama of `seeded_failures.py`, a freeze at step 40, 60 or 85 gave `no_divergence`; only the documented freeze at 100 was named (step 149).
    - The cause: the first two samples stood in as the early agreement whatever they held.
    - The fix applies only when the runs are identical early. A stand-in sample now counts as agreement only while the two runs are still the same there; it still gives the series' own movement. The sample where they first differ, and every sample after it, is judged.
    - The documented cases are unchanged: the same answers, and the same `early` table, when both stand-ins agree. Dense series and pairs that are not identical early are untouched.
    - The toy now names the seeded parameter at step 49 (frozen at 40) and step 99 (frozen at 60 or 85). When the first sample is already out there is no sample before it, so `before` is None and the reason says that every series was the same up to the last early step. The `early` table gains `movement_samples` and `movement_last_step` when the movement was measured on samples other than the agreement's.
    - A toy freeze at step 25 is not changed: rounding makes the runs differ from step 26, so they are not identical early, the series is not compared (the documented rule) and the answer stays `no_divergence`.
  - *DET-04: the single-rank watcher once the report is current again.* Reproduced on a store written by hand. The report went unreadable while the status changed, and the write inside the wait said `unreadable` and `unknown`. When the same report came back, nothing was rewritten, so the file stayed `unknown`. The key the watcher compares with what it wrote now holds the look's state, so the file is rewritten once the report is current again.
    - The write inside the wait still says the report is not current (`report_not_current`). That is the documented behaviour, and `test_watch_surfaces_watchdog_and_external_sources_during_report_grace` pins it; the review's suggestion to write it as current was not taken.
  - *DET-05: a rank inside its wait in the all-ranks watcher.* Reproduced: when rank 2's new report caused a write, rank 1, a moment behind inside its wait, was written as not judged and the run as `unknown`. It was rewritten on the next look.
    - `_RankMemory` now keeps the rank's last current look, and `_look_at_ranks` gives that look for a rank inside its wait, so the rank is judged with its last current report. This is the single-rank watcher's rule of writing from the last current report.
    - The key compared with what was written is now the one written, so a rank that comes back with the same report causes no second write. After the wait, behind is `stale` and unknown, as before.
    - `test_watch_all_ranks.py`'s `test_a_rank_between_a_part_and_its_report_is_no_news_but_is_not_left_stuck_once_written` pinned the old behaviour (three writes, rank 1 behind) and was changed on purpose: two writes, rank 1 current with its last report.
    - `_look_at_ranks` now returns one key instead of two, so one line of `all_ranks_evidence` (unpacking its result) changed.
  - *Checked* (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15, `pyarrow` installed; the server binary built here with `cargo build` into a private target folder).
    - The SDK suite on `3124fd2` unchanged: with the binary, 1,682 passed and none were skipped (plus six of the new tests, which that run collected and which failed, as they should); without it, 1,063 passed and 619 skipped.
    - With the fixes: with the binary, 1,699 passed and none skipped; without it, 1,080 passed and 619 skipped. The `experiments/` tests (`moe-bakeoff/test_fixtures.py`, `gate1/test_gate1.py`): 92 passed.
    - The new tests: 17 items, in `test_runs_compare.py` (6), `test_divergence.py` (8, two of them on the toy Llama) and the new `test_report_wait.py` (3), which reads only part names and hand-written reports: it ran without the binary, and was not run without `pyarrow`. On `3124fd2` the 15 that test the findings fail, and so does the changed all-ranks test; the 2 that check unchanged behaviour pass (no wait, and a freeze at step 100).
  - *Not checked, and open.*
    - The two watchers now differ for a write made inside the wait. One rank's file says the report is not current and the run state is unknown; the all-ranks file judges the rank from its last current report. Whether the single-rank watcher should write the last current look too is the author's call: it would change a tested, documented behaviour.
    - The TrainCheck identity sees ten invariants at most. The server writes the stall incidents into `watchdog.json` with the same field names as Python (`assessment`, `ranks`, `missing_ranks`; read in `watchdog.rs`), but the tests build the status without that list, so they go through Python's `detect_watchdog_incidents`.
    - A parameter frozen before the runs stop being identical early is still not named.
    - Nothing ran on a GPU, Linux or PyTorch 2.6. Nothing is pushed.

- [x] **Lane R3: three findings of the outside review fixed in the adapters (Nov 18, 2026): SDK-01, SDK-02, SDK-04.** Built in its own session on branch `claude/lane-r3-adapters-24a1a2`, from `3124fd2`, beside five other lanes that each own other functions. Not merged.
  - *SDK-01, one run ID on every rank.* A tracker's run usually exists on rank 0 only. `resolve_run` read it on each rank, so under `torch.distributed` rank 0 took its W&B or MLflow run's ID and entered no collective, while the other ranks either waited in `broadcast_object_list` for a generated ID rank 0 never sent (`init` hung until the group's timeout) or, with `TORCHELASTIC_RUN_ID` or `SLURM_JOB_ID` set, took the launcher's ID: one run's files split under two names. Now, when no `run_id` is passed and a process group of several ranks is initialized, rank 0 resolves the whole order (`MLFLOW_RUN_ID`, the MLflow run, the W&B run, the launcher's ID, a generated one) and every rank takes its ID and source from one `broadcast_object_list`. Whether to broadcast depends only on what every rank sees alike (`run_id` passed or not, the process group), never on a tracker or the environment. Rank 0 passes over a tracker that raises, with a warning, so the other ranks are not left waiting. A `run_id` passed in is used as given with no collective, as before. Without a process group the old order and the old warning for a generated ID stay, and a rank that takes an MLflow or W&B run's ID while `WORLD_SIZE` is above 1 is now warned that it cannot be shared (that split is reported, not fixed: there is nothing to share through).
  - *The labels and the Hugging Face path.* `tracker_labels` does not split: `init` reads it on rank 0 only, from the tracker whose ID it broadcasts, and only rank 0 writes the manifest; the two-rank test checks that the one manifest has the W&B run's project, name and group. The Hugging Face callback shares the tracker's ID itself (`_shared_tracker_run_id`) and passes it to `init`; the two-rank test runs both and they agree. Its manifest says `run_id_source: "explicit"` where `init`'s own path says `wandb`; `huggingface.py` belongs to no lane here, so that is left as it is.
  - *What it costs.* A job with a launcher's ID and no `run_id=` had no collective at `init` and now has one broadcast, so the rule that every rank calls `init` (which the drift check already needs) now holds for SLURM and `--rdzv-id` jobs too. Under NCCL, `broadcast_object_list` uses the current CUDA device unless the group was bound to one, so a job that never calls `torch.cuda.set_device` puts every rank's broadcast on device 0; the generated-ID path and the Hugging Face callback already had that exposure. Not run on NCCL.
  - *SDK-02, leaving `with session:` after `watch_accelerator`.* The wrapper `watch_accelerator` puts on `session.close` took no arguments, and `Session.__exit__` calls `close(_exception=...)` since the end marker (S4, Nov 18), so leaving the block raised `TypeError` and wrote no end marker. It now passes any arguments on. Nothing else replaces or wraps `session.close` (searched `sdk/src`, `sdk/tests` and `experiments/`).
  - *SDK-04, clipping on a model whose first parameter is frozen.* `clip_grad_norm_` was matched to a session by its first parameter only, which in a LoRA or PEFT model is a frozen base weight (the Trainer clips `model.parameters()`), so no `clip_grad_norm` was recorded. Torch's wrapper and the Accelerate wrapper now share one match (`capture._clips_session`: any of the session's trainable parameters, through `set.isdisjoint` over the ids, which stops at the first one) and one rule for the norm (`capture._clip_is_l2`: `float(norm_type) == 2`, as torch reads it). Torch's wrapper used to record any norm type as if it were the L2 total, while Accelerate's compared `norm_type == 2` (missing `"2"`). Only an L2 norm is recorded: `record_grad_norm` adds up the squares of a step's norms, which means nothing for an L1 or max norm. A call with another norm type records nothing as `clip_grad_norm`; a run with none has its spikes judged on `grad_norm` where that is recorded, which is read after clipping, so a spike that clipping flattened can go unseen. The record schema, detectors and golden files are unchanged.
  - *Tests.* `sdk/tests/test_run_id_ranks.py`, 7 cases of two Gloo CPU processes (a file store, a 15 s group timeout, and a 60 s limit on the processes, so a hang fails rather than stalls the suite): W&B on rank 0 with no launcher ID, with `TORCHELASTIC_RUN_ID` and with `SLURM_JOB_ID`; the Hugging Face helper and `init` agreeing; a launcher's ID alone; a generated ID; MLflow raising on rank 0. `sdk/tests/test_close_and_clipping.py`, 28: the `with` block closing cleanly and on an exception (the end marker says which) through two stacked watches, and clipping on both paths with the first parameter frozen or not, a wholly frozen list, and `norm_type` given by position and by name (`2`, `2.0`, `"2"`, `1`, `inf`, `"inf"`). In `test_tracker.py` the broadcast test is rewritten for the new payload and two are added. On the code of `3124fd2` 20 of them fail: 5 two-rank cases (rank 1 timing out in the broadcast; `run-abc123` and `run-slurm77` against `wandb-abc`; the Hugging Face helper and `init` disagreeing; rank 0 raising out of `init`) and 15 in one process. All pass after.
  - *Checked* (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15, Accelerate 1.16.0, no server binary). The SDK suite: 1,100 passed and 619 skipped (the tests that need the server binary), none failed; after the last edits (a docstring, and the two-rank test pinning its processes to the tested source) the touched files were run again: 95 passed. The `experiments/` tests (`moe-bakeoff/test_fixtures.py`, `gate1/test_gate1.py`): 92 passed.
  - *Not done.* No GPU, NCCL, real tracker or PyTorch 2.6 run; Accelerate up to 1.15 (which clips through torch's wrapper) not run. `sdk/README.md` (the `clip_grad_norm` field, the run ID row) and `init`'s docstring for `run_id` and `clip_norm` do not yet say that rank 0's whole resolution is broadcast or that only an L2 norm is recorded: those lines are not this lane's. The hand-over note `lane-r3-adapters.md` names every function touched.

- [x] **Lane R1: tensors the capture raised on or misread (SDK-08, SDK-10, SDK-05; Nov 18, 2026, on its branch).** Three high-severity findings of the outside review, confirmed by the top session on `main` at `3124fd2`. Changed: `_stats_hook` (with a new helper, `_output_unread`), `Session._record_weights` and `Session._read_updates` in `capture.py`, and `distributed.py`. No change to the record schema, `server/`, the Parquet schema, the protocol number, a detector rule or the golden files.
  - *SDK-08, a watched block's output on another device or a DTensor (`_stats_hook`).* The four numbers move to the buffers' device where that adds no wait for a device, and the block is left out where it would. Between two GPUs (`device_map="auto"`) the copy is device to device, which PyTorch orders with stream events and no host sync (`copy_device_to_device` in `aten/src/ATen/native/cuda/Copy.cu`, read at `v2.6.0`; not run, no GPU). From the CPU to an accelerator it is one blocking copy. The old hook already read that case, with three blocking copies inside `torch.where` (`where_self_out` copies a 0-dim CPU operand to the device; run on the old code with a CPU block beside buffers on MPS, which it read), so this is fewer waits, not a new one. An output on an accelerator with the buffers on the CPU would need a copy back to the host at every forward, so that block is left out. That covers the review's CPU-and-MPS model, and by reading (not run) FSDP's CPU offload, where the first parameter, and so the buffers, sit on the CPU. Also left out: an output on `meta`, on another kind of accelerator, nested or sparse, and a DTensor with a Partial placement (each rank holds an addend of the output, not its values). A Replicate or Shard DTensor is read on its local tensor. Under Shard that is this rank's part, as `grad_norms` and `weight_norms` already are under FSDP2. All of this is judged from type, layout and device before any operation, so it is the same under `torch.compile`; in eager mode anything else is caught and leaves the block out. A float8 output counts zeros on its float32 copy. A same-device output runs the same operations as before.
  - *Why a left-out block has no reason in the record.* It has no entry in that step's `layers`, as a block that did not run. Saying why needs four things. A record field (proposed: `layers_not_read: {block: reason}`, said once as `moe_refused` is). A list that `Session.__init__` passes to each hook. `_commit` and `_decode` writing it. The evidence and the server turning it into `not_checked`. That is a schema change and other lanes' code, so it is left for a decision. Each hook keeps its reason in a `problems` list (also its `problems` attribute), which only the tests read so far.
  - *SDK-10, complex parameters.* `_record_weights`, `_norms_f64`, `_sum_f64` and `_projection_f64` read a complex tensor as its real pairs (`torch.view_as_real` after `resolve_conj()`), as `_read_updates` already read Adam's moments. The norm is |z|'s, the sum adds both parts, and the projection weighs both, so a change in the imaginary part alone moves all three fingerprints. `weight_dtypes` counts the parameter under its own dtype. `_read_updates` now resolves a conjugate view too.
  - *SDK-05, FSDP2 (`_read_updates`).* The moments, and a DTensor step count, are read on the local shard (`unwrap_local`). What `update_rms` then means: on each rank, the RMS of the update over the rows it holds, not the whole parameter's. Weighting each rank's square by its element count gives the whole parameter's; a two-rank gloo test checks that against a plain run. It stays under the parameter's name without a marker, because no reader combines it across ranks. The evidence's detectors read one rank's records, and `divergence.py` compares the same rank of two runs. The update-size rule compares a rank's update with that rank's own `grad_norm`, which is also taken over its shards. A rank whose shard of a parameter is empty reads nothing for it. `grad_norms`, `weight_norms` and now a Shard DTensor's `layers` follow the same rule. Nothing in the record says a value is a shard's, and a marker would be a schema change, so none was added. Not checked: what `clip_grad_norm` holds under FSDP2, which the rule uses instead of `grad_norm` when the run clips.
  - *Swept for raises on the CPU.* A scratch script trained through each function. It used 26 kinds of block output, run in eager mode and under the `eager` and `aot_eager` backends, with inductor on two. It used 15 kinds of trainable parameter under 8 optimizers (AdamW, five Adam settings, fused AdamW and NAdam), plus an Adam state without its step count. Before this change, 52 of 209 cases raised into training. After it, 3 do, all in `parameter_fingerprints` (below). Fixed on the way, in `_record_weights`:
    - A parameter on `meta` raised. It now gets NaN, never a made-up 0, and "not known" for `weights_unchanged`.
    - Sparse COO and compressed parameters raised. They now get the norm of their stored values.
    - An MPS parameter beside buffers on the CPU raised (`TypeError`: no float64 on MPS; run on the old code with no layer hooks, since its forward raised first). It now gets its float32 norm, moved to the CPU before widening, and "not known". A first version widened before moving, which also fails on MPS, and its catch wrote NaN; the test now checks every norm against the real one.
    - Any other failure of a group's norm gives NaN and "not known".
    - A parameter not read exactly at one check is also "not known" at the next.
  - *Tests.* `sdk/tests/test_capture_tensors.py`, 29. Against the code before this change (the committed test file, run in a copy of `3124fd2`'s `sdk/`), 19 fail and 10 pass. The 10 are regression guards: integer, empty, one-element, float8 and float64 outputs, eager and compiled. The CPU-then-MPS reverse order fails there only on the hooks' new `problems` list. All 29 pass after. The two MPS tests skip where MPS is absent. The GPU-to-GPU move is checked on fake CUDA tensors for its decision and its copy's devices; the hook's whole path on two GPUs was not run. The review's three scripts (`sdk05.py`, `sdk08.py`, `sdk10.py`) now run without an error or `update_not_read`. Related suites (`test_compile.py`, `test_eval_forwards.py`, `test_capture.py`, `test_distributed.py`, `test_update_size.py`, `test_router_guard.py`, `test_review_fixes.py`): 238 passed and 4 skipped. The whole SDK suite without the server binary (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15.0): 1,092 passed and 619 skipped, the skips being the tests that need the binary. The `experiments/` tests (`moe-bakeoff/test_fixtures.py`, `gate1/test_gate1.py`): 92 passed.
  - *Not done, found and left (other lanes' code or a decision).*
    - The record field for a left-out block's reason (above).
    - `parameter_fingerprints` raises on `meta` and sparse tensors. `record_replica_spread` calls it right before an `all_gather`, so a raise on one rank leaves the others waiting. A NaN fingerprint would be read as drift and zeros as agreement, so this needs a decision.
    - A first trainable parameter on `meta` (one registered on the root module comes first) puts every buffer on `meta` in `Session.__init__`, and `flush` raises.
    - Under FSDP's CPU offload, by reading (not run), every block is now left out (before, the forward raised). Choosing the buffers' device from where the blocks run would be `__init__`'s.
    - `_decode` writes `weights_unchanged` only when the first parameter's check is known, so an unread first parameter drops the whole list for that check.
    - `_read_updates` moves each RMS to `_update`'s device, which is a copy back to the host every step for an MPS parameter beside buffers on the CPU (as before).
    - Nothing ran on a GPU, a real model or PyTorch 2.6, and the new test file has not run on 2.6 (DTensor `to_local` under Dynamo, the float8 cast, complex32, the two-rank spawn): the first CI run will be its first there. The venv has 2.15.0; `Placement.is_partial`, `DTensor.placements`, `torch.distributed.fsdp.fully_shard`, the CUDA copy and `where_self_out` were read at `v2.6.0`. Nothing is pushed.

    | step 51 | 50 | 50 |
    | step 100 | 100 | 50 |

- [x] **Review findings SDK-07, SDK-03 and SDK-06: which record a step's late parts land on (Nov 18, 2026, lane R2, branch `claude/lane-r2-capture-steps-988cd1`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see the next entry).** From the outside review of `78f8f7e`, as checked on `3124fd2`; `main` (`4425102`) is merged into the branch. Code in `capture.py` (its step accounting) and `lightning.py`. No detector rule, golden file, Gate 1 fixture, Parquet schema or protocol number changed; nothing under `server/`.
  - *The first version, reversed.* It wrote a full ring when the next step needed a row, so that what arrives for the step that filled it (a save, a second optimizer's update) still found that step in the ring. The author answered "Keep it" at 17:32, before its cost was measured; after the measurement in the entry above (a hang in the step after a block boundary left the whole block unwritten: 0 steps on disk against 50), the author reversed it ("Five, send R2 back").
  - *What replaced it: option 2, at the author's word ("Yes, option 2").* A second pass first wrote a full ring at once, as on `main`, and found SDK-07 and SDK-06 on the step that fills the ring could not be fixed that way: one step can be written only once in an attempt (`detect_repeated_steps`), every line of the records is a step (`sink.py`), and the end marker never reaches the server. Six options with their costs went to the author (leave it as on `main`; hold back only the newest step; write the block when the last optimizer steps; the end marker; a new carrier in the records; the record written twice). The author chose the second.
    - **What it does.** When the ring fills, `_commit` writes it at once but for its newest step (`_flush(hold_newest=True)`, `_hold_newest`). That step stays in the ring as its only row, so what arrives for it after it ended is recorded there as for any step in the ring, and is written when the next step ends, at a flush or at close (`_release_held`). The ring is still read off the device in the one transfer `main` makes; the held step is queued from that copy, and only a device row changed since (a later optimizer's update, its weight check read again, its loss) is read again, once a block. A run with one optimizer adds no transfer (a test pins that nothing is marked changed there). The writer queue holds two items for each full ring, so training waits at the same eight rings behind as on `main` (one item a ring at `every=1`). With one optimizer the records are the same as `main`'s: a seeded 130-step run with layers, a router, clipping, update sizes and weight checks every 10 steps (some on a ring's last step) gives identical records at `every` 3 and 50, `ts`, `step_time`, `data_wait` and `attempt` aside; with a save after step 49, the only difference is that the save is on step 49's record instead of step 50's.
    - **What it costs.** A run that hangs or is killed in the step after a block boundary has one step fewer on disk than `main`, never a block. The boundary script (`lane-r2-boundary-check.py` in the session notes; a CPU toy, `every` 50, stopped without `close()`): 0, 49, 50, 50, 99 and 100 steps on disk after 49, 50, 51, 99, 100 and 101 steps (`main`: 0, 50, 50, 50, 100, 100), and 49 after 50 steps then a save (`main`: 50). The server's last step is one behind at such a hang, so three binary-gated tests that wait for a block's last step without a flush or close take the first version's edits again: the stall test in `test_flight_e2e.py` trains 41 steps instead of 40, and `test_trigger.py`'s worker scripts `_STALLING_RANK` and `_RANK_UNTIL` call `session.flush()` after each stepping loop. Three others wait one step more or assert the save on its own step (`test_capture.py`, `test_data_progress.py`, `test_system.py`).
    - **What the references do.** wandb keeps the current step's row open until a higher step, an explicit commit or `finish()`, and drops data logged to an earlier step with a warning (`core/internal/stream/handler.go`, `handlePartialHistorySync`, `handleExit`). When the training process dies, its service quits without writing the open row ("no need to sync uncommitted data", `core/pkg/server/server.go`, `exitWhenParentIsGone`): one step lost on a kill, the cost chosen here. MLflow's `log_metric(key, value, step)` and wandb's `define_metric` give each value its own step instead, which here would be a schema change.
  - *SDK-07, a save right after a full ring.* It is on the record of the step it follows, after 50 steps with `every` 50 and after full rings of 4 and 1. A resume from a save made after step 50 is compared with that save (`source: "save"`, `saved_after_step: 49`, rng and data missing). Both resume checks, `data_checks.detect_resume_state` and the server's `detect/data.rs`, key a save by its `after_step`, so they read the same save as before. It also changes what a crash keeps: on `main`, a save made after step 50 rode on step 51's record, written only when the next block filled, so a run killed 10 steps after the save lost it; now it is on disk at step 51 (checked on the CPU toy). Still not written, as on `main`: a save before the first step, and one made after an explicit `flush()` with no step since, if the session then closes. No record is invented.
  - *SDK-03, a step the loss scaler skipped.* All four of the review's claims hold; unchanged by either pass.
    - Gradient norms are read in `scaler.update()` when no hook read them, since a skipped step never reaches the hooks. `scaler.step` has already unscaled them (`torch/amp/grad_scaler.py`), so the overflowed parameter shows Inf or NaN. Under clipping an Inf shows as NaN against 0 for the others, but a NaN makes every norm NaN (clipping multiplies all by it), so nothing is named. Reading before clipping would need lane R3's clip wrapper. `grad_spike`, `update_spike` and `non_finite` (Python and Rust) already pass over skipped steps, and `stalled_weights` over rows whose total is not finite.
    - `update_not_read: {"no optimizer": …}` is decided at the flush from the record's `step_skipped`, only for a step the scaler did not skip. An optimizer that holds none of the parameters is still named, at its first step that was not skipped.
    - A weight check due on a skipped step is made on the next step that was not. The skip is told on the host, with no sync: no optimizer stepped since the last step. A skipped step still carries no weight check, the premise of the DET-02 verdict.
    - `scaler.update(new_scale)` with no optimizer through the scaler since its last update is not a step and records nothing.
    - On a 400-step CPU run with 15 routine skips, with and without clipping and `updates=True`, the incidents were the same before and after. What differed: the skipped steps' norms, two weight checks moved from steps 49 and 99 to 50 and 100, and the false "no optimizer" entry gone from `not_checked`.
  - *SDK-06, two optimizers stepped one after the other.* An optimizer of the model that steps after the lead ended the step (a follower) belongs to that step, which is still the newest in the ring, also when it filled the ring.
    - Its update is merged into that row parameter by parameter; the step's weight check is read again after it steps (against the same earlier check) and optimizer ownership judged again; once a follower has been seen, the probe waits until the lead's next step (or the next commit, a flush or `close()`), and the one step probed before a follower was known is probed again. The Lightning callback adds a follower's loss to that step's: a sum, as for microbatches.
    - With two AdamWs over two halves of an MLP, every record equals the one-AdamW run's (update sizes, weight norms, unchanged weights, ownership) with `every` 1, 3 and 50. A gradient fault in the second optimizer's parameters is an `update_spike` naming `4.weight` at steps 299 (which fills the ring), 300 (the review's step) and 310, as with one optimizer. Between the two passes, with the ring written at once, the fault at 299 raised no `update_spike` and the one at 300 named `0.weight`.
    - Under a loss scaler, `scaler.update()` already ends the step after every `scaler.step`, pinned by a test. Lightning's automatic optimization with several optimizers raises in Lightning itself (`core/optimizer.py`), so only manual exists; it is tested with two backward passes, with `every` 50 and 1.
    - **Open.** The hooks cannot tell an optimizer that steps after the lead in the same iteration from one that steps before the lead in the next: one that starts stepping later but sits before the lead in the loop has its update, re-check and probe one step early, where on `main` its update was on the right record (pinned by a test). Fixing it needs an iteration-end signal, such as Lightning's `on_train_batch_end`.
    - **Left as is.** The replica-spread collective still runs before followers step. Lightning's AMP plugin calls `scaler.update()` after each optimizer, so there each optimizer's step is its own TensorQuill step (untested). Lightning's manual `global_step` counts every optimizer step while TensorQuill counts one per batch, so `start_step` jumps on a resume with several optimizers.

Pins that pass on both sides: a save after 6 steps with `every` 4, an explicit `flush()` writing every step, and the plain AMP loop with two optimizers. *Not run:* a GPU, Linux, PyTorch 2.6, the Rust detectors (read only).
