- [x] **Lane T2: capture paths that still raised into training or dropped data (Nov 18, 2026, lane T2, branch `claude/lane-t2-capture-raises-dad9e1`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes T1, T2, T3, T4, T5 and T7 merged").** These are the four items the plan's sweep found after the review's six lanes, checked on `main` at `3f93636`. The code is commit `2bfb569`, in `capture.py` and `distributed.py`. Lane T1 edits `init`'s docstring; this lane did not touch it. No record field, detector, golden file, Parquet schema, file under `server/` or protocol number changed. The hand-over note, `lane-t2-capture-raises.md` in the session notes, lists every function touched.
  - *Outcomes.*
    - 1, the drift check's fingerprints raised before an `all_gather`: the session path is fixed. Direct callers of public `replica_spread` can still be left waiting, which needs a decision.
    - 2, a root parameter on `meta` put every capture buffer on `meta`: fixed, except under FSDP's CPU offload.
    - 3, router counting with a tensor subclass under `torch.compile`: reproduced, not fixed, needs a decision.
    - 4, `weights_unchanged` dropped for an unread first parameter: fixed.
  - *1. The drift check's fingerprints raised right before its `all_gather`.* On `main`, `parameter_fingerprints` raised on a parameter on `meta` ("Tensor on device meta is not on the expected device cpu!"), on a sparse COO one ("reshape is not implemented for sparse tensors") and on a sparse CSR one ("Sparse CSR tensors do not have is_contiguous"). It does so inside `Session.record_replica_spread`, before the collective. In the new two-rank gloo test, where only rank 0 has a `meta` parameter, rank 0 raised and the test's process context then ended rank 1. In a real job, rank 1 would be left waiting in the `all_gather`; that wait was not observed here. What the author chose is now built:
    - **Every rank gathers the same rows.** The session gathers `_fingerprint_table`, a `[n_params, width + 1]` tensor: each parameter's fingerprints, then a flag column.
      - The flag is 0.0 where the parameter was read. Otherwise it is a code for why not: on `meta`, nested, each sparse layout, or its reading raised. That row's values are zeros.
      - The tensor's shape depends only on how many parameters there are.
      - Whether a parameter can be read is judged from its device, layout and type, with no values read.
      - The rows that can be read are computed in one pass, as before, with the same values and seeds. Only if that pass raises is each parameter read on its own, and a raise flags only that parameter.
    - **Left out by its flag, never by its value.** After the gather, each rank takes the largest code any rank gave (`_spread_and_unread`). Every rank sees every rank's codes, so all leave out the same rows.
      - The flag travels on the device into the spread ring, which is one column wider. `_decode` drops flagged rows on the writer thread.
      - A record with no row left has no `replica_spread` at all: never an empty map, never zeros.
      - A NaN weight that was read still gives a NaN spread and reads as `non_finite` drift. Identical replicas still read `[0, 0, 0]`.
      - No host read is added to the step. The flag is read in `_flush`, where the ring is already on the CPU.
    - **Named once.** A `RuntimeWarning` at the next flush, the same on every rank: "TensorQuill is not comparing the parameter 'X' across replicas: <why>. It is left out of replica_spread, so the cross-GPU drift check does not cover it." The why is this rank's own reason, or "on another replica in the group, <reason>".
    - **Which not-checked channel.** No existing channel fits a parameter left out of the drift check: `moe_refused` and `update_not_read` become the `router` and `update_size` entries of `not_checked`, which name other kinds.
      - The channels used are the warning and the name's absence from `replica_spread`.
      - When every replicated parameter is left out, the records carry no `replica_spread`. `replica_drift` then reads `not_configured` (no record compared any parameter), never `checked`.
      - Reading `unavailable` with a reason would need a record field, which is a schema change.
    - **On the wire.** The gathered tensor is one column wider: 32 bytes per parameter per rank, or 24 without the projection.
      - Three test fakes of `dist.all_gather` copied `parameter_fingerprints` into the gathered list. They now copy `_fingerprint_table`.
      - Two are in `test_distributed.py`. One is in `test_evidence.py`, which is not this lane's file; only that fake changed.
    - **The public functions are unchanged.** `parameter_fingerprints` and `replica_spread` still raise on `meta` and sparse tensors for a direct caller. `replica_spread` raises before its gather, so a direct caller's ranks can still be left waiting. The session no longer calls it. See *Open*.
  - *2. A root parameter on `meta` put every capture buffer on `meta`.* On `main`, `Session.__init__` took `self._params[0].device`, and `flush` raised "Cannot copy out of meta tensor; no data!".
    - **What changed.** `_buffers_device` now takes the first trainable parameter not on `meta`, or the CPU when there is none. A model without a `meta` parameter gets the same device as before. No record field was added.
    - **The same model with `probe=`** probes as usual.
    - **Not covered: FSDP's CPU offload.** The parameters are on the CPU at `init` (FSDP2's `CPUOffloadPolicy` moves the sharded parameter with `.cpu()`, `_fsdp_param.py` at v2.6.0). The same rule therefore keeps the buffers on the CPU, and every block is left out (R1's note; by reading, not run). Fixing that is a different change with its own cost; see *Open*.
  - *3. Router counting with a tensor subclass under `torch.compile`: reproduced on PyTorch 2.15.0, not fixed. Not run on 2.6.*
    - **The first sweep did not reproduce it.**
      - It covered five subclasses that accept every op the hook makes:
        - a plain `__torch_function__` subclass;
        - one with its own `__torch_function__`;
        - a `_make_wrapper_subclass` wrapper, without `__tensor_flatten__` and with it;
        - PyTorch's `TwoTensor`.
      - It ran three router outputs, default and `indices=` routers, eager mode and the `eager`, `aot_eager` and inductor backends: 120 runs, and none raised.
      - With `fullgraph=True` (default routers), TensorQuill made no compile fail that passes without it. The hooks are traced there, not run eagerly after a graph break. The wrapper without `__tensor_flatten__` fails under Dynamo without TensorQuill too, so that case cannot be reached.
      - `TwoTensor` routers are counted twice: 12 assignments a step where the others give 6. `counts.add_(hits)` dispatches to the subclass, which applies the op to both of its tensors.
    - **Two subclasses that refuse an op the hook makes reproduce it.** They refuse the way DTensor refuses a plain tensor beside it. The model itself never mixes them and trains without TensorQuill. Default and `indices=` routers behave alike.
      - **A traceable wrapper whose `__torch_dispatch__` refuses a plain tensor argument.** With TensorQuill, training raises "aten.eq.Tensor got mixed torch.Tensor and Strict" under `backend="eager"`, with and without `fullgraph`. The raise comes from the hook's `idx == expert_ids`.
        - Under `aot_eager` and inductor it is counted correctly (6 a step). AOTAutograd unwraps the subclass; that is by reading.
        - In eager mode the router is refused, through the hook's `try`.
      - **A `__torch_function__` subclass that raises on `eq`.** With TensorQuill and `fullgraph=True`, every backend raises Dynamo's `Unsupported: Observed exception`. Without `fullgraph`, Dynamo breaks the graph, the hook runs eagerly and the router is refused.
    - **The layer statistics raise too.** The same strict wrapper as a watched block's output raises "aten.maximum.default got mixed torch.Tensor and Strict" under `backend="eager"`, with and without `fullgraph` (`_stats_hook`). Eager mode, `aot_eager` and inductor read or leave out the block without raising.
    - **Why it is not fixed.** No check on the type alone tells a subclass that refuses the mix from one counted correctly today, and the guard's rule is "only what raises today is refused". The options:
      - (a) Refuse every router output that is a tensor subclass, from `type(t) is not torch.Tensor` in `_unusable`, in eager mode and compiled.
        - It was tried in a scratch copy, not committed. No run above raised, `fullgraph=True` included, with no graph break. `test_compile.py`, `test_router_guard.py` and `test_capture_tensors.py` pass (107 passed, 4 skipped).
        - The cost: the four subclasses counted correctly today are refused (named in `moe_refused`), and so is `TwoTensor`, which ends its double count.
        - Tensor parallelism's `AsyncCollectiveTensor` appears only in eager mode; under tracing, `wait_tensor` returns a plain tensor (`_functional_collectives.py` at v2.6.0, by reading). Eager tensor-parallel routers would be refused under (a).
      - (b) Do (a) under `torch.compile` only, and leave eager mode as today, where the `try` catches what raises. Eager runs keep counting subclasses, `TwoTensor`'s double count included. Not tried.
      - (c) Leave it.
      - Whichever is chosen for routers, `_stats_hook` needs the same choice for the layer statistics.
    - **Scripts.** The scripts are in the session notes: `lane-t2-router-subclasses.py`, `lane-t2-router-subclasses-fullgraph.py`, `lane-t2-router-strict-subclasses.py` and `lane-t2-block-strict-subclass.py`. No test was added for item 3: a strict xfail could pass unexpectedly on PyTorch 2.6, where this has never run, and turn CI red.
  - *4. `weights_unchanged` was dropped for an unread first parameter.* `_decode` wrote the list only when the first parameter's check was known. It now writes it when the check knew any parameter.
    - A parameter the check did not know stays out of the list, as the others already did.
    - A check that knew none (the first check, or Apple MPS) still writes no list.
  - *Tests.* There is a new file, `sdk/tests/test_capture_raises.py`, with 12 tests; none needs the server binary. One runs two gloo ranks with a file store, only rank 0 having a `meta` parameter. It fails rather than hangs if a rank never finishes.
    - Run on `main`'s code, 10 of the 12 fail:
      - the session tests, on the `meta` fingerprint raise;
      - the others on "Cannot copy out of meta tensor", on a `meta` buffer device, on `KeyError: 'weights_unchanged'`, or on the new helpers missing.
    - The other 2 pass on `main`. They are guards: a spread passed in is still recorded whole, and a check that knew no parameter still writes no list.
  - *Run* on `2bfb569` (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15.0; the server built in the worktree with `cargo build --offline`).
    - With the binary (`TENSORQUILL_SERVER_BIN`): 1,811 passed (`main`: 1,799).
    - Without it (the variable unset, the binary moved out of `server/target`): 1,192 passed, 619 skipped (`main`: 1,180 and 619).
    - Without `pyarrow` (the session notes' `nopyarrow` shim) and without the binary: 1,068 passed, 80 skipped (`main`: 1,056 and 80).
    - `experiments/`: 92 passed (`main`: 92).
    - Each count is `main`'s plus the 12 new tests. Nothing failed, so nothing was rerun. Six other sessions ran suites on this machine at the same time. The binary is back in `server/target`.
  - *Not run.* Nothing ran on a GPU, NCCL, Linux or PyTorch 2.6.
    - These PyTorch APIs were added, and each was read at v2.6.0: `Tensor.is_nested`, `Tensor.layout` with the sparse layouts, `fill_`, `amax`, `torch.multiprocessing.start_processes` with `join(timeout)`, and `init_process_group(timeout=...)`.
    - CPU and MPS parameters together take the single pass; that was run. The parameter-at-a-time path runs only when the single pass raises, as with parameters on two GPUs; it ran only in a test that makes the pass raise.
  - *Open, for the author.*
    - **What public `replica_spread` and `parameter_fingerprints` should do for a direct caller** whose parameter cannot be read. Today they raise, and `replica_spread` raises before its gather. The options:
      - (a) Gather the flag column, then raise on every rank alike. This costs one host read of the gathered flags per call.
      - (b) Return NaN rows. These read as drift if recorded.
      - (c) Return zero rows. These read as agreement.
      - (d) Return the flags too. This changes the return shape.
    - **Item 3's subclass raises,** for routers and for layer statistics. The options are listed above.
    - **FSDP CPU offload: every block is left out** (by reading). The options:
      - (a) Leave it. Gradient, weight and update norms are read on the CPU with no transfer.
      - (b) Put the buffers on a DTensor parameter's mesh device (`device_mesh.device_type`, as FSDP2's `_get_device_from_mesh` does).
        - The hooks would read the blocks with no host wait.
        - But each step's gradient norms, clipped norm, weight check and update sizes are read on the CPU and copied to the GPU buffers with a blocking copy (`memcpy_and_sync` when `non_blocking` is false, `Copy.cu` at v2.6.0). That is a host wait for the GPU stream every step, unless those copies in `record_grads`, `_record_weights`, `_read_updates` and `record_grad_norm` are made non-blocking.
        - FSDP1's offload has no DTensor, so (b) does not cover it.
      - (c) Read the block outputs back to CPU buffers: a host wait at every forward, which R1 refused.
    - **Why a parameter was left out of the drift check is in no record.** A field such as `replica_not_read` would let `replica_drift` read `unavailable` with a reason, but it is a schema change.
    - **By reading, not run:** a rank whose replicated parameters are all on `meta` keeps its buffers on the CPU. If the other ranks gather on CUDA under NCCL, that rank's gather tensor is on the wrong device.
    - **A session made before `to_empty`.** `to_empty` replaces `meta` parameters with new objects (checked: the old object stays on `meta`), so such a session keeps reading the stale ones. This is not new, and no warning was added.

- [x] **An NVML call that never returns no longer holds training (Nov 18, 2026, lane T3, branch `claude/lane-t3-nvml-74e3e2`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes T1, T2, T3, T4, T5 and T7 merged").** The open item in the C entry ("a hung NVML call, which blocks the writer thread and, once the 8-block queue fills, training"). Code in `system.py` only, tests in `test_system.py`. `capture.py`, the record format, the detectors, the golden files, the server and the protocol number are unchanged.
  - *Found, on `main` at `3f93636`.* NVML was read on the writer thread (`SystemReadings.write` → `Nvml.read`). The `NVML_SLOW_SECONDS` check runs only after a call returns, so a call that never returns held the writer. Once the queue was full, `Session._put` made training wait for it. Reproduced on CPU with a fake `pynvml` whose `nvmlDeviceGetTemperature` blocks until the test releases it: at `every=1` (a queue of 8 blocks, one reading each), after 3 healthy steps, 40 more steps did not finish in 10 s, and the backpressure warning ("8 steps behind; training now waits for it") was raised. `test_a_hung_nvml_call_never_holds_training` fails on `main` with "training waited for a hung NVML call". Two more hangs were found the same way:
    - a hung `nvmlInit` at the first reading, which was never timed;
    - a hung `nvmlShutdown` at the end. `Session.close()` joins the writer, which shut NVML down in `_drain`'s `finally`, so `close()` itself hung.
  - *Read first.*
    - **wandb.** NVML is kept out of the user's process. Its Go `core` starts the `wandb-symon` sidecar (Rust), bounds the start and the stop at 5 s each and then kills it (`core/internal/monitor/symonresourcemanager.go`). The sidecar runs each collector in its own task (`symon/src/monitors.rs`), with no timeout on any one NVML call. wandb's older Python assets (removed in `a1d03799a`) sampled on daemon threads, and `finish()` joined them with no bound. Their `SystemMonitor` was built in `wandb/sdk/internal/handler.py`, inside what that package's docstring calls the "Internal process".
    - **mlflow's system-metrics monitor** is the same: a daemon thread, joined with no bound.
    - **Lightning** runs `nvidia-smi` with no timeout.
    - **No reference bounds a single NVML call.** TensorQuill reads in the training process, so it bounds the writer's wait instead.
    - **pynvml** loads the library with `CDLL` (wandb's vendored copy, `pynvml.py:2026`), so a call in progress releases the GIL, and a thread hung in NVML does not stop the others.
  - *Changed.*
    - **Every NVML call of the session now runs on one daemon thread, `tensorquill-nvml`**: the import, `nvmlInit`, the readings, the error path's close and `nvmlShutdown`. The writer thread starts it on its first reading, so nothing is added on the training thread. Once it exists, the writer never touches `Nvml`. `Nvml` itself is unchanged, its in-reader slow check included.
    - **The writer waits a bounded time for each reading** (`SystemReadings._ask`): `NVML_SLOW_SECONDS` (2 s), or `NVML_OPEN_SECONDS` (10 s, new) for the first reading, which also opens NVML.
    - **A reading not back by then is left behind**, and that flush gets the one note `{"unavailable": "an NVML reading took over 2 s; NVML is off for this session"}`. For the first reading the note is `the first NVML reading, which opens NVML, took over 10 s; …`. This is the shape `server/src/detect/system.rs` already reads. NVML is then off for the session.
    - **The stuck thread is never joined and never asked again.** If its call ever returns, the reading is dropped, and the thread shuts NVML down and exits.
    - **At close** (`SystemReadings.close`) the writer asks the thread to shut NVML down and waits at most `NVML_SLOW_SECONDS`. Once a reading has been left behind, it does not wait at all.
    - **When NVML is unavailable** (a CPU model, no `pynvml`, a failed `nvmlInit`), the thread exits after the one note.
  - *Choices for the author to revisit (none blocks).*
    - **The open is now bounded, at 10 s;** before, it was not timed. It gets more room than a reading so that an NVML that is only slow to start is not turned off; how long `nvmlInit` takes on a real node was not measured. A working open that takes longer than 10 s now turns NVML off.
    - **A reading that comes back after the bound** is written as the note on that flush. `main` kept it and wrote the note on the next flush. The format is the same; only that case's content differs. A reading back in time but over 2 s inside NVML (the in-reader check) is kept, as before.
    - **Training can still wait once, up to the bound** (2 s, or 10 s at the first reading), when the writer's queue fills during that wait. At `every=1` with fast CPU steps it does: the backpressure warning shows in two of the new tests.
  - *Tests.* Seven new tests in `test_system.py`:
    - a hung reading mid-run: every step is recorded, `cuda` stays on every flush, the one note is written, and close is bounded. Nothing is asked of NVML after the hang, not even a shutdown. Once released, the thread shuts NVML down and exits;
    - a hung open;
    - a hung shutdown at close;
    - a reading slower than the bound that does come back: one note, the late reading dropped, never asked again;
    - the thread's name, that it is a daemon, and that it ends with NVML;
    - an unavailable NVML ending its thread;
    - no thread for a session that never flushes.
    `FakeNvml` gains `hang`, `release` and `calls`. Its fixture releases any hung call at teardown, and the session tests close on a thread with a bounded wait, so a regression fails instead of hanging the run. Against `main`'s `system.py` (plus one shim line, `NVML_OPEN_SECONDS`, so the fixture can set it), 7 failed and 16 passed. The four behaviour tests fail for the reasons above; the other three because `main` has no NVML thread. The 16 existing tests are unchanged.
  - *Run* (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15, CPU, while six other sessions ran suites on the machine). Each count is `main`'s baseline plus the seven new tests, and nothing failed, so nothing was rerun:
    - with the server binary: 1,806 passed (baseline 1,799);
    - without it: 1,187 passed and 619 skipped (1,180 and 619);
    - without `pyarrow` or the binary: 1,063 passed and 80 skipped (1,056 and 80);
    - `experiments/moe-bakeoff/test_fixtures.py` and `experiments/gate1/test_gate1.py`: 92 passed.

    After these runs, `system.py`'s comment on `NVML_OPEN_SECONDS` was reworded. In `test_system.py`, the late-reading test's margin was widened (the reading now comes back 2 s after the bound, and the writer must return within 1 s of it, against 0.3 s and 0.25 s before), so a busy machine cannot fail it. `_written`'s docstring was corrected. `test_system.py` alone then passed three times in a row (23 passed each time); the four suites were not run again on that final tree.
  - *Not run.* Nothing on a GPU, on Linux, with the real `pynvml`, or on PyTorch 2.6 (this lane calls no new PyTorch API). A real hung driver was not reproduced, only the fake.
  - *Open.*
    - **Wording outside this lane's files is now stale.**
      - `sdk/README.md:768`, under Limits, still says a hung NVML call blocks the writer thread and then training. Line 755 still says NVML is "read on the writer thread".
      - The C entry's Open list above (line 625) still names the hung call; this entry supersedes it.
      - In `capture.py`, `_drain`'s comment still says "NVML is read here, on the writer thread".
      - **The backpressure warning can now name the wrong cause.** If the writer's queue fills during the one bounded NVML wait, `_put`'s warning blames a slow sink, when NVML is the cause.
    - **One existing test name** in `test_system.py`, `…_nvml_on_the_writer`, was left as it was. The test itself now checks that NVML is read on one thread that is not the trainer's.
    - **A process that keeps running after a hang keeps one thread stuck in the driver for its lifetime.** It is a daemon thread and does not keep the process from exiting.
    - **Hand-over note:** `lane-t3-nvml.md` in the session notes, which lists every function touched.

- [x] **Lane T4: RUST-05, RUST-01's h2 half, and which ranks the server compared for duplicate batches (Nov 18, 2026, lane T4, branch `claude/lane-t4-server-2a4bc3`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes T1, T2, T3, T4, T5 and T7 merged").** Three server items left open by the outside review of `78f8f7e` and by the entry "What was checked in the all-ranks view". Each was first checked on `main` at `3f93636`. Under `server/`, `src/detect.rs`, `src/detect/data.rs`, `src/ingest.rs` and `tests/ingest_test.rs` changed. Outside it, only the all-ranks `duplicate_batches` entry in `sdk/src/tensorquill/evidence.py` (`run_kind_checks`, a new `_ranks_compared`, and item 5 of the rule comment), its tests in `sdk/tests/test_kind_checks.py`, and two assertions in `sdk/tests/test_data_server.py`. The Parquet schema, the shape of `detect`'s output, the golden files and the protocol number (2) are unchanged. No golden file needed a change: `test_golden.py` passes unchanged with this branch's binary. Hand-over note: `lane-t4-server.md` in the session notes, with every function touched.
  - *RUST-05: reproduced, and the history lookup fixed.*
    - Found. Five hand-made `.jsonl` files whose steps go back within one attempt were each read with `load_records(..., all_attempts=True)` and judged by both paths. The script is `lane-t4-rust05-repro.py` in the session notes. On `main` all five differed from Python. A to D were run through the binary; E was run through `judge` on `main`'s `detect.rs`.
    - The cause, cases C, D and E. `AttemptPass.history` was a `Vec` in the order the rows came, binary-searched with `partition_point`.
      - C. Steps 3 to 5 were written again inside attempt 0, and attempt 1 resumes past the history's end. Rust's "last ten steps" were no longer in step order, so it extrapolated from steps 4 and 5: an expected learning rate of 0.00024, against Python's 0.00088.
      - D. The resume lands on a step written twice. Rust compared it with the first writing (0.00093); Python compares it with the last (0.00072).
      - E. A step written again with no learning rate. Python's history drops the step and extrapolates; Rust kept the first writing and compared at the same step.
    - The fix. The history is a `BTreeMap` from step to (attempt, learning rate), so the last writing of a step wins, as in Python's dict. A rollback is `split_off`, and a writing without a learning rate removes the step. `compare_resume` takes `same_step` from `get` and the last ten steps from `range(..resumed_at)`. A `BTreeMap` rather than a sorted insert into the `Vec`, because a reversed attempt would make that quadratic. Rows in step order, which is all a store holds, are read as before.
    - The test. `a_resume_is_compared_with_the_last_writing_of_each_step_when_an_attempt_went_back` (in `detect.rs`, through `judge`) holds Python's numbers for D, C and E, and the in-order case. On `main` it fails at D (0.00093 against 0.00072). On this branch C, D and E match Python through `build_evidence(detectors="rust")`.
    - **Not fixed, for the author: an attempt whose first row is not its lowest step (cases A and B).** `AttemptPass` judges the resume on an attempt's first row; Python judges it on the attempt's lowest step. `ResumePass` (`resume_data_mismatch`, `resume_incomplete`) makes the same assumption.
      - A. A resumed attempt written 15, 16, 17, then 12, 13, 14. Python reports `resume_lr_change` at 12; Rust reports nothing.
      - B. A later attempt written 5, 6, then 0, 1, 2. Python reads a restart of the numbering and reports `repeated_steps` on 0, 1, 2, 5 and 6; Rust reads a resume at 5 and reports nothing.
      - The options, with their costs:
        1. Leave it. `judge`'s doc already asks for each attempt's rows in recorded order, and only hand-made or concatenated files go backwards; the store never does. Cost: offline `detect` differs from Python on such files.
        2. Read each attempt's rows in step order in `judge_with`, with a stable sort so a step written twice keeps its writings' order. As an experiment, the records were sorted before they were sent (`lane-t4-rust05-sorted.py`). Then A to E, and a run with a stretch of steps moved earlier, match Python exactly. A run with a stretch written again, and a reversed run, differ only in the scalar context's `median_before`, which Python takes over the records in file order. Cost: a sort, and a second copy of each attempt's rows, in offline `detect`. It also changes what every detector reads on such input, and whether Python's file-order medians are right is a question of its own.
        3. Refuse such input in `judge_with`, as it already refuses attempts out of order. Cost: the Rust path fails where Python answers.
        4. Use the attempt's lowest step (`first_steps`, already computed) for the resume and restart checks only. Fixes A and B for `AttemptPass` and `ResumePass`, and no other detector.
    - **The other detectors differ on such input too, on `main` and here alike.** One attempt of the repository's `everything()` run (`lane-t4-rust05-wide.py`): a stretch of steps moved earlier gives 28 differences, a stretch written again 8, the whole attempt reversed 3. Python sorts by step in most detectors; Rust reads the rows in the order they come. This is the same choice as option 2, and nothing was changed for it.
  - *RUST-01, the h2 half: does not reproduce, and nothing changed.* The probes are in the session notes, not in the repository.
    - Read in the sources. h2 0.4.20's `RecvStream::drop` clears the buffer, sets `is_recv = false`, and sends no reset. Once the response is sent while the request is still streaming, `maybe_cancel` sends `RST_STREAM(NO_ERROR)`.
    - Probe A (`lane-t4-h2-probes.rs`). Lane R6 opened one connection per stream; this probe put 300 refused streams on one tonic connection, 30 at a time, each with 4 MiB queued behind its refused 1 MiB batch.
      - All 300 read their own status: 150 `ResourceExhausted` and 150 `InvalidArgument`, median 35 ms, at most 163 ms.
      - No stuck stream. Every client's request side was let go within 10 ms of its status.
      - No leak. A healthy stream on the same connection afterwards was acknowledged, and the buffered bytes went back to the holder's row, then to 0.
    - The real client (`lane-t4-h2-pyarrow-probe.py`). The binary with `--quota-mib 1`, through pyarrow's Flight client, the one the SDK's sender uses. No SDK test sends a refused stream to the real server.
      - 160 refused streams on their own clients and 160 on one shared client, each writing 4 MiB more: all 320 read `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED: ingest buffer full`, and none hung (at most 0.15 s).
      - A healthy stream on the shared client and the holder's stream were acknowledged, and the server exited 0.
    - **The one wait found: a refusal behind a flush of its own rank.** Rank 0's end-of-stream flush was writing to a store that takes 3 s a request, and a second stream of rank 0 was refused with 4 MiB queued behind the refused batch. Both probes got their own status 9.0 s later (the part, the replay's reads and `incidents.json`), and both had their request side let go at once. The wait is bounded by the store, and it needs a second stream of a rank while its first is still flushing.
      - Probe B, another rank's rows. The wait is in `accept`, which takes the rank's buffer lock before it reads a row. The request stream is still held during it, and the review's reordering would not change it.
      - Probe C, a wrong `schema_version`: the state the review describes. The refusal is raised before `accept`, so `run_stream` returns with the key set and the request stream is dropped. `do_put` then waits in `detach` for the same lock, with the response open. The client still read `InvalidArgument: schema_version 2 is not supported`, with no h2 error. Here the review's reordering would remove the wait. A stream refused before it stored a batch never holds the buffer's acknowledgement, so its `detach` changes nothing but waits for the lock.
      - An option for the author, not taken because nothing went wrong: send the status before `detach`, or skip `detach` for a stream that never stored a batch. The cost is a change in `do_put`'s order; for a stream that did store batches, an acknowledgement from a flush could then come after its error, and tonic would drop it.
  - *Duplicate batches: the status always carries `ranks_compared`, and the all-ranks entry reads it.*
    - Found on `main`. `with_data` wrote the comparison into the status only when it found a duplicate. The new ingest test fails there at its first assertion: the key is absent (`Null`) where `[]` is expected.
    - The server.
      - Every run's status now has a top-level `ranks_compared`: the ranks with a hashed batch in their window, an empty list when there are none. It is made by a new `detect::ranks_compared`, which `duplicate_batches` and `detect --run` now share, so the list means the same in all three. The finding keeps its own copy, which `evidence.py` and `test_data_server.py` read there.
      - The list is in the signature that decides whether a status is written again, so a status where only it changed is written, a finished run's too. That one line was read, not tested on its own: in the test, every write that changes the list also changes a rank's step. A restarted server's status says `[]` until a rank's store is read again.
      - The test `the_status_names_the_ranks_whose_batches_were_compared_found_or_not` walks through `[]`, `[0]`, `[0, 1]` with no finding, the file, a new `Ingest` over the same store (`[]`, then `[1]`), and a duplicate whose top-level list equals the finding's.
    - The SDK rule, with a status that carries the list:
      - A judged rank the list names is `checked`, when the list has at least two ranks; one rank alone compared with nothing.
      - A judged rank it does not name is `not_configured`, unless its own report says its data could not be read, which stays `unavailable`.
      - The run's value is the worst judged rank's, as for every cross-rank kind.
      - The judged ranks that recorded `data` but are not named are listed under `not_compared_ranks`, with a `note` naming both causes: the server has not read their store since it started (none right after a restart), or their batches have no hash.
      - A status without the key, or with it in another shape, is read as one from an older server: what the ranks recorded, with the old note, unchanged. Finished runs keep the file an older server wrote.
    - **Why `not_configured` and not `unavailable` for a rank the list leaves out (the author may overrule).**
      - `unavailable` must be named by a `not_checked` entry (or `no_status`), and it makes the run `degraded`.
      - Making it so would degrade every watched run after each server restart, until each rank flushes again.
      - It would degrade a rank whose `data` has no `batch_hash` for good: an `IterableDataset`, which no report can show.
      - The rule already gives `unavailable` only when a rank's own report says its input could not be read.
      - The cost of `not_configured`: right after a restart the entry reads like a run with no data capture, except for `not_compared_ranks` and the note.
    - The tests.
      - New: `test_duplicate_batches_is_checked_on_the_ranks_the_servers_status_says_it_compared`. It covers both ranks listed, a restart (`[]`: `not_configured`, both named, run state `healthy`, one kind fewer on the count line), one rank of three not yet compared, a rank without data, one rank listed, and three malformed lists. On `main`'s `evidence.py` it fails at its first assertion: the old note is there.
      - The existing test for `duplicate_batches` is unchanged, and now covers the status without the key.
      - `test_ranks_drawing_the_same_batch_show_in_the_runs_status` (real binary) now also checks the top-level list and that the entry is `checked` with no note.
    - This closes the open item of "What was checked in the all-ranks view" on `duplicate_batches`.
  - *Run.* macOS, offline, with six other lanes running suites on the same machine (load averages 13 to 20).
    - Rust:
      - `cargo build`, and `cargo fmt --check`.
      - `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, with and without default features.
      - `cargo test`: 135 passed and 1 ignored (`main`: 133 and 1).
      - `cargo test --no-default-features --lib`: 98 passed (`main`: 97).
    - Python, from `sdk/`:
      - With this branch's binary: 1,800 passed, none skipped (`main`: 1,799). The one more is the new test in `test_kind_checks.py`.
      - Without it (the variable unset and the binary moved aside): 1,181 passed, 619 skipped (`main`: 1,180 and 619).
      - Without `pyarrow` or the binary: 1,057 passed, 80 skipped (`main`: 1,056 and 80). Runs 2 and 3 ran at the same time; no run had a failure to rerun.
      - `experiments/`: 92 passed (`main`: 92).
  - *Not run.* Linux, a GPU, S3 or GCS. A restart through the SDK's sender with live ranks; the ingest test restarts in process. Lane E's 64- and 1,024-rank runs, whose h2 errors R6 traced to lost connections.
  - *Open.*
    - For the author: RUST-05's cases A and B, and the reading order of offline `detect` (options 1 to 4 above). The `not_configured` rule for a rank the status does not list. Whether a stream refused before it stored a batch should get its status before `detach` (probe C).
    - Docs outside this lane's files still describe the old note: `sdk/AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` (the "A run folder" section) and `sdk/README.md` ("What was checked, limits"). The README's `watchdog.json` text does not mention `ranks_compared`.
    - A rank's report does not say whether its batches have a hash, so "no hash" and "not read since the restart" share one note.
    - R6's follow-ups stand: the server logs nothing when it refuses a stream.

- [x] **`compare` and `runs`: a note is not a series, and what a rank's file name, the manifest's world size and a shared file say (Nov 18, 2026, lane T5, branch `claude/lane-t5-compare-runs-01b491`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes T1, T2, T3, T4, T5 and T7 merged").** The five open items on `compare` and `runs` from "A refused router leaves no entry" and "Two gaps left by ... closed", checked on `main` at `3f93636` after lane R5's changes to `compare.py`. All five still held, and each was reproduced on CPU before any change. Each has a test that fails on `3f93636` and passes with the fix. Only `divergence.py`, `runs.py`, `compare.py` and their tests changed. `evidence.py` (lane T4's this wave), the MCP tool's row keys, the detectors, the golden files and everything under `server/` are untouched, and the listing gained no key.
  - *1. A note under `not_compared`.* Reproduced: a record carrying `moe_refused` (a refused router) or `update_not_read` (an optimizer whose update size is not read) put that key in part 4's `left_out.not_compared` beside `lr`. Both are notes, written once on the first record after the capture knew. They say why a series is missing; they are not data. The evidence already turns each into a `not_checked` entry (`refused_routers_not_checked`, `update_not_read_not_checked`), which part 2 shows as `unavailable`. `divergence._NOTE_KEYS` now skips them as `_IDENTITY_KEYS` are skipped, so they are neither compared nor listed. Data that is not compared is still listed as before. Left as they are: `moe_stats`, `moe_top_k`, `optimizer_ownership`, `weight_dtypes`, `weights_unchanged`, `step_skipped` and `grad_scale`. These are record data. They are listed as not compared, as the C3 entry lists `lr`, `probe` and `checkpoint`. On the recorded Granite, SmolLM2 and Trainer captures, the list is `lr`, `moe_stats`, `moe_top_k`, `optimizer_ownership`, `weights_unchanged`, and `probe`, `step_skipped` and `grad_scale` where they were recorded.
  - *2a. A file named for a rank was trusted to hold it alone.* Reproduced: `x.rank0.jsonl` holding ranks 0 and 1, and `y.rank0.jsonl` holding rank 1 only, were each listed as one rank, with a count of 1 and no note. `compare` read two ranks of both, so the listing and `compare` disagreed. For `y`, `compare` also said rank 0's file "holds no whole record yet", which is false.
    - The listing now reads the first line and the last megabyte of every file, not only files of their own name. A file named `<run_id>.rank<N>.jsonl` is trusted to hold rank N alone when its first and last records are rank N's. Otherwise it is read as a file of its own name: its name and its ends are among the ranks seen, and the count is settled only when the manifest's `world_size` equals them. Otherwise `rank_count` is null, and `rank_count_not_known` names the file ("is named for rank 0 and its first and last records name rank 1, so may hold other ranks"). A lone such file is `ranks_unknown`, by the rule for a lone file of several ranks.
    - `compare` now gives the reason as "its file y.rank0.jsonl holds no record of rank 0: its whole records are rank 1's".
    - Cost: for a run of several named files, one more first line per file. The last megabyte of a file of its own name is now read once where it was read twice, because the row reuses what the count read; a named file's is read once, as before. No file is read whole, and no detector runs; every new listing test fails if one does. Timed (best of 5):
      - 64 rank files of 30 Granite-sized records (43 KB a line): 60 ms, against 42 ms on `main`.
      - The recorded Granite, SmolLM2 and Trainer folders: the same rows and times, 4.5 ms against 4.3 ms for the Granite folder.
  - *2b. A local run with fewer rank files than its `world_size`.* Reproduced: a manifest of world size 2 beside one `.rank0.jsonl` file listed one rank, `healthy`, with a current evidence file. `compare` read it as a run of one rank. The store already counts such a rank (`_ranks_without_folder`: "the job's world_size is 2 and nothing is stored for this rank"), and the local files now do the same.
    - When every file holds its rank alone, the ranks of `range(world_size)` with no file are counted and unknown. The row has `ranks` [0, 1], `rank_count` 2, run state `unknown` (`unhealthy` when rank 0 shows an incident), evidence `ranks_unknown`, and the note "rank 1 has no file: the run manifest says world size 2". The `end` cell is `not_closed` with that rank, since a rank with no file closed none. When a file of its own name or a misnamed one is in the run, the count rule above applies instead.
    - `compare` reads such a run whole, with the rank unknown: "the run manifest says world size 2, and no file holds a record of this rank". Part 4 compares the ranks that have records and names the other under `ranks_without_records`.
    - Against a run of one rank, part 4 now answers `ranks_differ` where it compared rank 0 with rank 0. That is the existing rule: with another world size a rank reads another share of the data. `rank=N` reads one rank as before.
    - When a run has fewer files than its world size. Every rank is to call `init` ("capture on every rank" in the README), and the callbacks do: Lightning's `on_fit_start` broadcasts the run ID, so every rank runs it, and the Hugging Face `on_train_begin` runs on every process. A run still has fewer files than its world size in three cases: when `init` was called on some ranks only, when the ranks generated separate run IDs with no process group (which `tracker._unshared_generated_id` warns of), and while a rank has not written its first record. In each, that rank's records are not in this run's files, so it is unknown, not clean. A user who captures on rank 0 only now sees `unknown` where they saw `healthy`.
    - A run given as the path of one rank's file finds its manifest but does not look for its other files. Such a rank is "not read: only b.rank0.jsonl was given, and the run manifest says world size 2", never "has no file". `discover_runs` marks such a run `files_given`; that is a key of the run, not of the row.
    - One existing assertion changed on purpose. In `test_a_file_named_for_its_rank_or_of_one_rank_is_counted_as_before`, a `.rank0.jsonl` file with `world_size` 2 was counted as 1; it is now `[0, 1]`, 2. The file is still trusted, so the row has no `rank_count_not_known`.
  - *2c. A shared file in a run of several files.* Reproduced: `w.rank0.jsonl` (healthy) beside `extra.jsonl`, which holds ranks 1 and 2 with a spike on rank 2, and an evidence file written with `--rank 2`. The row read that evidence as rank 1's, said `current` with 2 of 3 ranks judged and 0 unknown, and would have said `healthy` had the spike been absent.
    - The rule for a lone shared file now applies inside a run of several files, and its evidence is given to no rank. Its ranks are unknown unless a file of their own judges them. Its incidents are listed with no rank, and the run is `unhealthy` if it shows any; otherwise it is `unknown`, never `healthy`.
    - An incident with the same identity as a judged rank's (`evidence._incident_identity`, the view's rule) is counted once. `ranks_with_incidents` names only judged ranks. The note says "extra.jsonl holds ranks 1, 2, and its evidence file is one rank's, without saying which".
    - Not chosen: giving the evidence to a rank. The evidence file does not name the rank it judged. Making it do so is `evidence.py`'s, and that would fix this at the source (a follow-up for lane T4's file).
  - *2d. The last step of a shared file.* Reproduced: a file whose last line is rank 1's step 59, while rank 0 has written step 60, was listed at step 59. `compare` said 60. A run of several files and a store already give the furthest rank's step, the maximum over ranks.
    - For a file that is not trusted to hold one rank, the listing now walks back through the last megabyte it already reads and takes each rank's last line. The row's `last_step` is the maximum of those steps. "One step ahead of another rank's", the wording of the item, is what that maximum means for every run of several ranks.
    - The real gap was the last line being behind. Each rank's last line, not the largest step in the tail: a resumed rank's rolled-back steps come before its last line, and a test pins that (a rank resumed from step 50 that is now at 100 is listed at 100, not at the 119 its first attempt reached).
    - The tail is parsed only where the row uses it: a file of several ranks in a run of several files, or a lone file read as several ranks. A lone two-rank file of 200 Granite-sized records a rank lists in 8.0 ms, against 1.3 ms on `main`.
    - This reading of "last step" follows precedent, not a decision the author made for this case: the maximum is what a store and a run of several files already give.
  - *From the reference repos.* PyTorch's Flight Recorder analyser takes each dump's rank from its file name (`components/loader.py`, `read_dump`). It compares the ranks it has dumps for with the ranks the process groups expect, and names the missing ones (`check_no_missing_dump_files`, "Missing dump files from ranks ..."); it does not analyse what is there as if it were all. That is the rule for 2b, as the store already applies it. Here the records carry their rank, so the name is checked where the listing already reads a record (2a). MLflow's file store lists runs from small meta files and opens no metric file (`_list_run_infos`), which is why the listing still reads no file whole.
  - *Tests.* `test_divergence.py` gains 1 test. `test_runs_compare.py` gains 4, with the helpers `cheap` (the listing runs no detector and reads no file whole) and `judged`. Their output on `3f93636`: 5 failed. The case of a run given as one rank file's path was added after that run, to the 2b test; on `3f93636` that test already fails at its first assertion. With the fixes, the five test files that touch `runs` and `compare` (`test_runs_compare.py`, `test_divergence.py`, `test_mcp_server.py`, `test_run_manifest.py`, `test_stall_evidence.py`) gave 183 passed.
  - *Run* (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15, on the final tree; the server was built here with `cargo build --offline`). The SDK suite in `sdk/`, with the binary in `TENSORQUILL_SERVER_BIN`: 1,804 passed, none skipped (`main`: 1,799). Without it (the variable unset, the binary moved out of `server/target`): 1,185 passed, 619 skipped (`main`: 1,180 and 619). Without `pyarrow` (the `nopyarrow` stub folder on `PYTHONPATH`) and without the binary: 1,061 passed, 80 skipped (`main`: 1,056 and 80). `experiments/` (`moe-bakeoff/test_fixtures.py`, `gate1/test_gate1.py`): 92 passed. The 5 more in each are the new tests. All four were run on the code as committed, after its last change, and once before that change with the same counts. Nothing failed, so nothing was rerun alone. No Rust check was run: nothing under `server/` changed.
  - *Not run.* Linux, a GPU, PyTorch 2.6, a real job that writes several ranks into one `.jsonl` file, and a real job of more than two ranks. The tests write such files by hand.
  - *For the author.*
    - 2b changes what a user sees. A local run with fewer rank files than its manifest's world size is `unknown` (or `unhealthy`), not `healthy`, and part 4 against a run of one rank answers `ranks_differ`, where it compared rank 0 with rank 0. That includes a run captured on rank 0 only. This mirrors the store's rule; the author may want it otherwise for local files.
    - 2d reads a shared file's last step as its furthest rank's, by precedent.
  - *Open.*
    - Only the first and last records are checked. A file named for rank N whose ends are rank N's but that holds another rank in between is still trusted, and so is a lone file of its own name with matching ends (as before).
    - The evidence file beside a `.jsonl` file does not say which rank it judged. With that in `evidence.py`, a shared file's incidents could be given to their rank.
    - Found, not fixed (outside this lane's items):
      - `runs._local_runs` keeps one file per rank (`files.setdefault`). A file of its own name whose first record is rank N, in a run that also has `<run_id>.rank<N>.jsonl`, leaves one of the two files out of the run, for `runs` and `compare` alike. Seen: `extra.jsonl`, starting with rank 0, took rank 0 and `w.rank0.jsonl` was left out. Fixing it means keying a run's files by path, not rank, which `compare` and the MCP tool read.
      - `compare`'s `last_step` for a local run counts every attempt (`all_attempts=True`), so a resumed rank gives the step its rolled-back attempt reached (119 where the listing now says 100). It is used for `ended_before_step`.
    - The README's `runs` paragraph still says a `.rank<N>.jsonl` file holds rank N alone and does not mention the world size count. It is not this lane's file; the new rule is in `runs.py`'s text, which is the `--help`.
    - Nothing is pushed or merged.

- [x] **M1, the TrainCheck part: TrainCheck's 20 silent errors run against TensorQuill on CPU (Nov 18, 2026, lane T7, branch `claude/lane-t7-traincheck-580c4c`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes T1, T2, T3, T4, T5 and T7 merged").** Everything is in a new folder, `experiments/traincheck-bugs/`: a script for each bug that could run, a shared harness, `run_all.py`, the 20-row catalog, `RESULTS.md` (written from `results.json`), a README and three tests. TensorQuill itself is unchanged. Every run starts it with `tensorquill.init(model, capture="full", updates=True, ...)`, watches the loaders, logs the loss, and builds the evidence with `build_evidence`. Each script also measures its failure with plain PyTorch (weights unchanged, replicas apart, repeated augmentations, a wrong sigma), so "nothing fired" can be told apart from "the bug did not happen". Each has a healthy variant written here, for false incidents. CPU only (PyTorch 2.15, Python 3.12, macOS); the two-process runs use gloo. One seed per configuration, nothing timed.
  - *Where the reproductions are.* Only 2 of the 20 scripts are on this machine. B1's `84911.py` is in the clone's tree (`docs/assets/code/`). B8's `115607_bug.py` is in its history only (deleted in `471f57e`). B3 has only a config in the history, which points at an external Megatron-DeepSpeed checkout. The other reproductions are in `OrderLab/TrainCheck-Evaluation-Workloads` (TrainCheck's AE guide), which was not fetched: a download, and the user's call. Every path in the clone's history was searched, `bugs.md` (deleted in `dfd8d84`) included. So the rows are run at levels that must not be added together:
    - TrainCheck's script: B8, kept as written except for one keyword 2.15 refuses (`save_config`).
    - TrainCheck's script with the model and data swapped: B1 (EfficientNet-b0 and CIFAR-100 need downloads; its training loop, freezing loops and optimizer line are kept).
    - Re-created from Table 3's synopsis: B4, B9, B10, B14, B19.
    - Simulated: B3 (the clipping mechanism, without DeepSpeed), B6 (the pre-1.9 worker seeding put back), and the effect only of B2, B17 and B18 (one shared run, as TrainCheck reuses one trace for the three).
    - Not run: B5, B7, B11, B12, B13, B15, B16, B20.
  - *Caught: a kind that fires on the buggy run and not on the healthy one.*
    - **B10** (forward on `ddp.module`) and **B9** (`.to()` after DDP puts new parameters in place, so DDP's hooks stay on the old ones; the trigger used is `overwrite_module_params_on_conversion`, the issue's own was not read), both re-created: `replica_drift` at step 49, the first cross-rank check, on both ranks. TrainCheck reports within one iteration; with `replica_every=1` B10 is seen at step 0, its first update. The two are one measurement: the same training with the gradient sync lost two ways (the replicas end 0.1107 apart in both).
    - **B3, simulated** (two ranks as one tensor-parallel group, clipping on rank 0 only, as the BLOOM-176B account has it): `replica_drift` at step 49, or step 1 with `replica_every=1`. The LayerNorm weights end 2e-3 apart while the two runs' losses end at 0.6791 and 0.6794.
    - **B1, swapped:** caught in 2 of its 4 configurations. `stalled_weights` fires at step 19 with weight checks every 5 steps, and at step 199 over 200 steps at the default 50. The incident names the 12 BatchNorm parameters that get gradients and says no optimizer holds them: the root cause. It takes two conditions:
      - Enough weight checks. At TrainCheck's own length (22 steps) with the defaults no check falls in the run, and nothing fires.
      - `init` called before the freezing loops (the first gap below).
    - **The simulated P2P effect:** one rank's first-layer weight loses a tenth of its elements at step 75; `replica_drift` at 99, the next check.
  - *Not caught, though the failure showed.*
    - **B4** (no `zero_grad`, on TrainCheck's own `mnist.py`): the gradient norm climbs from 5.2 to 38 over 102 steps and the model stays at chance. `grad_spike` does not fire, since the median rises with it. `update_spike` cannot read Adadelta (listed under `not_checked`).
    - **B6, simulated:** 75% of the augmentations repeat across the four workers. TensorQuill hashes sample indices, which stay distinct, and `duplicate_batches` compares ranks, not workers. The review's reading counted B6 as within reach; in this simulation, this lane's reading of a one-line synopsis, it is not caught.
    - **B14** (two LSTM tensors left out of a checkpoint, then a resume with `strict=False`): `resume_incomplete` checks which components a checkpoint holds, not whether the model's tensors are all in it. The manifest records the model's tensor count and a hash of its keys, but no rule compares them with the model. With no RNG state or data position saved, `resume_incomplete` fires on the healthy run too, as an observation.
    - **B19** (SpectralNorm evaluated before any training forward): the first evaluation divides each weight by -0.038 and -0.020 times its true sigma, validation loss 1517 against 3.07. It happens outside the training steps, which is all TensorQuill records.
  - *Ran, but no test.* **B8**'s bug does not happen on 2.15: the compiled loop's Adam step counter equals eager's. And `tensorquill.init` cannot start inside the script as written. The script builds its model inside the compiled function, Dynamo traces `init`, and it raises an `AssertionError` on `mark_static_address` into the user's code. Wrapping `init` and `session.log` in `torch.compiler.disable` works.
  - *Not run, and why.*
    - **Missing packages or downloads:** B5 and B13 need Transformers versions with the bug (5.23.0 is installed) and Hub files; B7 needs lightning-thunder; B11 Pixtral's processor files; B12 flash-attn on a CUDA GPU; B16 DeepSpeed's MoE on several ranks; B20 mmpretrain with mmcv. Each row in `RESULTS.md` says what it would need.
    - **Out of TensorQuill's reach as built,** judged from the synopses and its code, not tested: B5 (lengths), B7 (activation dtypes), B11 (batch shapes after the processor), B12 (evaluation), B13 (model loading), B15 (planned step count; TrainCheck misses it too) and B20 (which parameters are meant to be frozen). B16's hang needs the server's watchdog or NCCL Flight Recorder dumps, which a gloo CPU run here does not produce.
  - *False incidents.* `activation_drift` needs 60 records, so it judges 12 of the 17 healthy configurations; B1's three 22-step runs and B8's two 6-step runs are too short.
    - Anomalies: `activation_drift` fired on 11 of the 12, and one `activation_spike` (B4). The 12 are seven distinct trainings (B9, B10, B10 every-step and the hardware run are one healthy training; B3's two and B14's two one each), so it fired on 6 of 7, B1's 200-step run the only clean one. It fired on 10 of the 12 buggy runs long enough to judge, so it never counts as a catch.
    - Observations: one `resume_incomplete`, B14's with the minimal checkpoint (no RNG state or data position saved).
    - Invariants: none.
  - *Gaps found, not fixed* (TensorQuill was not to be changed here):
    - **`init` placement.** It tracks only the parameters that need gradients at that moment.
      - Started just before B1's training loop, after the freezing loops, it took the optimizer, which holds only frozen parameters, as a foreign one and recorded no step at all. Its one warning names only DeepSpeed ZeRO and Megatron as causes, and the evidence was empty, run state `unknown`.
      - Started right after `model.to(device)`, which in `84911.py` comes before the freezing, it catches the bug.
      - The guide ("after the model is on its device and before `torch.compile`") allows both.
    - **`init` under Dynamo.** It raises when traced (B8).
    - **`resume_incomplete`** does not compare a checkpoint's model tensors with the model (B14).
    - **Batch hashing** sees indices, not content (B6).
    - **`grad_spike`** does not see a steady climb (B4).
    - **`replica_drift`** at its default 50 steps sees a desync up to 49 steps late.
  - *What it means for M1.* The TrainCheck part is not finished as M1 states it ("how many each catches"). That needs TrainCheck's workloads repository (a download), the library versions each bug lived in (installs), and GPUs for at least the three hardware rows, B3, B12 and B16. What these runs do say:
    - By level, never summed: none caught with TrainCheck's script as shipped (B8's bug does not show on 2.15). B1 caught with its script swapped, with enough weight checks and `init` before the freezing. B9 and B10 caught as re-created, one measurement. B3 caught as simulated, and the hardware effect too.
    - Not caught where the failure showed: B4, B14 and B19 (re-created) and B6 (simulated). Three of the four fall to existing kinds that look at the wrong thing (`grad_spike`, the batch hash, `resume_incomplete`).
    - Of the review's seven "within reach": B1 caught (swapped, two conditions), B9 and B10 caught (re-created), B3 caught (simulated), B6 not caught (simulated), B8 untestable on 2.15, B16 not run.
  - *Run.* `run_all.py` with the project venv: 34 runs (17 configurations, buggy and healthy), 33 completed; the one that did not is B8 compiled without `torch.compiler.disable`, the `init` failure above. B1's stand-in model first had blocks without a `forward` of their own, so no activation statistics were recorded; it now calls `block(x)` as EfficientNet does, and B1 was run again with the same incidents. The `experiments/` tests from `sdk/`: 95 passed (the 92 and this folder's 3). The SDK suite was not run, since no SDK file changed. Nothing ran on a GPU, NCCL or Linux. Nothing is pushed.

- [x] **Lane T1: run identity across ranks (Nov 18, 2026, lane T1, branch `claude/lane-t1-run-identity-a8d17d`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Lanes T1, T2, T3, T4, T5 and T7 merged").** Four open items from the sweep after the R1–R6 merges: R3's broadcast under NCCL and a `run_id` passed on rank 0 only (both from the Open list of "Lanes R6, R4, R5, R3 and R1 merged"), the callbacks' `project=` and `name=` (from "Projects, run names and comparing runs"), and the docs R3 left incomplete. Code in `tracker.py`, `huggingface.py` and `lightning.py`; in `capture.py` only `init`'s docstring. No record field, detector, golden file, Gate 1 fixture, file under `server/` or protocol number changed.
  - *Read first.*
    - **PyTorch at `v2.6.0`.**
      - `broadcast_object_list` puts its tensors on `_get_object_coll_device`, which under NCCL is CUDA's current device.
      - `distributed_c10d._get_default_store()` returns the default group's store, with the group's own prefix on every key (`_new_process_group_helper`). PyTorch itself uses the default store this way (`torch/distributed/_transport/_api.py`).
      - `Store.set`, `get`, `check`, `wait(keys, timedelta)` and the `timeout` property are all bound in `init.cpp`.
      - torchrun's agent creates a new TCPStore for each restart (`rendezvous.py`).
    - **A TCPStore client does one operation at a time.** `set`, `get`, `check` and `wait` each hold one mutex for their whole length (`activeOpLock_`). `ProcessGroupNCCL`'s `globalStore_` is the same underlying TCPStore as the default store. With `TORCH_NCCL_DUMP_ON_TIMEOUT=1` or `TORCH_DISTRIBUTED_DEBUG=DETAIL` (off by default), NCCL's heartbeat monitor `check`s it every second, and the watchdog `set`s a dump signal on a timeout. One long `wait` would hold both up, though for less than the monitor's 480 s heartbeat timeout. So the ranks poll with `check` rather than wait.
    - **Accelerate and Lightning.** Accelerate (`utils.operations.broadcast_object_list`) and Lightning (`DDPStrategy.broadcast`) share objects with `broadcast_object_list`, after setting each process's device: `PartialState.set_device`, and Lightning's `setup_environment`, which runs before `on_fit_start`. Accelerate's trackers run on the main process only and share no run ID.
    - **W&B's `init_timeout`** defaults to 90 s.
  - *Item 1: sharing the ID touches no GPU.* Confirmed open on `main` by reading the code; not run, since there is no NCCL here. `resolve_run` and `huggingface._shared_tracker_run_id` each called `broadcast_object_list`.
    - **What it does now.** Under a process group of several ranks, `resolve_run` calls `_shared_run`. Rank 0 resolves the whole order, puts `[run ID, source]` as JSON under `tensorquill/run_id/<n>` in the default store, and never waits. Every other rank polls for that key with `check` (10 ms, doubling to 1 s), then reads it with `get`. `n` counts the exchanges in each process, so a second `init` reads its own key.
    - **The bound.** A rank polls for at most `RUN_ID_WAIT_SECONDS`: 300 s, or the store's own timeout if that is shorter.
    - **Failures.** Any failure gives the rank an ID of its own, by the same order (its explicit one, its tracker's, the launcher's or a generated one), and one warning: no store, MPI's placeholder store, the bound running out, an unreadable entry. A failed write on rank 0 gives one warning. Nothing raises.
    - **The Hugging Face callback.** Its own exchange is removed; it passes its arguments to `init`, which resolves and shares the ID. Its runs now say `run_id_source: "wandb"` or `"mlflow"`, where they said `"explicit"` (R3's open point). A tracker that raises on rank 0 is passed over on this path too.
  - *Item 2: a `run_id` on some ranks only.* Reproduced on `main` with two Gloo processes and the collectives allowed:
    - With a `run_id` on rank 0 only, rank 1 sat in the broadcast until gloo's 15 s timeout raised. With one on rank 1 only, rank 0 raised the same way.
    - Both callbacks had the same fault: each entered its own collective only on ranks with no `run_id`. Lightning's `ddp_spawn` with a `run_id` on rank 0 only failed with `ProcessRaisedException`.
    - **Decided here.** Under a group of several ranks, every rank exchanges whatever it was given. `run_id` is the first step of rank 0's order, so rank 0's resolution wins on every rank, as for the other sources. A rank given a different `run_id` from rank 0's writes under rank 0's, with one warning. A rank whose bound runs out keeps its own.
    - **The Lightning callback** no longer broadcasts. Rank 0 passes the logger's run ID (or its own `run_id`), the other ranks pass what they were given, and `init` shares rank 0's. Making the broadcast unconditional instead is the other way; both are under *Open*.
  - *Item 3: the callbacks' `project=` and `name=`.* Not reproduced. Both callbacks pass every keyword argument they do not use to `init`, so `TensorQuillCallback(project=..., name=...)` already reaches the manifest. Run on `main` for both callbacks, in one process. Nothing changed for it.
  - *Item 4: the docs.* Changed: `sdk/README.md`'s run ID row and `clip_grad_norm` field, and the `run_id` and `clip_norm` paragraphs of `init`'s docstring. They now say:
    - rank 0's whole resolution, a `run_id` included, is every rank's, through the store, with the bound and the fallback;
    - a clip call counts when any of the model's trainable parameters is in it;
    - only an L2 norm is recorded.
  - *Tests.* 13 new; none needs the server binary.
    - **`sdk/tests/test_run_id_ranks.py`:** 16 runs of two Gloo CPU processes, up from 7.
      - **Set-up.** A file store, or a TCP store on loopback; a 15 s group timeout and a 60 s limit on the processes. While TensorQuill runs, the worker makes every collective that could carry the ID raise.
      - **Cases:**
        - W&B on rank 0, with no launcher ID, with torchrun's and with SLURM's;
        - the Hugging Face callback;
        - a launcher ID alone, and a generated ID;
        - MLflow raising on rank 0;
        - a `run_id` on rank 0 only, through `init` and through the callback;
        - the same `run_id` on both ranks;
        - a `run_id` on rank 1 only;
        - rank 0 three seconds late, through either store;
        - two `init`s in one group;
        - rank 0 never calling `init`, through either store.
      - **On `main` (`3f93636`)**, run from a copy of its `sdk/` holding these tests:
        - With the guard, 15 of the 16 failed. The one that passed, the same `run_id` on both ranks, makes no collective on `main` either; it is kept as a check that such a run neither waits long nor warns.
        - With the collectives allowed, the five item 2 runs failed: a `run_id` on rank 0 only (twice), on rank 1 only, and rank 0 never calling `init` (twice). So did the Hugging Face case, whose source was `explicit`. The others passed: on `main` the broadcast does agree there. So the late rank 0 and the two `init`s fail on `main` only on the guard.
    - **`sdk/tests/test_tracker.py`:** four new tests, and two reworked to run on a real `HashStore` behind a faked group of two (the helper is `_ranks_sharing_a_store`). They cover rank 0's ID and source reaching rank 1, an explicit ID on some ranks only, the bound running out, a store that fails (MPI's placeholder, or no store at all), and a tracker that raises on rank 0. On `main` the five that exchange failed on the collective.
    - **`sdk/tests/test_huggingface.py`:** one test replaced, checking that the callback's run takes its source from the tracker. On `main` the source read `explicit`.
    - **`sdk/tests/test_lightning.py`:** one new test, a `run_id` on rank 0 only, under `ddp_spawn` with a 20 s timeout. It failed on `main`.
  - *Checked* (macOS, Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.15.0, Transformers 5.23.0, Accelerate 1.16.0, Lightning 2.6.6), on the final code, while six other sessions ran their suites on the machine. The four suites ran one after another, twice: first with one long `wait`, then on the `check` polling. Each round gave the same counts, and nothing failed, so no test needed a rerun.
    - With this worktree's server binary: 1,812 passed (`main` 1,799).
    - Without the binary: 1,193 passed and 619 skipped (1,180 and 619).
    - Without `pyarrow` or the binary: 1,069 passed and 80 skipped (1,056 and 80).
    - `experiments/` (`moe-bakeoff/test_fixtures.py`, `gate1/test_gate1.py`): 92 passed (92).
    - Each SDK count is the baseline plus the 13 new tests.
  - *Not run.* NCCL or a GPU. Linux. PyTorch 2.6. A real W&B or MLflow run. torchrun's agent store: the TCP case uses a TCPStore that rank 0 hosts. MPI.
  - *Open, for the author.*
    - **The bound, 300 s, is this lane's choice.** It is above W&B's 90 s init timeout and below NCCL's default group timeout of 10 min. It is a module constant, `tensorquill.tracker.RUN_ID_WAIT_SECONDS`. A rank 0 later than that splits the run, with a warning on each other rank.
    - **The cost of item 2's rule, nearest the rule that nothing may pause training.**
      - A rank other than 0 now waits at `init` until rank 0 reaches `init`, even when it was given a `run_id`; before, an explicit `run_id` returned at once.
      - When rank 0 is late, for example in `wandb.init`, which the Trainer runs in the same event, the wait comes where the first collective would have waited anyway.
      - When rank 0 never calls `init`, each other rank waits up to 300 s and then writes under an ID of its own. Before, it waited in the broadcast for the group's timeout and raised, or returned at once if it had a `run_id`.
      - Options: keep this; or let a rank that has its own `run_id` skip the wait and keep its ID. The second avoids the pause but splits the run whenever ranks are given different IDs, and gives up the warning.
    - **Lightning: dropped or unconditional.** The callback's `strategy.broadcast` was GPU-safe, since Lightning sets the device first, but it ran only on ranks with no `run_id`.
      - **Dropped (done here):** bounded, never raises, no collective. But Lightning's XLA strategy, which starts no `torch.distributed` group, no longer shares the logger's run ID.
      - **Unconditional broadcast instead:** keeps XLA sharing, but stays a collective that can raise into `on_fit_start`, or hang to the group's timeout.
    - **Lightning with `MLFlowLogger`.** The experiment and run names are not read: the logger creates its run through `MlflowClient`, so `tracker_labels` sees no active run, and such a run is `uncategorized` unless `project=` is passed. Reading the names would mean using `MLFlowLogger._experiment_name` and `_run_name`, which are private attributes.
    - **MPI.** Its default group's store is a placeholder, so every rank writes under its own ID, with a warning.
    - **Other docs still say "broadcast"; none is this lane's.** `sdk/README.md` rows 53 and 55 (the adapters), and `sdk/RUN_ON_YOUR_JOB.md` lines 72 (a generated ID "broadcast from rank 0"), 110 (Lightning) and 147 (Hugging Face).
    - **The single-process path** still lets a tracker's exception out of `init` (R3's open point). Unchanged.

- [x] **F1, the capacity case: a Megatron-style router's dropped assignments are counted as routed (found by the author on `main` at `e693e2f`, Nov 19, 2026; reproduced here the same day; fixed by lane U1 as the author decided, option (a), and merged, see "Wave U merged").** The review's F1 ("Router counts can be the wrong routing") in a form the README's caveat does not cover. The README's bullet "When router counts are the routing" says that with a capacity limit after the hooked module, the user should hook the module whose output holds the chosen experts. Megatron's `TopKRouter` drops at capacity inside its own forward, and its output, the one that holds the chosen experts, is the output TensorQuill already hooks and misreads.
  - *Mechanism.* `TopKRouter.forward` returns `(probs, routing_map)`: a float `[tokens, experts]` tensor and a bool `[tokens, experts]` mask (`megatron/core/transformer/moe/router.py` in the reference clone at `4b11b8168`). With `moe_expert_capacity_factor` set, `apply_router_token_dropping` (`moe_utils.py`) keeps at most `ceil(tokens × top_k / experts × capacity_factor)` tokens per expert and zeroes the probabilities it drops (`final_probs = routing_probs * final_map`). `_first_tensor` in `capture.py` skips bool tensors, so `_router_hook` finds no integer tensor and counts the top-`top_k` of `probs`. A token with an assignment dropped still adds `top_k` picks, the dropped ones landing on whichever zero entries `topk` returns.
  - *Run.* A CPU stand-in that follows Megatron's routing and `apply_router_token_dropping`, both drop policies, with and without padding to capacity, counted by `init(routers={"router": Router(4, 2)})` on `main` at `e693e2f` (`f1-megatron-capacity-drop.py` in the session notes):
    - two tokens, both choosing experts 0 and 1, capacity 1: the routing map routes [1, 1, 0, 0], and TensorQuill counted [2, 2, 0, 0];
    - four tokens that kept 1, 0, 1 and 2 of their two assignments: map [1, 1, 1, 1], counted [2, 4, 1, 1];
    - 64 tokens at capacity factor 1.0: map [24, 32, 32, 32], counted [26, 38, 32, 32] with the "probs" policy; map [32, 32, 28, 32], counted [34, 34, 28, 32] with "position";
    - with no capacity limit the counts match ([34, 31, 33, 30] both ways);
    - with `pad_to_capacity` the map is the capacity mask, padding slots included: [32, 32, 32, 32], while the assignments with a nonzero probability were [29, 32, 32, 31] and TensorQuill counted [29, 36, 32, 31].
  - *What it does.* Wrong per-expert loads with no error and no note: `moe_imbalance` and `starved_experts` judge the wrong counts, and the dropped tokens, a load problem in themselves, do not show. Nothing published claims Megatron MoE support (`RUN_ON_YOUR_JOB.md`'s Megatron section counts steps and "has not been run"), so no claim is wrong today; none may be made before this is fixed.
  - *Acceptance test (the author's).* With capacity dropping on, TensorQuill's per-expert counts equal the returned routing map's, tokens with fewer than `top_k` assignments included. With `pad_to_capacity` the map also holds padding, so there the counts should equal the map's entries with a nonzero probability: a reading of Megatron's code, to be confirmed by the author.
  - *Options, for the author.*
    - (a) Read the map: when a router's output holds a bool tensor of shape `[tokens, n_experts]`, count its columns, leaving out entries whose probability in the paired float tensor is zero (the padding). This is judged from types and shapes, so it is the same under `torch.compile`.
    - (b) Until (a) is built, refuse such a router with a "routing unavailable" reason in `moe_refused`, from the same signal.
    - An `indices=` function cannot express it: it must return one or `top_k` indices in `0..n_experts-1` for every token, and a -1 for a dropped assignment refuses the router at the flush.
  - *Decided by the author, Nov 19, 2026: option (a).* "read the bool routing map [..., n_experts], filtering probs != 0 when paired with a same-shape float tensor". This also settles the padded reading above: with `pad_to_capacity` the counts are the map's entries whose probability is not zero. Sent to lane U1; not built.
    - *What the sentence leaves open, and what lane U1 was told.*
      - The order of sources stays as it is, with the map added third: an `indices=` function, then an integer tensor, then the bool map, then the top-k of a float tensor. Only an output of a float tensor and a bool map is counted differently from today.
      - A bool tensor is taken as the map when its last dimension is `n_experts` and its other dimensions multiply to the tokens the hook was called on, so that a mask over the experts alone is not read as one. The token check is skipped where the count is symbolic, as the integer branch does.
      - The paired float tensor is the first of the same shape. An output of logits, probabilities and a map would pair the logits, which are never zero, and count the padding: lane U1 states the pairing rule and this limit.
    - *One limit of the filter.* A real assignment whose weight is exactly zero, such as one that underflowed in half precision, is left out with the padding. Such an assignment adds nothing to the layer's output, but its token was still sent to the expert.
    - *Still open from the audit's C06.* A router's default integer output with indices out of range, `[-1, 8]` for 4 experts, is recorded as `[0, 0, 0, 0]` with no refusal (run on `main`); only an `indices=` function's unmatched indices are counted. Not part of this decision.
  - *Priority.* After what can still raise into training (T2's item 3, `init` traced by Dynamo, SDK-22's gather), and before any claim of Megatron MoE support. The open bias caveat is the related limitation for other routers: `RUN_ON_YOUR_JOB.md` still says nothing of a bias or a capacity limit after the router (S7's *Not done*).
  - *Not run.* Megatron itself (not installed; the stand-in follows the reference clone), a GPU, and the dense top-k indices Megatron returns instead of the map for Flex dispatchers without a capacity factor and for virtual-expert balancing.

The writer's `join` in `close()` is bounded.

The number of queue entries per ring (`capture.py:1079`) is kept. Sent to lane U1 with C03. W&B's core counts its buffers in items, 32 work items a channel (`core/internal/stream/stream.go:30`): the unit is the record, the size is not comparable. The only calls that pass `nowait=True` are the three that publish console output (`wandb_run.py:1487`, `:1502`, `:1954`), so a logged step takes the waiting path. Not read: whether a stuck disk in W&B's service process backs up as far as that socket.
