- [x] **Lane U3: the probe and the loader wrapper leave the user's training as they found it (Nov 19, 2026, lane U3, branch `claude/lane-u3-probe-data-f892b2`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Wave U merged").** C02, C17 and C18's halves in `probe.py` and `data.py`, from wave U. Each was reproduced on `main` at `1767a4a` before any change. The code is in `sdk/src/tensorquill/probe.py` and `data.py`. `datawait.py` needed no change. The tests are in the new file `sdk/tests/test_lane_u3_probe_data.py`, which does not need the server binary. No record field, detector, golden file, file under `server/`, `capture.py` or protocol number changed. Hand-over note: `lane-u3.md` in the session notes.
  - *Reproduced on `1767a4a`.* The new file has 37 tests. Run against `1767a4a`'s `sdk/src`, 21 fail. pytest's `pythonpath = ["src"]` puts the worktree's own code first, so runs against older code pass `-o pythonpath=`.
    - C02: another thread's 5-D SDPA call raised `ValueError: too many values to unpack` while a probe was open. Zero keys raised in `amax`. Two probes that closed in the order they opened left the first probe's wrapper in place for good. They also left the whole process on the compiler's `force_eager` stance, which `_eager_stance` sets for the process: a new finding, and the reason `test_compile.py` failed when run after that test. Another thread's forward of the same model filled the probe's histograms. Deterministic mode was off inside the probe.
    - C17: after `watch_loader`, `type(loader.batch_sampler)` was `tensorquill.data.BatchSampler`, and the loader and the sampler did not pickle. A full pass of the unwrapped loader left 20 index batches in the wrapper's queue. A second wrap made the first wrapper note `None` for every batch. Two interleaved passes of one wrapper with two workers noted each other's indices. A pass that drew every index batch ahead was held whole, with no bound. Iterating a wrapper after the session closed raised a `RuntimeWarning` under `-W error`, as did an `ids=` callable that failed.
    - C18: a Llava config with text `flash_attention_2` and vision `sdpa` ended with every part on `flash_attention_2`. A probe function that drew from `random` changed the next draw at seed 123 from 0.0523636 to 0.0871867, the audit's numbers.
  - *C02, fixed.* What a probe changes for the whole process is now counted across open probes (`_open_probe`): the first probe to open sets the eager stance and installs the SDPA wrapper, and the last one to close undoes both.
    - The wrapper is made afresh at each first open, around the function then in place. It reads a call only while it is the installed wrapper, and only on a thread with a probe open, the newest one there. A call from any other thread, or while compiling, goes to the function it wraps with its own arguments.
    - It is taken out only if it is still the attribute's value. A wrapper another tool put over it stays, and from then on the old wrapper only passes calls through.
    - This branch's first commit (`f3b2ed6`) had a single wrapper. If another tool wrapped it during a probe, the next probe took that tool's wrapper as the original, and every SDPA call recursed. The second commit (`276be95`) fixes it with the fresh wrapper; `test_a_wrapper_put_over_the_probes_during_a_probe_does_not_make_a_later_probe_recurse` fails on `f3b2ed6` with `RecursionError`. The probe's statistics, and the binding of the call's arguments, run inside a `try`. A failure is that entry's `{"error": ...}`, after the original has returned, and the probe's other entries, histograms and head numbers stay. Both detectors read such an entry as no value for that probe (`_attention_values`; `AttentionStats` in `detect.rs`), as they already read an unreadable one. The block and head hooks also read only the probe's own thread, checked inside `if state.active`, so compiled code still sees no-ops.
    - Tests: `test_another_threads_attention_call_is_untouched_while_a_probe_is_open`, `test_another_threads_forward_does_not_reach_the_probes_block_hooks`, `test_statistics_that_fail_are_that_entrys_error_and_the_rest_of_the_record_stays` (5-D and no keys), `test_overlapping_probes_leave_the_original_function` (both closing orders, and the stance back to `default`), `test_a_wrapper_put_over_the_probes_during_a_probe_does_not_make_a_later_probe_recurse`.
  - *C02's deterministic setting, changed.* `run_probe` no longer calls `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(False)`. With the mode off, nothing changes: `_histogram` calls `histc` as before. With it on, `_histogram` counts with `_histc_counts` instead.
    - It uses histc's own arithmetic, float32 `int((v - lo) * bins / (hi - lo))` in that order, then `bincount` without weights. The arithmetic was read at `v2.6.0` in `HistogramKernel.cpp` (LINEAR_INTERPOLATION) and in `SummaryOps.cu` (`getBin`). The work is chunked, at most `CHUNK_ELEMENTS` int64 indices at once.
    - Deterministic on CUDA, from PyTorch's source at `v2.6.0` and at the clone's head:
      - `_bincount_cuda` calls `alertNotDeterministic` only when weights are given; without them it counts with integer atomics.
      - `index_add_` on CUDA takes `index_put_(accumulate=True)` when the mode is on (`Indexing.cu`), which the documentation lists as deterministic.
      - `histc` was the probe's only op on the list that raises. `hi - lo` stays a device tensor, because CUDA divides by a CPU scalar as a product with its reciprocal (`div_true_kernel_cuda`).
    - Test: `test_with_deterministic_algorithms_on_the_histogram_is_histcs`, 12 cases, all equal to `histc`. It is an equality check, and it passes on `1767a4a` too. The cases are values on and next to the bin edges, the top value repeated, one value (the widened bounds), a tiny span, 1e30, bf16, non-finite values and several chunks. `test_the_probe_leaves_the_deterministic_setting_alone` failed before.
    - Two differences from histc, both beyond what histc counts correctly. Counts are int64, where histc adds in float32 and stops counting a bin at 2^24. Where `(v - lo) * bins` overflows float32 (a span above about 5e36), histc's bin index is undefined; this path puts the value in the last bin. That range is C22's and was not changed with the mode off.
  - *C17, fixed.* The batch sampler's class is swapped to a recording subclass only while one of the wrapper's passes is opened: from `iter()` up to its first batch. Accelerate's `DataLoaderShard.__iter__` is a generator that draws from the sampler only at the first `next()`, read in the clone. The recording reaches only the pass being opened on that thread, through a thread-local claim, and each pass has its own queue.
    - At every other moment the sampler is its own class. It pickles and saves with `torch.save`. A pass over the unwrapped loader records nothing. Two wrappers of one loader, or two passes of one wrapper, do not share a queue.
    - During the window the subclass pickles and copies as the sampler's own class. It is rebuilt through `copyreg._reconstructor`, because pickle refuses `__newobj__` with another class.
    - A pass that holds more than `_FIFO_LIMIT` (4,096) index batches stops recording and notes its remaining batches as unknown. A batch is never paired with another batch's indices.
    - Once the session is closed the wrapper yields the loader's batches and notes nothing. So `Session.close` needs no new call, and none was made in `capture.py`.
    - Tests that fail on `1767a4a`:
      - `test_a_watched_loader_pickles_before_during_and_after_and_keeps_its_sampler`
      - `test_iterating_the_unwrapped_loader_keeps_nothing_and_does_not_shift_the_wrapper`
      - `test_two_iterators_of_one_watched_loader_each_note_their_own_batches`, with 2 workers
      - `test_a_second_wrap_does_not_take_the_first_wrappers_batches`
      - `test_spawn_workers_with_a_watched_loader`, persistent spawn workers, on the class check
      - `test_indices_drawn_far_ahead_are_noted_as_unknown_not_shifted`: `1767a4a` held the whole pass
      - `test_a_watched_loader_after_the_session_closed_passes_batches_through`
    - `test_while_a_pass_is_being_opened_the_sampler_pickles_and_copies_as_its_own_class` tests the new window and fails on `f3b2ed6`.
    - Coverage only, passing on `1767a4a` too: two iterators with no workers, an early exit (0 and 2 workers), and `test_a_loader_whose_iter_is_a_generator_is_noted`.
  - *C18, fixed (`probe.py`, `data.py`).*
    - Configs: `_configs` lists the model's config and its nested `sub_configs`, each with its setting, a config before its sub-configs. Every config set to FlashAttention is switched to `sdpa`, the others are left, and afterwards each has its own value back. Both writes go through each config's own setter, in that order, writing only where the value differs: transformers' setter writes a config's value into every sub-config below it.
      - Also new: a model whose top config is `sdpa` and whose text config is `flash_attention_2` is now switched for the probe. Before, it was not switched, and its text model's attention was not read.
      - Test: `test_a_composite_configs_parts_are_as_they_were_after_a_probe` (three Llava settings). A tiny transformers Llama set to `flash_attention_2` was also checked by hand: switched, both attention calls read, `flash_attention_2` after.
    - RNG: `_fork_host_rng` saves and restores Python's `random` and, when NumPy is already imported, NumPy's global state, next to `torch.random.fork_rng`. Like `fork_rng`, it also rolls back a draw another thread makes during the probe. Test: `test_a_probe_leaves_pythons_and_numpys_random_state_as_it_found_them`.
    - The loader's warning: `_report` still gives the `RuntimeWarning`, and the existing `pytest.warns` test is unchanged. Where warnings are errors, it catches the exception and writes the message to stderr instead. It does not use `warnings.catch_warnings`, which changes process-wide state. Test: `test_a_failure_to_note_a_batch_is_not_raised_under_warnings_as_errors`.
  - *Run* (macOS, CPU, PyTorch 2.15, transformers 5.23, this worktree on `PYTHONPATH`).
    - The new file: 37 passed. `test_probe.py`, `test_data_progress.py`, `test_data_e2e.py` and `test_datawait.py` pass unchanged. `test_compile.py`: 4 passed, alone and after the new file, so no graph break or recompile.

  - *Open, for the author.*
    - With deterministic mode on, the probe's forward now runs under the user's setting. A probe function that calls an op that raises in that mode, for example `generate` with top-p sampling (`cumsum` on CUDA), now fails as a probe error where it ran before. The same holds for `warn_only=True` and its warnings. It is recorded as the probe's `error`; U1 makes its warning safe.
    - The probe still replaces `F.scaled_dot_product_attention` for the process while it runs, though now no other thread is affected. A thread-local `TorchFunctionMode` would avoid replacing the attribute at all. It was not built.

    - While any probe is open, the whole process is on `force_eager`, so another thread's compiled code runs eagerly for that time. This was so before; the stance is the process's in PyTorch.
    - A loader wrapper that iterates the batch sampler again after its first batch, for example one that cycles an exhausted loader, now notes those batches as unknown. `1767a4a` recorded them.

- [x] **Lane U4: captured steps lost between the local file and the server (Nov 19, 2026, lane U4, branch `claude/lane-u4-flight-sink-82ad94`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Wave U merged").** Audit findings C07 (the writer's half), C08, C09, SDK-21, SDK-26's offset scratch file, SDK-14 and C24's end-marker half. Code in `sink.py` and `flight.py` only; tests in `sdk/tests/test_lane_u4_flight_sink.py`, and two changed expectations in `test_flight.py`. No record field, detector, golden file, file under `server/` or protocol message changed.
  - *Read first.* wandb keeps a `random.Random()` of its own for its run IDs (`sdk/lib/runid.py`) and holds its retry sleep as capped state (`sdk/lib/retry.py`). The server answers a batch with `{"end_byte_offset": N}` and nothing else, and keeps the highest offset per (run, rank) across streams (`ingest.rs:304`), so an ack can name an offset the current stream never sent.
  - *Reproduced on `1767a4a` before any change* (`lane-u4-repro.py` and the audit's `c07.py` in the notes folder). C07: after a torn last line, a new `JsonlSink` joined its first step to the fragment, and neither could be read. C08: `send_file` to a second, empty store returned true and that store got nothing. A replacement file of the same size, sent again to the first store, returned true and none of its steps were sent. A missing file returned true. C09: `OverflowError` on attempt 1,025, and the outage used up draws of the global `random`. SDK-21: a line with `"attempt": null` held the sender at offset 0 for good. Offset scratch file: a second writer of the same offset file, between the first's write and rename, made the first's rename fail.
  - *C07, fixed.* A `JsonlSink` that opens a file whose last byte is not a newline writes one newline before anything else.
    - The fragment stays where it was, as a line of its own that is not a whole JSON object: incomplete history, told apart because it does not parse. A fragment that is a whole object (the process died just before its newline) becomes a whole line again.
    - Nothing is truncated or moved, so the file is only ever appended to, the sender's saved offsets stay valid, and a clean or empty file gets no byte, so its end marker still matches.
    - The sender's torn-line rule is unchanged: it waits at the fragment until a newline comes, and the next writer now supplies it.
    - If another process still has the file open and is in the middle of that line, the newline cuts its line in two and that step is lost. Two writers appending to one file interleave lines anyway, and the sink takes no lock.
    - Tests: `test_c07_sink_ends_a_torn_last_line_so_the_first_new_step_is_read`, `test_c07_a_fragment_that_is_a_whole_object_becomes_a_whole_line`, `test_c07_sender_waits_at_a_torn_tail_and_reads_the_step_the_next_writer_appends`, `test_c07_real_server_stores_the_first_step_after_a_torn_tail` (real server). `test_c07_a_clean_or_empty_file_is_not_touched_and_its_end_marker_still_matches` guards the clean case and passes on `1767a4a` too. `test_flight.py`'s `test_line_torn_by_a_crash_is_skipped_and_other_runs_lines_are_not_sent` now writes the new steps through `JsonlSink` and requires step 1: `[0, 1, 2]`, two lines skipped.
    - The reader's half is lane U7's: `load_records` must skip a line that is not a whole JSON object anywhere in the file, not only at its end, and keep reading. It raises on such a line today, before and after this fix.
  - *C08, fixed as far as the client can know.* The offset file is now JSON. It holds the offset, the server's address, the run, the rank, and SHA-256 digests of the file's first 64 KiB (at most the offset) and of the 4 KiB before the offset. `read_offset` still returns the number and still reads the old plain-number file.
    - A sender resumes at the offset only when all of these match. Anything else sends the file again from byte 0, which the server's step filter makes safe, and `sender.replayed` says why: another server, run or rank; an offset file from before this change; a file shorter than the offset; or different bytes (a file deleted and written again, or emptied and rewritten in place).
    - The digests come from the bytes actually sent.
    - The server can ack an offset this stream never sent. `detach` (`ingest.rs:587`) clears only the ack channel, so the highest offset of a stream that ended before it was stored is acked on the next stream. Such an ack is not taken when this stream started again at byte 0, because it names the old file's bytes, nor when the file does not reach it. Otherwise its digests are read from the file. The worst case is an honest false from `close()`, and later acks name this stream's own batches. Tests: `test_c08_ack_of_an_offset_not_sent_after_a_replay_is_not_taken`, `test_c08_ack_beyond_the_end_of_the_file_is_not_taken`. Both fail on this lane's first commit, `beea4c9`, which took such an ack.
    - Behaviour that changed for callers: `unacked_bytes()` returns None for a file that cannot be read. An SDK from before this change reads the new JSON offset file as 0 and sends the file again, which is safe. A sender started before its file exists now reports an error and retries, where it used to wait quietly.
    - The inode is not used: a copy of the same bytes rightly resumes, and a new file under an old inode is told apart by its bytes.
    - A missing or unreadable file is an error. `close()` and `send_file` return false at once and print that the file cannot be read, and `unacked_bytes()` returns None.
    - Tests: `test_c08_offset_saved_for_one_server_is_not_used_for_another`, `test_c08_replaced_file_of_equal_size_is_sent_from_its_start` (deleted and written again, and rewritten in place), `test_c08_offset_without_a_binding_is_sent_again_from_the_start`, `test_c08_missing_file_is_an_error_and_not_nothing_to_send`, `test_c08_real_servers_with_two_stores_each_get_the_whole_file` (real server). `test_c08_unchanged_file_resumes_and_sends_nothing_twice` is a guard: it counts 100 rows received for 100 steps and fails on `1767a4a` only for the new `replayed` attribute.
    - `test_flight.py`'s `test_tls_ca_is_loaded_at_init_and_ignored_for_plaintext` asserted that a sender of a file never written closes with true; it now asserts false.
  - *C09, fixed.* The delay's upper bound is kept as state, doubled after each failure and held at the cap, so the loop never computes `2**attempt`. The jitter comes from each sender's own `random.Random()`. Tests: `test_c09_thousands_of_failures_keep_retrying_at_the_cap` (5,000 failures on a fake clock, the real loop), `test_c09_an_outage_leaves_the_global_random_untouched` (a seeded global generator gives the same draws after 200 failures), and `test_c09_backoff_ceiling_doubles_and_holds_at_the_cap`.
  - *SDK-21, fixed for the whole class.* Each record is converted on its own, and if Arrow refuses the batch, its rows are tried one by one. A record that cannot be stored is skipped and counted in `skipped_lines`, like a line that is not JSON, and the sender moves on. That covers a null or a string in a key column, a step above int64, a `moe_top_k` above int32, and a `NaN` inside a nested object. Tests: `test_sdk21_unstorable_lines_are_skipped_and_counted_and_the_sender_moves_on`, `test_sdk21_rows_to_send_keeps_the_good_rows_of_a_batch_arrow_refuses`.
  - *Offset scratch file, fixed.* The scratch file is `.<name>.<pid>.<thread>.tmp` and is removed if the write fails. Test: `test_offset_writers_in_one_directory_do_not_share_a_scratch_file`.
  - *Heartbeat client (SDK-14), fixed.* A client dropped after a failed beat is closed then, and the last one when the thread ends. Test: `test_heartbeat_closes_each_client_it_drops_after_a_failed_beat`. No leak or fork hang was shown to come from the old code; this closes the gap the audit named.
  - *End marker without its records file (C24's first half), fixed.* `read_end_marker` returns `unreadable`, with the reason, when the marker is there and the records file cannot be read. `runs.py` and `evidence.py` treat every state but `closed` alike. Test: `test_end_marker_without_its_records_file_is_not_closed`.
  - *Run.* The new file holds 22 tests; the two ack tests were added after the full run below and fail on `beea4c9`. The audit's `c07.py` and `lane-u4-repro.py` on the fixed code: steps `[1, 3]` with one unreadable line; the second store gets all five steps; the replaced file's steps are sent; a missing file returns false; the null-attempt line is skipped and close returns true; 3,001 failures without an error and the global `random` untouched; two offset writers both succeed. After the ack change, the new tests, `test_flight.py`, `test_flight_e2e.py`, `test_object_store_e2e.py`, `test_two_ranks_e2e.py`, `test_stall_evidence.py`, `test_watch_all_ranks.py`, `test_compile.py`, `test_server_reports.py` and `test_data_server.py` pass with the server binary set: 144 passed. Of the other 20 new tests, every one except the clean-file guard fails on `1767a4a`'s code, each on the defect itself, apart from the C08 resume guard and the `_backoff` and `rows_to_send` unit tests, which fail for want of the new attribute and helpers (`rows_to_send` was given a stand-in that converts as `1767a4a` did, and Arrow raised on it). The two changed `test_flight.py` tests fail there too. The new tests, `test_flight.py`, `test_flight_e2e.py`, `test_object_store_e2e.py`, `test_two_ranks_e2e.py`, `test_stall_evidence.py` and `test_watch_all_ranks.py` pass with the server binary set: 119 passed. Full `sdk/tests` with the worktree on `PYTHONPATH` and the server binary set: 1,857 passed and 1 failed, in 22 minutes, while six other lanes ran tests on the same Mac. The failure was `test_flight_e2e.py::test_evaluation_is_paused_and_measured_but_a_quiet_training_phase_is_a_suspected_hang`. The server measured the evaluation pause at over 1 s, so it recommended 3 s of silence where the test expected 2. Run alone afterwards, it passed three times out of three. It had also passed in the 119-test run. It times a pause from phase changes, which this lane does not touch. `test_compile.py` passed within the full run.
  - *Not run.* Linux, a GPU, PyTorch 2.6, a real network filesystem, two processes writing one file at once. CI does not run the two real-server tests until `tests/test_lane_u4_flight_sink.py` is added to the server job's file list in `ci.yml` (lane U8's file).
  - *Wording that no longer matches (listed, not edited).* `sdk/README.md` lines 58 and 327 describe the offset file as the offset alone. This plan's specification, line 142, names `.offset.tmp` as the scratch file, and line 143, "Torn-line recovery", does not say that the next writer ends the torn line.
  - *Open, for the author.*
    - **Which store it is.** The server sends nothing that names its store. So a store wiped behind the same address still reads as the one the offset was saved for, and one store reached by two addresses (`localhost` and `127.0.0.1`) is sent the whole file again. The design, for lane U6 or later: the server writes a store ID once at its store root and returns it with each ack, as `{"end_byte_offset": N, "store_id": "..."}`; the sender binds the offset to that instead of the address. Not built, since it changes the server.
    - **A sink write that fails part way through a line.** If a disk fills mid-run, the next write in the same process joins its step onto the fragment; only a new `JsonlSink` repairs it. This waits on C03's decision about what a failing sink does.
    - **One field Arrow refuses drops the whole step.** For example, a `moe_top_k` above int32. Moving that field to `extra_json` would keep the step.

- [x] **Lane U7: the evidence readers' missing, wrong and unreadable reports (Nov 19, 2026, lane U7, branch `claude/lane-u7-evidence-e59dd0`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Wave U merged").** Seven items of Wave U, in `evidence.py`, `evidence_system.py`, `mcp_server.py` and `trigger.py`, each reproduced on `main` at `1767a4a` before it was changed. New tests are in `sdk/tests/test_lane_u7_evidence.py`. Of its 24 tests, 23 fail on `main` and pass here; the 24th guards that rows with no run ID still read as before. Two of them need the server binary (`TENSORQUILL_SERVER_BIN`) and skip without it: they run in CI only once the server job lists this file (U8's `ci.yml`). Outside the lane's files, expectations changed in five test files, listed under *Tests outside this lane's file*. No file under `server/`, golden file, record field written by the capture, Parquet schema or protocol number changed. Hand-over note: `lane-u7.md` in the session notes.
  - *Outcomes.* All seven reproduced, and all seven fixed.
  - *1. A duplicate-batch incident beside a stuck collective.* On `main`, `build_evidence` raised `KeyError: 'assessment'` and wrote nothing. The inputs were a complete two-rank dump with a collective hang, a status that reports `duplicate_batches`, and steps recorded before the dump. Now only a `job_stalled` or `rank_silent` reading `suspected_hang` is corroborated. The fix is in `_check_other_sources`, which the store path also uses. Test: `test_a_duplicate_batch_finding_beside_a_stuck_collective_leaves_both_in_the_report`. It skips before PyTorch 2.10, the Flight Recorder analyzer's own minimum.
  - *2. MCP answered rank 1 with rank 0's values.* In a shared capture, rank 0 gives expert 1 all of its 100 assignments and rank 1 gives it none. On `main`, `get_series` for rank 1 against rank 0's evidence file answered 1.0, labelled rank 1. Against the compact file, it gave rank 1's values with rank 0's overloaded and dead flags.
    - Every evidence file now has a top-level `rank`, the rank it judged, written by `build_evidence` and by the store path. The compact file keeps it.
    - MCP reads `rank`, then `detection.rank`.
    - A file's samples, or its compact flags, are used only when its rank and run match the records read. Any other rank is judged from its own capture. For a store it is read from its records, with no flags; that store path has no test. The answer then carries `evidence_file`, a sentence saying why.
    - A file that names no rank (written before the key) is not trusted.
    - With no rank asked, the file's own rank is read, where `main` read the capture's lowest.
    - Tests: `test_every_evidence_file_says_which_rank_it_judged`, `test_rank_1_is_never_answered_with_rank_0s_saved_samples`, `test_a_compact_files_flags_are_used_only_for_the_rank_it_judged`, `test_an_evidence_file_that_does_not_say_its_rank_is_not_taken_on_trust`.
  - *3. The Python reader blamed the wrong run.* On `main`, rank-0 rows of runs X and Y gave evidence for X that showed Y's nonfinite gradient. A later attempt of Y cut X's rows, because attempts were keyed by rank and attempt only.
    - `build_evidence` now refuses rows of more than one run with `ValueError`, before attempts are resolved or any rule runs, and before the Rust call. Both detector paths therefore refuse. The Rust binary refuses the same rows itself (measured: "send one rank of one run"). `detect.rs` is unchanged.
    - `resolve_attempts` keys attempts by run as well.
    - **Rows with no run ID**, from files written before `run_id` existed, read as the Rust path reads them: as the run "". A file of only such rows is one stream, as before. Such a row beside a named run's rows is refused, as Rust refuses it ("rows of  rank 0 beside rows of X rank 0").
    - The command line's one read gives the refusal as a usage error with either detectors, as the Rust path already did, not a traceback.
    - Tests: `test_records_of_two_runs_are_refused_before_any_rule_runs` (two cases), `test_the_command_line_refuses_a_file_of_two_runs_with_a_usage_error`, `test_the_rust_detectors_refuse_the_same_rows`, `test_a_later_attempt_of_another_run_does_not_roll_this_one_back`, `test_rows_with_no_run_id_still_make_one_stream`.
    - **What the refusal means for a user.** A job resumed without a shared run ID (no `run_id=`, no tracker, no launcher's job ID) is a second run. Its records are no longer judged with the first run's, so `resume_incomplete`, `resume_data_mismatch` and `resume_lr_change` do not reach across the two.
    - **When the two runs mix.** By default each run writes `tensorquill-out/<run_id>.rank<rank>.jsonl`, so the second run already goes to a file of its own and is judged alone there, as on `main`. The two only mix in a file named by the user (`sink=` a path both runs append to), in a memory sink, or when files are joined by hand. That is where the refusal is now met.
  - *4. Evidence that strict JSON readers rejected.* On `main`, a replica fingerprint's `"nan"` or `"inf"` from the capture was written as a bare `NaN` or `Infinity`.
    - Now `json_safe` writes a nonfinite number as `"nan"`, `"inf"` or `"-inf"`, as the capture does, with `allow_nan=False`. The evidence file, the compact file (through `write_evidence`), the trigger's payload and its state file all do this. The evidence in memory keeps its floats, so the golden and parity tests do not move.
    - The `non_finite: true` finding is kept.
    - A written file goes through a strict parser, and the parsed file then goes through the summary, MCP's `list_incidents` and `get_incident`, and a trigger.
    - Also checked by a script, not a test: all 65 record sets of `test_server_detect.CASES` were written, strictly parsed and read back through the summary, every incident's `get_incident`, every router's `get_series` and a trigger, with no error. `everything` is the one golden case with nonfinite values. `compare` given two such evidence files as paths ran too.
    - Tests: `test_a_nonfinite_reading_is_written_as_the_capture_writes_it_and_read_back_whole`, `test_the_compact_file_and_the_triggers_payload_are_strict_too`.
    - **The webhook body and the command's standard input used to carry `null` for a nonfinite number.** They now carry the string. The one assertion of `None` in `test_trigger.py` now expects `"nan"`. `PAYLOAD_VERSION` stays 1.
    - **Rust difference.** The binary's report writes `null` for the same reading (`norm_spread: null` beside `non_finite: true`), which loses NaN versus infinity.
  - *5. A second run's first incident was held back.* On `main`, a second run whose evidence went to the same path had its rank-0 `non_finite` at step 0 held back by the first run's firing.
    - Each saved firing now records `run_id`. A firing is held back by run, rank, kind and step.
    - Stall episodes are kept per run, under `episodes` in the state file. `episode`, the current run's, is still written for an older trigger.
    - A state file in the old format is read with each firing's run taken from its id (`<run_id>/<ranks>/<kind>/<step>`, with `/<n>` for a later episode). A firing whose id cannot be read holds back as it did.
    - **A firing of another run sends nothing.** On `main`, run A's failed firing was tried again with run B's incident, under A's id.
    - Tests: `test_a_fresh_run_at_the_same_path_fires_and_a_restarted_watcher_does_not`, `test_a_state_file_in_the_old_format_is_still_read_by_run`, `test_stall_episodes_are_kept_apart_by_run`, `test_an_earlier_runs_failed_firing_is_never_sent_with_this_runs_incident`. They run only a shell `printf` into a file in the test's folder, and contact no webhook.
  - *6. A torn line in the middle of a capture.* On `main`, `build_evidence` and the command line's one read raised `JSONDecodeError` and gave no evidence. The watcher skipped the line without a word.
    - `read_jsonl` returns the records and the number of lines left out. The steps after such a line are read.
    - `build_evidence`, the command line and the watcher's tail use it. The evidence carries `lines_left_out`, a count, as MCP's source already does, and the summary says so in one line. MCP already read whole lines.
    - `load_records` itself still raises: `compare.py` and MCP catch that to set their own flags.
    - Tests: `test_a_torn_line_in_the_middle_is_skipped_counted_and_said`, `test_a_last_line_being_written_is_counted_and_a_whole_one_without_newline_is_read`.
  - *7. Three small ones.*
    - **The shared temporary file.** Each writer now uses a temporary file of its own in the destination's folder, then renames it (`replace_atomically`, used by `write_evidence` and the trigger's state file). On `main`, the second rename raised `FileNotFoundError`. The state file still assumes one writer: the later save wins. Tests: `test_two_writers_of_one_evidence_file_do_not_take_each_others_temporary_file`, `test_two_triggers_saving_one_state_file_do_not_fail`.
    - **The median.** The weight-trend median is now `statistics.median`: ratios 1 and 5 give 3, where `main` gave 5. `weight_trends` is made in Python on both detector paths and is not a frozen golden key. Test: `test_the_weight_trend_median_of_two_parameters_is_their_mean`.
    - **The repeated step.** Six growing allocations at one repeated step raised `ZeroDivisionError` on `main`. Now `memory_growth` is reported with no rate (`bytes_per_step` and `steps_to_total` null), the summary says every reading was at that step, and `repeated_steps` is reported. Tests: `test_growing_memory_at_one_repeated_step_reports_the_repeat_and_no_rate`, `test_the_rust_detectors_on_the_same_rows_and_the_summary_of_their_answer`.
    - **Rust difference.** Rust reports both too, with a null rate but `steps_to_total: 0.0`. The Python summary leaves the forecast out when there is no rate. The change for U6: require `step > first_step` in `server/src/detect/system.rs` (the rate, near line 169) and write both as null.
  - *Tests outside this lane's file.* Each change is to an expectation that the brief's new behaviour makes wrong. No other assertion moved.
    - **Two run IDs where the test meant one resume.** These now pass `run_id="resumed"` to both sessions, as a resume under torchrun, SLURM or a tracker keeps one ID:
      - `test_evidence.py::test_repeated_steps_after_resume_and_start_step`.
      - `test_data_adapters.py::test_lightning_records_its_checkpoints_and_the_resume_that_loads_one` and `::test_the_trainer_records_its_checkpoint_folders_and_a_complete_resume_finds_nothing`. Both are the `TensorQuillCallback(...)` calls; nothing else in that file changed. This was cleared with the session that started the wave, after checking that no other lane touches the file.
    - **`"nan"` in place of `null` in the trigger's payload.** `test_trigger.py`'s webhook test now accepts `"nan"`, or `null` when `test_watch_rust.py` runs it through the Rust detectors, whose report already has null there.
    - **Written files compared with evidence made in memory.** `test_reader_thresholds.py::test_each_of_the_six_flags_reaches_the_detectors_on_a_local_file` and `test_server_reports.py`'s `as_text` now pass both through `json_safe` before comparing them as text.
  - *Run.* `sdk/tests` with this worktree on `PYTHONPATH` and the server binary built from `main`'s server code set: 1862 passed, none failed or skipped, in 19 minutes, at the branch's last code commit (`3e9b94a`). The first full run, before the expectations listed above were changed, had 7 failures, all from this lane's changes and none from timing. `test_compile.py` is unchanged and passes: this lane touches no capture code. Run on macOS, on the CPU, with the main checkout's venv.
  - *Not run.* PyTorch 2.6, Linux, a GPU or NCCL. The new code is reader-side and uses nothing version-specific.
  - *Open, for the author.*
    - **The repeated-steps hint for a rerun without a run ID.** A user who reruns a script with no tracker, no job ID and no `run_id=`, into a custom-named file, gets a new generated ID. The file is now refused as two runs, where `main` reported `repeated_steps` with "pass start_step=". This follows the Rust refusal, as the brief asks. Whether such a rerun should instead read as a resume is the author's call.
    - A watcher with `--detectors python` on a file that two jobs append to now stops with the refusal, as the Rust watcher already did. It could instead keep one run and say so.
    - `load_records` still raises on a line that is not JSON: `compare.py` and MCP catch that to say a line was left out. Making it skip would need their flags taken from `read_jsonl`'s count instead (two places in `compare.py`).
    - Whether the payload change (null to `"nan"`) deserves `PAYLOAD_VERSION` 2.
    - The two Rust changes above, for U6 or a later wave.
    - `runs` lists a shared custom-named file under its first record's run only. `compare` would now refuse such a file if it ever reached two runs in one; not run.
  - *Wording that no longer matches this branch (listed, not edited).* Where the docs should tell a user to keep one run ID across a resume: `sdk/RUN_ON_YOUR_JOB.md` line 97 ("Resuming from a checkpoint: pass `start_step=`") and `sdk/README.md` lines 35 (`attempt`) and 649 (checkpoints). `sdk/README.md` line 616 says an incident is new when "its rank, kind and step have not fired before"; it is now within its run, and a new run whose evidence goes to the same path fires its own. New output that no document describes yet: `rank` and `lines_left_out` in the evidence file; `evidence_file` in `get_series`'s answer; `run_id` on each firing and `episodes` in the trigger's state file; `"nan"`, `"inf"` and `"-inf"` in place of `null` in the trigger's payload. Nothing found in `RUN_ON_YOUR_JOB.md` or `AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` (the latter already says a value that is not a number is the string `"nan"`, `"inf"` or `"-inf"`).

- [x] **Lane U6: the server called a run finished or healthy when it was not, RS01 to RS05 (Nov 19, 2026, lane U6, branch `claude/lane-u6-server-0779e1`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Wave U merged").** The audit's five P1 rows on the server, each reproduced first on this branch's base, `1767a4a`, by a test that fails there. Under `server/`, `src/watchdog.rs`, `src/ingest.rs`, `src/storage.rs`, one line of `src/main.rs` (the new `report_grace` takes its default), the tests in `src/detect.rs` and `tests/ingest_test.rs` changed; nothing outside `server/` but this entry. No field of `watchdog.json` or `incidents.json` was added, removed or renamed, and the summary keeps its five words, so the protocol number stays. Hand-over note: `lane-u6.md` in the session notes, with the output before and after.
  - *RS01: fixed.* After a clean end of its stream, a heartbeat naming a new attempt changed the rank's attempt and left it finished: the status read `summary="finished"`, `attempt=2`, `state="finished"`, `attempt_without_steps=true`, and a job started again that could not open its stream kept that verdict. Now that beat makes the rank a stream not open yet: `reconnecting` for the start window from that beat, then `lost`, critical the configured time after the window, and the run is no longer finished. The beat resets what belongs to one attempt, as it already did for a dropped stream, and the time the rank had been finished is not counted toward `abandon`. A beat of the same attempt after the close changes nothing; a stream the new attempt opens and closes cleanly finishes the rank, whether or not it stored anything. Tests: `a_finished_rank_whose_job_starts_again_and_only_beats_is_not_finished` (heartbeats only, past the start window and through a restore) and `a_new_attempt_that_opens_its_own_stream_and_closes_it_cleanly_is_finished`, in `watchdog.rs`. Left open: a new attempt's first beat while the earlier attempt's stream is still open, then that stream's clean end, still reads finished. The server cannot tell that close from the new attempt's own: a sender that waited longer than its hold opens its stream before its first beat, and a rule written for the first case made such a run that stored nothing and closed cleanly read `lost` for good. Telling them apart needs to know which attempt owns a stream, which is RS05's question.
  - *RS02: fixed.* Rank 0 of a world of two closed before the grace ran out, and the run read `finished`, so a restarted server did not take it up, while rank 1 had never connected. Now inside the grace no rank is called missing and no alarm is raised, as before, but a run is `finished` only when every expected rank has been seen and finished; until then it is `active`, and `degraded` with the rank missing after the grace. Test: `a_run_is_not_finished_while_an_expected_rank_has_not_been_seen`, with a restart inside the grace and one after it. The existing test `a_run_is_finished_only_when_every_rank_and_the_whole_world_finished` accepted `finished` at that point; its expectation is now `active` with no rank missing.
  - *RS03: fixed.* `rank_parts` took the first `attempt=` folder anywhere in a part's path, so a local store under `.../attempt=7/` gave every part attempt 7, and a resume at a lower step was read as rows written twice. The attempt is now the folder directly under the rank's, read relative to it with `Path::prefix_match`, as `_newest_part` in `evidence.py` already reads it; a part straight in the rank's folder is attempt 0, as before. Which files are listed is unchanged (RS09 is about that). Tests: `a_parts_attempt_is_the_partition_under_its_rank_not_one_in_the_store_root` in `storage.rs`, and `replay_reads_each_parts_attempt_from_under_its_rank_whatever_the_store_root_holds` in `detect.rs` (attempt 1 to step 99, attempt 2 resumed at 50: attempt 2, last step 59, 60 rows; on the base 7, 59 and 110).
  - *RS04: fixed.* The run state in `watchdog.json` judged a rank by any report its detectors had cached, of whatever rows. With attempt 1's report cached and attempt 2's report held back, the status read attempt 2 at step 79 and `healthy`. Now each cached report keeps the attempt and last step it read, and judges its rank only when it is of the attempt the status names for the rank, the status does not mark the rank `attempt_without_steps`, and it is not behind the newest part this server stored for the rank. "Match the status" is read that way for the step on purpose: the status's `last_step` counts rows still in the buffer, which wait up to the flush interval (10 s) before they are stored and judged, so comparing with it would make a run that streams normally read `unknown` most of the time. The newest part stored is what `read_server_report` in `evidence.py` compares with. A part is stored first and judged a moment later, so a report behind the store still judges its rank for `report_grace` from when it fell behind, 30 s by default, as Python's watchers wait `REPORT_GRACE_SECONDS`; without it, one `incidents.json` write per part on an object store would leave some rank of a large run behind at most status writes. A report of another attempt gets no grace. A report that does not judge its rank still lists its incidents, which were found in stored rows, so a known incident keeps the run `unhealthy`. A rebuild of the detectors that fails or panics now drops the rank's batch hashes with its report, so the status no longer lists the rank in `ranks_compared` from rows before the rebuild. Tests, in `ingest_test.rs`: `a_cached_report_of_other_rows_than_the_status_shows_does_not_judge_the_rank` (no grace: two flushes, the second report's write held back and the attempt changed; then a third with the same attempt held back; then rows only buffered, which keep the rank judged), `a_report_a_moment_behind_the_store_judges_its_rank_for_the_grace_and_one_of_another_attempt_never` and `a_failed_rebuild_drops_the_ranks_batch_hashes_from_the_comparison`; and `a_report_of_an_earlier_attempt_does_not_make_the_rank_judged` in `watchdog.rs`, which also holds that the incidents of a report that does not judge are listed. To hold the report back and make reads fail, the test store gained three knobs. `IngestConfig` gained `report_grace`; no command-line option was added.
  - *RS05: reproduced, not fixed; the ordering is the author's to choose.* With attempt 1 at step 100, attempt 2 resumed at step 30, then a new step 101 from attempt 1's process still alive, the status went back to attempt 1 at step 101, and forward to attempt 2 at its next batch. The guard against an earlier attempt holds only until the new attempt's first batch. Replay sorts parts by attempt number, so there it keeps attempt 2 (last step 40, the late 101 cut off). A replacement given a lower number by a host whose clock is behind, which the watchdog accepts on purpose, is the opposite case: with attempt 1000 to step 99 and 900 resumed at 50 to 120, replay reads attempt 1000 and leaves out all 71 rows of 900. With RS04's fix such a rank reads `unknown`, because the live attempt is 900 and the report's is 1000. RS01's open case above is the same question for streams. The repro is `lane-u6-rs05-repro.rs` in the session notes, not committed. The options, with their costs:
    1. The newest attempt owns the live stream from its first beat, and an earlier attempt's rows are kept but are never progress, whenever they come. Replay stays by number. Cost: the lower-numbered replacement is never progress, and reads stalled, then lost, while it steps.
    2. Ownership by arrival: the attempt seen last owns the rank, and a lower number is taken only when the owner has stopped (its stream dropped or finished, or its beats stale). Replay orders attempts by when each was first stored, which the store does not record today: a manifest per rank, or the time each attempt's first part was written. Cost: a new file or field the readers, Python's included, would read, and its order must survive a restart (the `restart` carry would hold the owner).
    3. Attempt numbers made to sort by start: the SDK takes the attempt from a monotonic source shared by the job (the launcher's restart count, or the larger of the clock and the last attempt stored plus one). Replay and the watchdog stay as they are. Cost: a change in the SDK, outside this lane, and a sender that does not use it keeps today's behavior.
    4. Leave it, and document that two processes of one rank streaming at once, or a replacement with a lower number, are read by number. Cost: the flip-flopping status and the replacement's rows left out of the detectors stay.
    No fix is common to all four, so none is committed. `_newest_part` in `evidence.py` takes the largest (attempt, step) as the newest part, so options 2 and 3 reach lane U7's file as well.
  - *What Python's readers would need (lane U7's `evidence.py`, not edited).* RS01 and RS02 change only the summary and the states the server writes, and the readers take the summary from the file: nothing to change. Python's `run_state` does not look at `attempt_without_steps`: a status that marks it beside a current report of the earlier attempt reads `healthy` there. To match this branch, a rank whose status entry marks `attempt_without_steps`, or names another attempt than its report, would be unknown. No expectation in `sdk/tests` had to change.
  - *Run.* `cargo build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, the same clippy with `--no-default-features`, `cargo test --locked` and `cargo test --locked --no-default-features --lib`, all in `server/`, on macOS: all pass (105 unit tests; 39 in `ingest_test.rs`, 38 passed and one ignored as before). The three ingest tests were also run against `1767a4a`'s server code: all three fail there (`healthy` at attempt 2, step 79; `healthy` with attempt 2's report held; the batch hashes still compared after the failed rebuild). The 27 Python files of CI's server job, with this branch's binary: 1114 passed, none skipped. The whole `sdk/tests` suite with this branch's binary: 1838 passed, none skipped.
  - *Not run.* Linux, a GPU, an object store other than local disk and memory. The new tests need no server binary; CI's server job runs them through `cargo test`.
  - *Seen, not in this lane.* RS06: a new attempt keeps the earlier attempt's phase clock, untouched here. A finished run is not taken up by a restarted server, so a heartbeat-only resume after a restart starts as a new rank (`starting`, then `silent`) with no `last_step`. A rank whose stream dropped longer ago than `abandon` and that beats with a new attempt still reads `abandoned` (only a finished rank's clock is reset). The data window of a rank whose new attempt is seen only in its beats stays in `ranks_compared` until a part is stored. A status written by an older server with a finished stream and `attempt_without_steps` is restored as finished. Lane T4's open items stay open.
  - *Open, for the author.* RS05's ordering: option 1, 2, 3 or 4 above, which also decides RS01's open case. Whether 30 s is the right `report_grace` for the server, and whether it should be a command-line option.

- [x] **Lane U1: capture paths that could still raise into or block training, no decision needed (Nov 19, 2026, lane U1, branch `claude/lane-u1-capture-65695e`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Wave U merged").** Cut from `main` at `1767a4a`. The code is in `capture.py`. The tests are in the new `sdk/tests/test_lane_u1_capture.py`, which needs no server binary. Two lines of `sdk/tests/test_capture.py` changed too; see below. The hand-over note, `lane-u1.md` in the session notes, lists every function touched, with the scripts.
  - *Found on the base, before any change* (macOS, CPU, PyTorch 2.15).
    - C01: `c01.py` gives 0.5 with capture off and raises `NotImplementedError` with it on. This lane also found three more cases in the same read. CSR, CSC, BSR and BSC gradients raised. A float8 gradient raised. A sparse gradient on `meta` was dropped with nothing said.
    - C04: `c04.py` commits 8 steps after `close()`, then blocks; it had not finished after 20 s.
    - B8: the experiment's buggy variant, run on a scratch copy, exits 1 with "Attempt to trace forbidden callable `mark_static_address`". A compiled loop that calls `optimizer.step()` also raised under `-W error`, even with `init` called outside it: Dynamo warned while tracing the session's optimizer hook (`sys._getframe`).
    - C10: `probe_head="nope"` raised its `ValueError`. It left 6 hooks on the model, which had none before, and wrote the run manifest.
    - C19: a `scaler.update` installed before `init` was gone after `close()`. With two sessions on one scaler, the second session's hook was gone once the first closed.
    - The probe: under `-W error`, `optimizer.step()` raised "TensorQuill probe failed and was skipped".
    - The docstring said 24 bytes. The gather is 32 (`distributed.py:236`).
  - *Fixed, each with tests that fail on the base.* 28 of the file's 36 tests fail when run on the base's code; the other 8 check behaviour that must stay as it was.
    - **Gradients (C01).** An unreadable gradient is now left out on its own.
      - `meta` and nested gradients are judged from device and layout alone.
      - Sparse gradients are read from their stored values, as the weight check reads sparse weights.
      - A group whose norm raises is read again one tensor at a time.
      - The step runs as it does without TensorQuill. The other gradients keep their norms, and `grad_norm` sums only the gradients that were read.
      - Each unread parameter is named once, under a new record field `grads_not_read: {name: why}`, on the first record written after it was found. It is carried the way `update_not_read` is. The Python row conversion for the stream (`flight.record_to_row`) puts it in `extra_json`; the Rust side was not run. The evidence reads past it.
      - Tests: `test_a_gradient_on_meta_is_left_out_on_its_own_and_the_step_runs_as_without_tensorquill`, `test_a_sparse_gradient_is_read_from_its_stored_values` (5 layouts), `test_an_unreadable_gradient_is_named_and_the_others_keep_their_norms` (3 kinds).
    - **A closed session (C04).** Every public entry point returns at once when the session is closed: no raise, nothing queued and no warning. The brief first allowed one warning. The session that started the lane corrected that: the shared rule wins, because a warning on the training thread is an exception under `python -W error`. To tell a user once, a later change could log at DEBUG on the `tensorquill` logger, which `-W error` does not touch, or count the calls and write the count on the end marker. Neither is built.
      - `watch_loader` and `time_data` hand back what they were given.
      - `close()` flushes through the private path.
      - The four `RuntimeError("the session is closed")` raises are gone.
      - Tests: `test_every_public_call_on_a_closed_session_returns_at_once_and_queues_nothing`, which runs 24 rounds of every public call (three times the writer queue's size) under a 20 s deadline; `test_a_closed_session_does_not_raise_under_warnings_as_errors`.
    - **`init` under tracing (B8).** `init` is now `@torch.compiler.disable`. The optimizer hooks and the scaler's wrapper are registered through it too, so a traced `optimizer.step()` runs them eagerly. Behind that wrapper the pre-hook finds the optimizer's frame by skipping `torch/_dynamo/` frames.
      - Tests: `test_init_inside_a_compiled_function_runs_and_captures`, which is B8's loop (counter 1, 2, 3, 3, 4, 5, and six records); `test_init_then_compile_the_model_still_captures`; `test_the_optimizer_hooks_do_not_raise_when_optimizer_step_is_traced_under_warnings_as_errors`.
      - `test_compile.py`: 4 passed, so 0 graph breaks and 0 recompiles.
    - **A rejected `init` (C10).** `_plan_session` now makes every check of the model and the options, with nothing changed. `init` runs it before it resolves the run.
      - `Session` then makes its effects in `_install`. Each effect registers its undo as it is made: hooks, the heartbeat counter, the writer thread, clipping, the scaler, the optimizer hooks and the loader timer.
      - `init` undoes its own effects: the default sink with its file and folders, the run manifest (the earlier bytes or no file), and the stream's heartbeat and sender. It never closes a sink the caller passed in.
      - Removing an earlier run's end marker is now the last step.
      - Tests: `test_a_rejected_init_leaves_hooks_threads_globals_and_files_as_they_were` (7 rejections), `test_a_later_failure_undoes_every_effect_made_before_it`, `test_a_later_failure_puts_an_earlier_runs_manifest_and_end_marker_back`.
    - **The scaler (C19).** Each scaler now has one `update` wrapper, shared by every session that watches it. When the last session leaves, the wrapper is taken out, but only while it is still the instance attribute. A wrapper someone installed on top after `init` is left in place, and ours under it passes calls through. `_scaler_original` is gone.
      - Tests: `test_closing_puts_back_an_update_someone_installed_before_init`, `test_two_sessions_on_one_scaler_keep_counting_whichever_closes_first` (both orders), `test_a_wrapper_installed_on_top_after_init_is_left_in_place`, `test_closing_leaves_a_scaler_with_its_class_method`.
    - **Warnings (the probe's, and the others training reaches).** `_warn` calls `warnings.warn`. Where a filter turns the warning into an error, it logs the message on the `tensorquill` logger instead.
      - It is used for the probe's warning, the foreign-step warning, the router refusal, and the replica-unread warning.
      - The backpressure warning in `_put` is left as it is (C03).
      - The probe's failure is still in its record as `{"error": ...}`.
      - Tests: `test_a_failed_probe_does_not_raise_out_of_the_step_under_warnings_as_errors`, `test_a_refused_router_and_steps_of_another_model_do_not_raise_under_warnings_as_errors`.
    - **The docstring.** It now says 32 bytes per parameter tensor, or 24 without the projection, with the flag named. Test: `test_init_says_how_many_bytes_the_drift_check_gathers`.

That is the contract C04 replaces. Its last two lines now assert that the call returns, with no warning, and writes nothing. This needs the author's yes.

  - *Item 8, C03, added to this lane after the author's decision (logged on `main` at `5c44ae7`): "stop capture, warn loudly once, and let training go on."* Done in commits of its own, after items 1 to 7 (`a037441`), so those can be merged alone.
    - *Reproduced first* (`c03.py`, `c03tb.py`): a sink whose `write` raised made `optimizer.step()` raise `RuntimeError: the TensorQuill writer thread failed` at step 1 (`_after_optimizer_step` > `_commit` > `_flush` > `_raise_worker_error`). Inside `with session:` the same error replaced the user's own `LookupError`. A writer that fell behind made `_put` wait without bound.
    - *Built.* One stopped state, `_stop(cause)`. It runs on the training thread only; the writer thread only keeps the sink's error, which the next step turns into a stop.
      - The causes are a sink that raised, a queue found full at `_put` (the block is dropped), and at `close()` a writer that has not finished within `CLOSE_SECONDS`.
      - The cause is kept as `session.writer_error`. One `RuntimeWarning` names it, sent through `_warn`, which cannot raise.
      - After the stop, every public entry point returns at once and says nothing (`_closed_call`). The exceptions are `set_phase`, `tick` and `phase`, which the heartbeat reads: they stay live.
      - The writer writes the blocks queued before the stop, which are an unbroken run of steps, then ends. Nothing more is copied off the device.
      - `_raise_worker_error` and the wait in `_put` are gone. The queue's capacity is in one place (`WRITER_QUEUE`, `_queue_blocks`).
    - *Hooks.* The forward hooks stay as they are. The clipping and loader hooks come off.
      - The optimizer hooks become a no-op in PyTorch's hook dictionary, and `close()` removes them. Removing them at the stop would raise: the stop is reached inside PyTorch's loop over those hooks, and an `OrderedDict` changed in size during iteration raises (run here). `RemovableHandle.hooks_dict_ref` and `.id` exist at PyTorch v2.6.0 (read in the clone).
      - The scaler's wrapper comes off, as `close()` takes it off.
      - With a `replica_group`, the stopped rank keeps its optimizer and scaler hooks. It counts steps and enters the drift check's `all_gather` on the same steps as the other ranks, and drops the result. Without that, the other ranks wait in the collective. That costs one float64 pass over the replicated weights per check on the stopped rank. It is a third point the decision did not settle; see "Open" below.
    - *After a stop.* The forward hooks keep their cost on every forward, since they stay on the model. Their buffers are no longer read.
    - *`close()`.* It never raises and never waits without bound.
      - On a streaming run with a stuck sink, `close()` after a stop waits about 8 s at most: 1 s for the writer, then the heartbeat's join (2 s) and the sender's `close` (`CLOSE_TIMEOUT`, 5 s). Both bounds were read in `flight.py`; this path was not run.
      - Its own flush, the sentinel put and the join share one deadline, `CLOSE_SECONDS` (60 s). Past it, capture is taken as stopped.
      - After a stop it waits `STOPPED_CLOSE_SECONDS` (1 s) for the writer. A sink still in a write is not closed under it; only a stream's heartbeat and sender are stopped. The writer is a daemon thread and does not hold the process.
      - No `<name>.end.json` after a stop, so the file reads as cut off.
      - A failing `write_end_marker` is caught.
      - Inside `with session:` the user's exception comes out as the same object, with no context from TensorQuill.
    - *Tests* (`test_lane_u1_capture.py`, 14 new; all 14 fail on `a037441`, and the file's other 36 pass there):
      - `test_a_failing_sink_stops_capture_and_every_step_runs_as_without_tensorquill`: `every` 1 and 4, plain and under `-W error`; the final weights equal a run without TensorQuill, with one warning or one log line.
      - `test_the_users_own_exception_comes_out_unchanged_after_the_sink_failed`
      - `test_a_sink_that_never_returns_holds_neither_training_nor_close`
      - `test_after_a_stop_no_end_marker_the_cause_kept_and_exactly_one_warning`
      - `test_after_a_stop_the_optimizer_hooks_do_nothing_and_close_takes_them_off`
      - `test_what_the_heartbeat_reads_stays_live_after_a_stop`
      - `test_close_returns_within_its_bound_when_the_last_write_is_slow`
      - `test_a_stop_causes_no_graph_break_and_no_recompile`, under `eager` and `aot_eager`: `error_on_recompile`, with no graph-break counters.
      - `test_a_stopped_rank_counts_the_steps_a_scaler_ends_as_a_live_one_does`: under a GradScaler, a step the scaler skipped (an inf gradient) ends a live session's step at `scaler.update()`. A stopped rank's count now matches, 4 steps and 4 gathers; before this, it read 3 and 3, which would leave its gather a step behind the other ranks'.
      - `test_a_stopped_rank_keeps_entering_the_drift_checks_gather`: two gloo ranks, with rank 0's sink failing. With the schedule-keeping turned off for one run, rank 1 waited in the `all_gather` until gloo's 60 s timeout and the test failed.
    - *Existing tests changed.* All are outside this lane's two files and need the author's yes.
      - `test_capture.py::test_a_failing_sink_is_reported_on_close` is now `test_a_failing_sink_stops_capture_and_close_returns`.
      - `test_capture.py::test_a_slow_sink_makes_training_wait_instead_of_growing_memory` is now `test_a_slow_sink_stops_capture_instead_of_making_training_wait`. It expects steps 0 to 2 and one warning.
      - `test_system.py::test_a_hung_nvml_call_never_holds_training` now expects steps 0 to 10 and a stop naming NVML. It also accepts all 43 steps with no stop, on a machine too slow to fill the queue in 0.5 s.
      - `test_system.py::test_a_hung_nvml_open_never_holds_training` passed unchanged, but it stopped capture after step 8 of 30 (the same in three runs) without checking records. It now says so: steps 0 to 8 and a stop naming NVML, or all 30 on a machine too slow to fill the queue.
      - The comment at `test_system.py:341` now asserts no stop.
      - In the full run, these two NVML tests are the only existing tests that emitted the stop's warning.
    - *The trigger, measured and not chosen.* The queue holds `WRITER_QUEUE` rings, 8: 8 steps at `every=1` and 400 steps at `every=50`, plus the block being written.
      - A writer stall of T seconds stops capture when training runs faster than the capacity divided by T.
      - The bounded wait for NVML (`system=True`) can be such a stall. At `every=1` a 2 s wait (`NVML_SLOW_SECONDS`) stops capture above 4 steps/s, and the 10 s wait for the first reading, which opens NVML (`NVML_OPEN_SECONDS`), stops it above 0.8 steps/s. At `every=50` the same waits stop it above 200 and 40 steps/s.
      - Measured with the fake NVML at a 0.5 s bound: 40 fast steps at `every=1` stopped capture after step 10, the same in three runs.
      - Measured with the fake NVML at its 1 s open bound: 30 fast steps at `every=1` stopped capture after step 8.
      - A short disk pause fills 8 steps at the same rates.
      - `close()`'s last flush goes through the same `_put`, which does not wait. So a writer that is healthy but still busy with the queued blocks can lose the last block and, with it, the end marker.
      - Options:
        - (a) A capacity counted in steps, for example at least 256 steps whatever `every` is. A block holds only the CPU copy of a few numbers per layer and parameter, so this costs little memory.
        - (b) A writer that skips the system reading while it is behind.
        - (c) A bound in seconds of lag instead of a count.
    - *A streaming run, measured and not fixed.* One rank streamed to the read-only server binary (silent after 2 s), and its local sink failed after 3 writes while training went on. Script: `lane-u1-c03-stream.py` in the session notes.
      - Still training after the stop: about 2 s later the server read the rank as `silent`, the run as `stalled`, with `job_stalled` / `suspected_hang`. The heartbeat still said phase `train`, but its step counter no longer moved.
      - Stopped, then `close()` while training went on for 8 s more: `finished`, and one look at the store said "No incidents detected" and "Run state: healthy".
      - Both are wrong verdicts on a healthy run. Nothing in the stream says that capture stopped.
      - What it would take is in other lanes' files: a message or heartbeat field saying that capture stopped and why (`flight.py`, the heartbeat), and a server state for it that is neither a hang nor `finished` (`server/`).
      - One option inside `capture.py`: at the stop, keep the heartbeat counter moving or set a phase such as `capture_stopped`. The server then would not call it a hang, but it still would not say why. Not built: the brief asks to measure here.
    - *Run.* The new file: 49 passed. The full `sdk/tests` with this worktree on `PYTHONPATH`: 1267 passed, 619 skipped, 1 failed. The failure was one of the new tests, which checked the stop before the writer thread had stored its error, a race under the machine's load. That test and one other now step until the stop has happened, and the new file then passed 49 of 49 three times in a row. `test_capture.py` and `test_system.py` pass.
    - *Stale wording, not edited.*
      - `sdk/README.md` line 768: a hung NVML call "can make training wait once".
      - `sdk/README.md` line 839, under "Things to know": the bounded writer queue, where training waits if the sink falls behind.
      - `sdk/README.md` line 331, "Nothing raises once training runs": the earlier note that this was not true of the local sink can now be dropped.
      - The Focused Plan's row on TensorQuill stopping a run.
      - `_put`'s own text ("training now waits for it") is rewritten here; it now names the stop.
    - *A compiled loop that calls `optimizer.step()`.* A stop while compiled made no recompile, with `error_on_recompile`. With `clip_grad_norm_` inside the compiled function, TensorQuill recompiles at step 1, before any stop, and does so on the base `1767a4a` too. Without TensorQuill it does not recompile. That is older than this lane and is left open.

      - The trigger, with the options above.
      - What a streaming run should say after a stop.

  - *Item 8, the queue's size, decided by the author after the measurement above (logged on `main` at `148cecf`): "Sizing the queue in steps (~400, with a 2-ring floor)".* This replaces the trigger's "measure, do not choose".
    - *Built.* `QUEUE_STEPS = 400` and `QUEUE_MIN_RINGS = 2` are the one place the size is set. `_queue_rings(every)` is `max(2, ceil(400 / every))`: 400 rings at `every=1`, 8 at 50, 4 at 100, 2 from 200 up. `_queue_blocks` keeps one queue entry per ring at `every=1` and two otherwise.
      - The stop's warning now counts its steps from the real capacity.
      - `WRITER_QUEUE` is gone. Its uses changed: in `capture.py`, the constant, `_queue_blocks` and `_put`'s message; in the tests, `test_capture.py`'s slow-sink test (it now monkeypatches `QUEUE_STEPS` to 2, giving 2 blocks at `every=1`) and two tests in the new file (they set `QUEUE_STEPS` to 8 and count with `_queue_blocks(1)`).
    - *`close()` at the floor.* `close()`'s last flush may now wait for room in the queue, up to `close()`'s own deadline, `CLOSE_SECONDS` (60 s). Training only ever uses the put that does not wait.
      - The bound: a wait inside `close()` pauses no training step. 60 s covers a slow network filesystem writing a few blocks, and it still lets a process with a sink that is truly stuck end within a minute, and then without an end marker.
      - Test: `test_close_at_the_two_ring_floor_waits_for_a_slow_but_working_writer`, at `every=200` with the queue full at close. All 600 steps and the end marker are written.
    - *`_flush` cannot leave the ring broken.* Lane U5 found that one raise inside `_flush` (the replica-unread warning under `-W error`, on the base) made every later step raise `IndexError`. `_flush` now turns any exception into a stop, which clears the ring. Test: `test_an_exception_part_way_through_a_flush_stops_capture_and_later_steps_run`. U5's own reproducer, `lane-u5-werror-flush.py`, runs every step on this branch.
    - *Tests.* Also `test_the_writer_queue_holds_about_400_steps_with_a_floor_of_two_rings` (`every` 1, 3, 50, 100, 200 and 1000) and `test_the_stop_warning_names_the_real_capacity`. All fail on `0add73a`. The `every=50` case fails only by the new function's name: its size, 16 blocks, is unchanged. The two tests that now monkeypatch `QUEUE_STEPS` fail there only on that name.
    - *Existing tests changed again.* The two NVML hang tests in `test_system.py` no longer stop: their 40 and 30 steps fit in a queue of 400. Each now asserts that every step is written and there is no stop.
    - *The NVML wait, by the bounds and not run on a GPU.*
      - A writer stall of T seconds stops capture when training runs faster than the queue's steps divided by T. The queue holds about 400 steps, plus the block being written; it holds 2 rings, so 2 × `every` steps, from `every=200` up.
      - With `system=True`, a hung reading (`NVML_SLOW_SECONDS`, 2 s) stops capture above about 200 steps/s. The first reading, which opens NVML (`NVML_OPEN_SECONDS`, 10 s), stops it above about 40 steps/s, and only at the start of the run. At `every=1000` the same waits stop capture above 1000 and 200 steps/s.
      - The fake NVML measured before the change stopped capture exactly where this count puts it: 3 steps written, 1 in the write and 8 queued.
    - *Not changed.* What a streaming run reads after a stop, as measured above.
    - *Notes from other lanes.* Lane U3's loader wrapper reads `session._closed` to pass batches through. A stopped session keeps `_closed` false, because `close()` must still run, but its `data()` returns at once, so the wrapper already does nothing harmful. The wrapper could read `_closed or _stopped` to skip hashing the indices. Lane U5's `tracker._say` and this lane's `capture._warn` do the same job; one could replace the other at the merge. U5 also asks for the replica-unread warning once per reason rather than once per parameter; that is not changed here.
