  - *Item 10, F1, decided by the author (logged on `main` at `543f2cc`): "read the bool routing map [..., n_experts], filtering probs != 0 when paired with a same-shape float tensor. option a".* Done in commits of its own, after item 9. With it, nothing in `capture.py` is held back.
    - *Reproduced first, on this branch.*
      - `f1-megatron-capacity-drop.py` counted `[2, 2, 0, 0]` where the map routes `[1, 1, 0, 0]`, and the other misreads as the F1 entry lists them.
      - `router121.py`, the script from `CORRECTNESS_AUDIT.md`: 36 of 36 full maps matched, 0 of 85 with fewer assignments.
    - *Built* (`_routing_map`, `_router_hook`). The order of sources: an `indices=` function, then an integer tensor, as before; then a bool routing map; then the top-`top_k` of a float tensor.
      - A map is the first bool tensor whose last dimension is `n_experts` and whose other dimensions multiply to the tokens the hook was called on. The token check is skipped where that count is symbolic.
      - A map is read only when the output also holds a floating-point tensor, so only an output of a float tensor and a bool map is counted differently. A bool tensor alone is refused as before; `test_router_guard.py`'s bool case holds that.
      - The count, per expert, is the true entries of the map. Entries whose paired float is exactly zero are left out. The paired float is the first floating-point tensor of exactly the map's shape; with none of that shape, every true entry counts.
      - `_unusable` (device, layout and dtype, and item 9's subclass check) applies to the map and to its paired float.
      - There is no branch on the data, so it traces the same under `torch.compile`. `Router`'s docstring states the order and the pairing rule.
    - *Run after the change.*
      - `f1-megatron-capacity-drop.py`: two tokens `[1, 1, 0, 0]`; the partial drop `[1, 1, 1, 1]`; 64 tokens `[24, 32, 32, 32]` ("probs" policy) and `[32, 32, 28, 32]` ("position"); no capacity limit `[34, 31, 33, 30]`, as before; padded to capacity `[29, 32, 32, 31]` and `[32, 32, 24, 30]`, the entries with a nonzero probability.
      - `router121.py`: 121 of 121 maps match. Tokens with fewer than `top_k` assignments, and with none, are counted as the map says.
    - *Tests* (13 new; 11 fail on `ee0166e`, and the 2 that pass there guard what must not change).
      - `test_a_megatron_style_router_is_counted_as_its_routing_map_routes` (7 cases): each checks the router's own map or nonzero probabilities, and the two cases that use no random numbers check the plan's numbers too.
      - `test_every_one_of_the_121_two_token_maps_is_counted_as_it_routes`
      - `test_a_mask_over_the_experts_alone_is_not_taken_for_a_routing_map` (guard)
      - `test_with_logits_before_the_probabilities_the_logits_are_paired_and_padding_is_counted`, which pins the limit below.
      - `test_a_routing_map_on_another_device_is_refused_as_any_output_there_is`
      - `test_a_compiled_megatron_style_router_counts_as_in_eager_mode` (`eager` and `aot_eager`, each with a control that compiles without TensorQuill).
      - `test_compile.py`: 0 graph breaks and 0 recompiles. The full `sdk/tests` with this worktree on `PYTHONPATH`: 1300 passed, 619 skipped, none failed.
    - *Limits, stated.*
      - The pairing takes the first float tensor of the map's shape. An output of logits, probabilities and a map pairs the logits, which are never zero, so with `pad_to_capacity` the padding is counted.
      - A real assignment whose weight is exactly zero, after an underflow in half precision for example, is left out together with the padding.
    - *Downstream* (script `lane-u1-f1-downstream.py`): a capture of 120 steps whose router keeps 80 of 128 assignments a step (capacity factor 0.6), run through `build_evidence` with the Python detectors and with the read-only server binary's `detect`.
      - Both ran and agreed: no incident, with `moe_imbalance` and `starved_experts` checked. The same with no capacity limit.
      - No MoE rule needs tokens × `top_k` assignments: shares are taken over each step's own total.
      - One soft assumption: the top-k ≥ 3 jump rule estimates tokens as the total divided by k (`evidence.py` `_jump_overloaded`, `detect.rs:1006`). With drops that estimate falls with the kept assignments.
      - `moe_stats.routed_tokens` is the number of assignments, not tokens. It already was with `top_k` above 1, and is now the assignments kept.
    - *Open, for the author.*
      - Nothing in the record or the manifest shows which source a router was counted from. A router that returns `(probs, routing_map)` now counts differently from before, with nothing in the record saying so. Proposed: `moe_source: {router: "indices=" | "integer" | "routing_map" | "top_k"}`, said once, as `moe_refused` is. Not built: a schema change with readers in the evidence and the server.
      - The audit's C06 integer case, `[-1, 8]` for 4 experts recorded as `[0, 0, 0, 0]`, is still open and undecided; not touched here.
    - *Not run.* Megatron itself is not installed; the stand-in follows the reference clone. Also not run: a GPU, inductor, PyTorch 2.6.

- [x] **Lane U5: a tracker or logger that raises no longer fails `init`, and a drift-check gather that cannot run is skipped on every rank (Nov 19, 2026, lane U5, branch `claude/lane-u5-tracker-gather-9170fa`; merged into `main` at the author's word, see "Wave U merged").** These are the three items Wave U gave this lane: C05, SDK-22's gather, and a Lightning logger property that raises. Each was reproduced on `1767a4a` before any change. The code is in `tracker.py`, `distributed.py` and `lightning.py`. The tests are in a new file, `sdk/tests/test_lane_u5_tracker_gather.py`: 20 tests, none of which needs the server binary. `capture.py`, the record fields, the detectors, the golden files, everything under `server/` and the protocol number are unchanged. The hand-over note is `lane-u5.md` in the session notes, with the scripts `lane-u5-*.py`.
  - *Outcomes.*
    - C05: fixed.
    - SDK-22: fixed where every rank would fail alike, before anything is sent. A gather that fails once it is issued still raises. A catch there was measured to make things worse, so no catch ships; the design for the rest is below.
    - The Lightning logger: fixed.
    - **Merge dependency.** SDK-22's skip leaves `optimizer.step()` free of a raise only once `capture.py`'s warning for a parameter it is not comparing cannot raise. On this branch alone, under `-W error`, that warning still raises out of `optimizer.step()` at the next flush, and every later step then raises `IndexError` (`lane-u5-werror-flush.py`, the same on the base through T2's `meta` path). U1's `_warn` covers it; merge U1 with or before this lane.
  - *Reproduced on the base* (macOS, CPU, PyTorch 2.15, Lightning 2.6.6, Gloo).
    - **C05.** The audit's `c05.py` gave `init raised RuntimeError tracker temporarily down`. A W&B run whose `id` raises, and a `wandb` module whose `run` raises, made `init` raise the same way.
    - **SDK-22, injected.** T6's script, adapted by T2, still raised out of `optimizer.step()` at the scheduled step.
    - **SDK-22, two real cases on CPU** (nothing injected):
      - A group with Gloo registered for CUDA tensors only (`init_process_group("cuda:gloo")`), standing in for NCCL given CPU tensors. Both ranks raised `No backend type associated with device type cpu` out of `optimizer.step()` at the first scheduled check.
      - A rank outside the replica group: three ranks, with the group made of ranks 0 and 1. On rank 2, PyTorch's `all_gather` warned that the rank does not belong to the group, returned nothing, and `torch.stack([])` raised out of `optimizer.step()`. Rank 2 then stopped issuing the training run's all-reduce, so ranks 0 and 1 timed out in it.
    - **Lightning.** With a logger whose `run_id` raises, `trainer.fit` raised `RuntimeError` from `on_fit_start`.
  - *C05, fixed.*
    - **One lookup for every path.** On one process, or on several ranks with no process group, `resolve_run` now calls `_resolve_here`, the guarded lookup rank 0 already used. A tracker that raises anywhere in reading its run's ID is passed over with one warning ("could not read the tracker's run"). The order then goes on: the launcher's ID, else a generated one.
    - **The several-ranks warnings are kept.** `_warn_tracker_unshared` and the warning for a generated ID (now `_warn_generated_unshared`, which replaces `_unshared_generated_id`) are kept.
    - **The project and run names.** `tracker_labels` now also guards reading `wandb.run`. Each label was already guarded.
    - **No warning can raise.** Every warning in `tracker.py` now goes through `_say`. When a filter makes the warning an error (`python -W error`), the message is logged on the `tensorquill` logger instead, as U1's `_warn` does in `capture.py`.
    - **Found while testing, on two ranks under `-W error`.** Rank 0's own warning about the tracker raised before rank 0 put its ID in the store. So rank 0's `init` failed, and rank 1 waited until the test's 90 s limit.
    - **Tests:**
      - `test_c05_a_tracker_that_raises_gives_a_generated_run_id_and_one_warning`, four cases;
      - `test_c05_a_tracker_that_raises_falls_through_to_the_launchers_id`;
      - `test_c05_a_project_or_name_that_raises_is_left_out` (this one passed before too);
      - `test_c05_tracker_labels_never_raises`;
      - `test_c05_under_warnings_as_errors_init_still_returns`;
      - `test_c05_the_several_ranks_warnings_are_kept`;
      - `test_c05_the_single_process_path_takes_the_guarded_lookup`;
      - `test_c05_two_ranks_under_warnings_as_errors_share_rank_zeros_generated_id`.
  - *SDK-22: a catch around the gather was tried first, and does not ship.*
    - **The prototype.** A catch around the session's gather returned every row flagged and kept the schedule. It ran on two Gloo ranks with a 10 s group timeout. `dist.all_gather` raised on rank 1 only, once and then at every scheduled check. Each step also ran an all-reduce, standing in for DDP's. The script is `lane-u5-catch-one-rank.py`.
    - **What happened.**
      - Rank 0's step sat 10.0 s, the whole group timeout, in a gather rank 1 never entered.
      - Rank 1's next training all-reduce then failed after 10.0 s.
      - With the failure at every check, both ranks' training all-reduces raised by step 4.
      - In PyTorch 2.6 the default timeout is 30 minutes for Gloo and 10 minutes for NCCL (`kBackendDefaultTimeout`, `kProcessGroupNCCLDefaultTimeout`, read in the source). What NCCL's watchdog then does to the job was not run here.
    - **The cause.** A rank cannot tell from its own error whether its peers failed too. A rank that catches and goes on issues the training run's next collective while its peers wait in this one. That is the hazard the brief named, so there is no catch around the collective.
  - *SDK-22: what ships, a check every rank decides alike, with no collective.*
    - **The check.** `distributed._gather_problem(group, device)` runs on the host before the gather, from what every rank of a data-parallel job shares, and skips the gather when:
      - the group has no backend for the table's device type (`ProcessGroup._get_backend` raises, as for NCCL, which serves CUDA only, given a CPU table, for example under FSDP's CPU offload);
      - there is no default process group (destroyed, or never made);
      - the rank is not a member of the group. PyTorch's own collectives return at once on such a rank, so its peers never wait for it.
    - **Where it decides nothing.** A group that is not a `ProcessGroup` (the stand-ins tests pass with their own `all_gather`) is still gathered. So is anything the check cannot read.
    - **What a skip gives.** `_spread_and_unread` issues no collective. It returns zeros, the new code `_NOT_GATHERED` on every row, and the reason. The session then leaves every parameter out of `replica_spread` and names each once, by T2's path, with no change to `capture.py`.
    - **The codes.** The code is 10, added at the end, so the codes T2 numbered keep their numbers between ranks. `_RAISED` is still 9. An unknown code still reads "reading it raised an error".
    - **The parameters are no longer read when skipping.** The device is decided first, and the fingerprints are not computed.
    - **Tests:**
      - `test_sdk22_a_group_with_no_backend_for_the_tables_device_skips_on_every_rank` (two processes over `cuda:gloo`);
      - `test_sdk22_a_rank_outside_the_replica_group_skips_and_its_peers_are_not_left_waiting` (three processes; every step runs an all-reduce over all three, so a rank that skipped or added a collective would break it; ranks 0 and 1 still compare);
      - `test_sdk22_the_check_is_decided_without_a_collective`;
      - `test_sdk22_existing_codes_keep_their_numbers`;
      - `test_sdk22_a_stand_in_group_is_still_gathered` (this one passed before too).
  - *SDK-22: what is not covered.*
    - **Failures once the gather is issued still raise out of `optimizer.step()`.** Examples: a process group that failed or was aborted on one rank, a device bound with `device_id` other than the table's, a timeout. In each, the training run's own collectives on that group fail too.
    - **Ranks whose sessions have different device types.** The check trusts that every replica's session is on the same kind of device. Ranks that differ would decide differently: one would skip while its peers gather, which is the hazard of the catch above and worse than today's raise. The store agreement in the design below closes it.
    - **The verdict.** A drift check that never gathered reads `not_configured`, not `unavailable`, because no record carries the reason. That needs a record field, a schema change, as T2 noted.
    - **Warnings.** The session warns once for each parameter, so a skipped check gives one warning per replicated parameter. One warning per reason would do; that is in `capture.py`.
  - *SDK-22: the design for the rest (not built).*
    - **At `init`, before the first scheduled check** (`capture.py`, U1's file), each rank runs `_gather_problem` and writes its verdict to the default store under a key for that `init`, as T1 shares the run ID.
    - **One verdict for all.** Each rank waits a bounded time, at `init`, never inside `optimizer.step()`, for every rank's verdict. It then puts "on" (every rank said yes) or "off" (any said no, or the wait ran out) under one key with `compare_set`, first writer wins. `compare_set` is in PyTorch 2.6 for TCPStore and FileStore. Every rank then takes the value the store holds, so all switch the check on or off together, even a rank whose wait ran out.
    - **What it cannot cover.** A failure inside an issued collective. That is a failed group, which the training run's own collectives meet too.
  - *The public `replica_spread`: options, nothing changed.* It still raises on a parameter on `meta` or sparse (through `parameter_fingerprints`), and a gather that fails raises from it.
    - (a) Leave it. It is the caller's own call, outside TensorQuill's hooks; say so in its docstring.
    - (b) Build it on `_gather_problem` and `_fingerprint_table`, as the session is. Every rank enters the same gather and none waits. But it gathers 32 bytes per parameter, not 24, and it must say which rows were not read: a NaN row would read as drift, and a shorter result breaks `[n_params, 3]`.
    - (c) Raise a `ValueError` before the gather for a parameter that cannot be read. This is safe only when every rank raises alike: a parameter on `meta` on one rank only leaves the others waiting, which is T2's finding again.
  - *Lightning, fixed.*
    - **What changed.** `_logger_run_id` lists the trainer's loggers and reads each one's `run_id` or `version` inside a `try`. A logger that raises is passed over with one warning (through `_say`), and the next is read. With none left, `init` resolves rank 0's ID by its order and shares it with every rank.
    - **Tests:**
      - `test_lightning_a_logger_that_raises_does_not_abort_the_fit`;
      - `test_lightning_a_logger_that_raises_is_passed_over_for_the_next`;
      - `test_lightning_loggers_that_cannot_be_listed_or_read_give_none`;
      - `test_lightning_a_logger_that_raises_on_rank_zero_still_gives_every_rank_its_id` (two processes, `ddp_spawn` over Gloo; both rank files share one generated ID).
  - *T1's open choices, reported only.*
    - **`RUN_ID_WAIT_SECONDS`, 300 s.** The bound is the store's own timeout when shorter. Under `env://` and torchrun that is the group's timeout. A store passed to `init_process_group(store=...)` keeps its own timeout, though: a FileStore's is 300 s. So on the base, the two-rank test with a 15 s group timeout ran into the test's 90 s limit.
    - **A rank given its own `run_id`** still waits for rank 0's. Unchanged.
    - **Lightning's XLA strategy.** Not checked: there is no XLA here.
    - **`MLFlowLogger`'s names.** The callback passes only the run ID. The project and name come from `MLFLOW_EXPERIMENT_NAME` and the active run, which `MLFlowLogger` does not set. Unchanged.
  - *Seen outside this lane's files, not edited.*
    - **For U1, under `-W error` (`lane-u5-werror-flush.py`).** On the base, the session's warning for a parameter it is not comparing (`capture.py:1836`, reached by T2's `meta` path) raised out of `optimizer.step()` at the flush. Every later step then raised `IndexError: index 2 is out of bounds`. U1's notes say `_warn` now covers that warning. The `IndexError` after a raise inside `_flush` means the flush leaves its state broken when anything in it raises.
    - **C15.** `record_replica_spread` copies a precomputed spread with `spread_buf[:, :width].copy_(spread)` (`capture.py:1817`), so a `[1, 3]` table is broadcast to every parameter.
  - *Wording that no longer matches (listed, not edited).*
    - **The Focused Plan's row on TensorQuill stopping a run** says "a drift-check gather that fails, such as NCCL given CPU tensors" can still raise. NCCL given CPU tensors is now skipped; a gather that fails once issued still raises.
    - **`sdk/README.md`, the drift-check paragraph (:271) and the limits list (:838)** do not say that the check is skipped, on every rank, where its gather cannot run.
    - **`init`'s docstring (`capture.py:2782`)** still says 24 bytes. That is U1's item.
  - *Run.*
    - The new file: 20 passed; 18 of them fail on the base.
    - `test_tracker.py`, `test_run_id_ranks.py`, `test_lightning.py`, `test_distributed.py`, `test_capture_raises.py` and `test_compile.py` (still 0 graph breaks and 0 recompiles): 80 passed with the new file.
    - The full `sdk/tests` with this worktree on `PYTHONPATH`, at `38436bd`: 1239 passed, 619 skipped, 0 failed; the skips were not sorted by cause here.
    - The PyTorch APIs used were read in the `v2.6.0` source: `_rank_not_in_group`, `GroupMember.NON_GROUP_MEMBER`, `get_backend`, and `Store.compare_set` for the design. `ProcessGroup._get_backend` is bound there, and its `getBackend` raises "No backend type associated with device type" for a type with no backend, as on 2.15. A `device:backend` string registers only the types it names, so the `cuda:gloo` test should behave the same on 2.6; it was not run there.
  - *Not run.* NCCL, a GPU, MPI, Linux, PyTorch 2.6 itself, XLA.
  - *Open, for the author.*
    - Whether to build the store agreement at `init` for the rest of SDK-22. It is in `capture.py`.
    - Option (a), (b) or (c) for the public `replica_spread`.
    - Whether a drift check that never gathered should read `unavailable` with its reason, which needs a record field.

- [x] **SDK-06: the drift check stays where it is, and the limit is left open for v1 (decided by the author Nov 19, 2026: "Keep the gather where it is and leave SDK-06 open for v1").** The second audit's residual of the first review's SDK-06: after a second optimizer steps, the drift check is not repeated.
  - *What lane W4 found, which owns the judgement.* Today's timing is safe for the collective: every rank takes the gather at the lead optimizer's commit, before the others step. The only safe move is one gather per scheduled step number, at the lead's next pre-step, timed by the step number alone, with the gather owed at `close()` dropped on every rank.
  - *Why it is not moved.* The current schedule gives every rank a predictable point to enter the collective (`capture.py:2327`). Moving it changes that point and loses the last scheduled check when training ends. For a monitor whose first rule is to keep training running, the sampling gap is accepted.
  - *The limit, stated as it is.* The check runs after the lead optimizer steps and before the second optimizer steps. With the default 50-step interval, drift the second optimizer introduces right after a check can be detected at the next check if it persists. If the run ends or the replicas agree again before then, it can go undetected. "A one-interval delay" would overpromise and is not used.
  - *Done.* The limit is in `sdk/README.md`, beside the bullet on the check being a collective, and in `OPEN_BUGS.md`. Lanes V1 and W4 are told to write no code for it.
- [x] **Waves V and W: the three contracts signed off by the author, with amendments (Nov 19, 2026: "I'd sign off all three with amendments").** The notes are `contract-1-attempt-ownership.md`, `contract-2-stall.md` and `contract-3-capture-stopped.md` in the session notes' `wave-vw/` folder; the amendments are appended to each and their owner lanes fold them in. No source file is changed by this entry.
  - *Contract 1, which attempt owns a rank (lane W2).* The plan's "first seen by the server" was wrong in one real case, which W2 found: the server is down while attempt A runs, the job restarts as B, B heartbeats first, and B's sender then uploads A's unsent rows; A would look newer. The rule as approved separates a claim from rows. A claim is a live process naming its own attempt, by a heartbeat or by a new optional batch key, `sender_attempt`; an attempt first met in a claim is the newest. An attempt first met only in rows goes just before the attempt that sent them. The newest owns the rank; attempt numbers are identities, not order. The order is kept per rank in `attempts.json`, written before the reply that places an attempt, and replay and every store reader take it from there; a local file uses first appearance. Numeric order sat in more places than the plan named: the offline binary's `judge_with` refuses A = 900 then B = 2, and the server's `Stored.newest` would leave B `behind`.
    - Amendment: until B writes its first row the rank shows `attempt: 2`, `last_step: null`, `attempt_without_steps: true`. Step 100 belongs to A.
    - Amendment: a failed write of `attempts.json` is not accepted in memory. The server returns a retryable failure to the telemetry sender and places nothing, or it could acknowledge B, restart and forget that B owns the rank. Training continues.
  - *Contract 2, when unchanged weights are a stall (lane W3; lane V1 for the capture).* A tracked parameter absent from a written `weights_unchanged` list has its streak reset; the parameter stays tracked. Reproduced in both engines on `66b52d7`: the audit's case fires at step 3 today and gives no incident under the rule, and the author's acceptance case fires at step 5. No golden file changes on the Python path; Rust is not written. The three cells, approved as recommended: an unchanged weight with no gradient entry holds its streak; an explicit zero or non-finite parameter gradient resets it; a non-finite total gradient permits resets and adds no stall evidence.
    - Amendment: a parameter missing from the list may have been unreadable. That breaks a streak, and it cannot by itself justify telling the user the weight "changed at earlier checks". `trained_before` is qualified wherever it is presented, or only confirmed changes are counted.
  - *Contract 3, capture stopped on a streaming run (lane W2).* One new heartbeat key, `capture_stopped` with a short reason, on every beat after the stop, and a bounded last beat at `close()`. A rank state and a sixth summary word of the same name, with no severity and no progress incident; sticky for the attempt; a cleanly closed stream still reads `capture_stopped`; a run with such a rank never reads healthy. Approved with its limit: if every stop notice is lost and the server sees a clean close, it reads `finished`, accepted for v1.
    - Amendment: once the server has observed the stop it keeps the reason whatever state is visible. The draft kept it only while the state was `capture_stopped`, so a restart while the rank read `unresponsive` or `lost` would erase it. The lifecycle test gains that restart.
  - *Decided by the top session while the lanes ran, each recorded in `wave-vw/changes-after-launch.md`.*
    - RUST-12's coverage case moved from V7 to W3: Python's `input_counts` counts skipped steps the same way and a parity test holds the two together.
    - Lane W4 adds one call in `capture.py`, `_agree_on_gather(...)` in `Session.__init__`, after V1 is merged: the function exists only on W4's branch. Until then its end-to-end test is a strict expected failure.
    - Lane V8 edits four lines in V4's test files that read the nested output folders of `seeded_failures.py`, which its CLI-19 fix makes flat.
    - C16: lane V2's fixture is shared by byte-identical copies. Across the encoding change a resume is reported through the existing `not_checked` entry with one reason text in both engines; the other three detectors give no incident and nothing new.
    - Lane W1's three fields are approved in `extra_json` and `system_json`, no column: `moe_unassigned`, `moe_left_out`, `read_at`.
  - *W1's two MoE fields are written as explicit zeros (decided by the author the same day).* `moe_unassigned` is 0 when a router's IDs were checked and none were unassigned; `moe_left_out` is an all-zero per-expert vector when its routing map was checked and nothing was left out. The field is omitted for a router when the check did not run or did not apply, and readers treat a missing value, older records included, as unknown. "We checked and found zero" is a result and must read differently from "this writer did not measure that". The storage cost of the per-expert vector is accepted for v1, so each expert's routing-map count is `moe[e] + moe_left_out[e]` without guessing.
  - *Still the author's.* A public `skipped=` on `session.step()` for the Megatron example (X09's detector half), which the author said can wait.

Fixed with a plain loop; no version-specific code.

The probe would have recorded an error for that step. Fixed without calling `histc` when every value is the same: the bounds are reported as before and the values fall in the middle bin, where `histc` puts them when the widening holds (checked equal on 1.0, -3.0 and 0.0).
  - *Python 3.13 and 3.14 (two jobs), a fault in two of lane V1's tests.* They read an argument's text out of `init`'s docstring by a fixed indent of twelve spaces; Python 3.13 strips a docstring's common indentation when it compiles it. - [x] **A capture-stopped rank that then goes silent keeps raising its incident (Nov 19, 2026, decided by the author: "yes keep raising").** The bug list had this as C03's open part: such a rank, once it reads `unresponsive` or `lost`, raises `job_stalled` (alone) or `rank_silent` (beside an active rank). Two lanes of wave X went opposite ways on it: X3 made the Python reader raise nothing, and X6 confirmed the server raises the same incidents and left it, citing contract 3. The decision is contract 3 as signed, with no amendment:
  - *Why.* A capture-stopped rank still sends heartbeats, so their stopping is new evidence, the same evidence that raises the incident for any rank. Raising nothing would leave a job that really hangs after a capture stop with no signal; a false alarm costs one look. The case the bug list was right about is already handled: with fresh beats and a work counter standing still, such a rank raises nothing.
  - *The sibling repo read first.* wandb has one rule for silence: a run with no file-stream heartbeat for 5 minutes is moved to `crashed` (the sweep is in its server, read here from the comment in `core/internal/leet/heartbeat.go`), the local viewer words it "presumed crashed" and revives the run if writes return, and the one exemption is a `Preempting` signal the client sends before a silence it expects. `capture_stopped` says recording stopped, not that the rank will go silent, so it is not that exemption.
  - *What follows.* Lane X3 was told to undo its C03 commit (`fd7e5ef`) on its branch; the server is unchanged. Not done: the incident does not yet say that capture had stopped earlier, at which step and why; that wording is a change in both engines, listed in the bug list under C03 and not started.

- [x] **Decision, SDK-22: the drift check's gather leaves the training step for v1 (Nov 20, 2026; decided, nothing built).** The author was shown a proposal to remove the monitoring collective from the training path, asked "what do you think", and to "Approve taking the drift gather out of the training step for v1?" answered "Yes, approve".
  - *What is decided.* A session no longer issues a collective inside a training step on its own schedule. Each rank records its own fingerprints at the scheduled step, and the comparison is made by a reader that has every rank's record for the same run, attempt and step: the all-ranks reader in Python and the server's cross-rank stage. A rank whose record is missing makes the verdict insufficient coverage, not clean. The cost accepted: the verdict comes after the records do, not inside the step.
  - *Why.* The gather is one `all_gather` in `_spread_and_unread` (`distributed.py`), run in the step. A process group that fails after `init` fails or waits there, which is SDK-22's open part and against the rule that nothing in TensorQuill blocks or raises into training. The agreement at `init`, the stopped rank that keeps entering the gather, and the two waits of L18 exist only to keep that gather safe.
  - *What the code already has for it.* The projection is seeded by a parameter's position in the checked list (`_projection_weights`), so ranks compute comparable fingerprints with no exchange. The server already compares ranks' data hashes per run in its cross-rank stage (`ingest.rs`), and Python has a cross-rank rule in `memory_imbalance`.
  - *Sibling repos.* PyTorch Lightning's `sync_dist`, the cross-rank reduce for logged metrics, is off by default with a warning about communication overhead (`core/module.py`). SageMaker Debugger has each worker save on its own, and its reader returns by default only the steps received from all workers (`docs/analysis.md`, `show_incomplete_steps`).
  - *What it costs beyond latency.* One rank's file, or one rank's detectors, can no longer raise `replica_drift`: today each rank's record carries the finished `replica_spread`. The rule moves out of the per-rank detectors in both engines, and a record field for the fingerprints is new. Old captures that hold `replica_spread` keep their verdicts (the author's second message, below). What was found in the repository: 72 golden files name the field, and in the two read (`gate-1-unseen-1.json`, `system-112-steps.json`) it is only the label of a rule that was `not_configured`; no golden or experiment file was found with recorded spread rows, and the recorded captures in `~/Downloads/runs` were not looked at. The refusal for ranks on different device types is made at `init` today; no per-rank device field was found in the records, so the reader needs one to keep that refusal.
  - *How it sits with the rest.* It is a wave of its own, after the six lanes proposed on Nov 20: it edits `capture.py`, `distributed.py`, `evidence.py`, `detect.rs`, the server's cross-rank stage and `replica_check.py`. D1 (naming the rank) becomes a reader-side majority count over the stored fingerprints; D1 is still held by the author and is not released by this decision. With two ranks that disagree, either could be wrong; a consistent majority names an outlier.
  - *The author's second message, the same day,* settled what the first left open and added three points.
    - *The two explicit calls stay.* `replica_spread()` and `session.record_replica_spread(group)` remain collectives a user calls on purpose, documented as such: every participating rank must call them, and they can block.
    - *Order.* The decision comes before the six lanes start. XC2 (`replica_check.py`) moves out of lane 5 and is reserved for the SDK-22 wave, which is a wave of its own after the six merge. Proof result 1 is amended before the new comparison is built.

The suites of lanes V7, W2 and W3 ran there for the first time, with those of waves X and Y. None of the 87 skips was for want of the binary or pyarrow; they are optional packages the job does not install (`transformers`, `lightning`, `rerun`, `accelerate`).
  - *Failure 1, the server job.* `test_all_ranks_rust.py::test_the_same_kind_at_the_same_step_naming_different_things_is_two_groups` (its body is in `test_watch_all_ranks.py`): after four ranks sent to a server and one all-ranks look, the first incident group held ranks `[1, 2]` where `[0, 1, 2]` was expected. The failure on `e158ed8` was the same symptom in another test, rank 0 missing from `judged_ranks`. Two pushes in a row: a pattern in the all-ranks reading of a server's store on a slow runner, cause not found.
  - *Failure 2, Python 3.11 with PyTorch 2.15.* `test_a_stopped_rank_keeps_entering_the_drift_checks_gather`, and not as a timeout this time. Lane X2's progress lines show both ranks completing all four gathers, `session.close` returning (on rank 0 with the planted `OSError('disk gone')`), and `destroy_process_group` returning; then rank 0's process aborted as it exited, with `terminate called without an active exception` on stderr, SIGABRT, exit code -6, and no stack dumped. So this occurrence is a crash at interpreter shutdown on the rank whose writer had failed, not a rank waiting in a gather. Whether the timeout on `99c3b8f` (Python 3.12, PyTorch 2.6) was the same fault is not known. It bears on the rule that TensorQuill never terminates training: a job that finishes and then exits with an abort code is reported as failed by a launcher.
  - *Investigated the same day, on the author's "Yes, investigate". Failure 1: cause found, not fixed.* The server's status gives a rank whose stream is open and whose first batch is not stored yet `attempt` 0 and `last_step` null, state `starting`. `_not_current_by_status` in `evidence.py` (lane X3's L6) compares that 0 with the attempt of the rank's report and reads "attempt 1 was judged, and the server's status shows attempt 0": the rank becomes `earlier_attempt` and no value judges it. The status is rewritten only on the server's interval, 10 s by default, so the snapshot can stand for up to 10 s after the rank's report is current. Reproduced with the real server at its default interval and the test's own arguments, the only addition a wait before the first send so the 10 s tick falls while rank 0 is sending: 3 of 12 trials left `watchdog.json` as `[(rank 0, attempt 0, no step, starting)]` and gave judged ranks `[1, 2, 3]` and the first group ranks `[1, 2]`, which is the CI result; the other 9 had no status file or a later snapshot and were right, as were 12 of 12 with the status written every 0.05 s. Rank 0 because it is the first to send. That the runner stalled about 10 s before the first send is inferred, not seen in the log. It reaches past the tests: at the start of any run the all-ranks view can call a rank an earlier attempt until the next status. Fix proposed, not made: an entry that has stored nothing (`last_step` null, not `attempt_without_steps`) is not compared. It cannot key on attempt 0, which is also the attempt of records that name none. The server's own rule compares in memory and is not affected.
  - *Failure 2: cause not found.* The lead first written here, an SDK daemon thread still in native code at shutdown, is withdrawn: on rank 0 `close` returned at once, where a join that times out waits 1 s; the worker's sink is a JSONL file, so no sender or heartbeat thread exists; and locally no SDK thread was alive after `close` with the failing sink or without, 20 runs each. Found while looking: a session stopped by a writer error is held by a reference cycle through the error's traceback, whose `_drain` frame holds the session. With the traceback kept, the session outlives its last reference and still holds the process group until a garbage collection; with it cleared, or after a normal close, the session is freed at once (measured with a one-rank gloo group and with the collector off). That is a leak worth fixing on its own, by keeping the error without its traceback and letting go of the group at close. It is not shown to cause the abort: lane X2's wrappers in this test keep the session alive on both ranks until shutdown. Not reproduced: macOS does not abort this way, and there is no Linux here. Next evidence proposed, in the test only: `faulthandler.enable` on the worker's stacks file, so an abort dumps every thread, and a line written at exit.
  - *The re-run.* Both failed jobs were run again with no change and passed: 2,619 passed and 87 skipped in the server job, 1,759 passed and 256 skipped in the Python 3.11 job. The run's conclusion is success.
  - *What the SDK-22 decision does and does not change here.* Removing the automatic gather retires the path the two-rank test exercises; it does not explain an abort at exit, which happens after the last gather returned.
  - *Not done.* No product code was changed. Failure 1's fix and its test, the leak's fix, and the test's extra diagnostics are proposed and await the author. Six test servers from other sessions' older runs were found still running on this machine and were left alone.

Kept as they were: another attempt that has stored steps, a new attempt marked `attempt_without_steps`, a claim of another attempt that has stored nothing, attempt 0 with steps stored, and an entry with no `last_step` key. The rule keys on both attempt 0 and nothing stored, because a claim of another attempt with nothing stored, on a server that did not know the rank, is still an earlier attempt's report.

- [x] **Decision for the SDK-22 wave: each rank also records an exact signature of each parameter; the fingerprints still decide the incident (Nov 20, 2026; decided, nothing built).** The author asked whether to refer to the sibling repositories for the wave, said "do it" to a closer reading of two of them, asked "which is better", and to "record the signature, fingerprints decide the incident?" answered "yes".
  - *What was read.* `lm-resiliency`, `detection/c3.py`: a tensor's bytes are read as 64-bit integers and folded into at most 512 chunks by an XOR salted differently at each level, so that a swap of two elements does not cancel; a final chain mixes in the byte count, then the number of dimensions, the shape and the dtype. The fold runs on the tensor's device and 4 KB is copied to the CPU. Its verdict rule (`classify_evidence`): all equal is `Agree`; a largest group of no more than half is `Inconclusive`; otherwise `Attributed`, with a bitmap of the ranks that differ. It gathers the signatures inside the job, which this project has decided not to do. TrainCheck: each process writes its own records (process ID, variable name and type, `new` or `update`, step and stage, attributes with a tensor hash, a nanosecond time), and its cross-process check matches values by the overlap of the times they were live, not by step. Its hash function was not read in detail.
  - *What is decided.* Every rank records, beside its float fingerprints, one exact signature for each compared parameter. `replica_drift` is decided by the fingerprints and the tolerance, as today. The evidence says whether the signatures agreed, and D1's attribution uses them when they disagree. Whether a signature mismatch alone becomes an incident is decided after the eight-GPU run shows what healthy replicas do.
  - *Why record it.* Float fingerprints can match for different weights (C14: swapped rows share a norm). Equal-or-not values make the majority count of D1 exact, where three ranks can each sit within a tolerance of a neighbour and still span more than it. Eight bytes a parameter a check; a field added after the format ships leaves old captures without it.
  - *Why it raises nothing yet.* That healthy replicas are bit-identical was not checked on a real multi-GPU run. Under plain DDP they should be; sharded parameters, mixed GPU types or a non-deterministic kernel could differ by a bit with nothing wrong, and a false `replica_drift` costs more than a missed one.
  - *For the record format,* from the same reading. Observations are matched by run, attempt and step, never by time: TrainCheck's matching by live time depends on each host's clock, which contract 1 does not trust. A parameter is identified by its name, with its shape and dtype in the record, so a position in a list is never the only identity. A 64-bit signature is written as a string, since a JSON number above 2^53 loses digits in some readers. The verdict has three values: agree, attributed, inconclusive.
  - *To measure before it is on by default.* The time to compute the signature on large parameters, against the published +0.70% a step for router-only capture. The signature code would be new here; `lm-resiliency` is BSD-3-Clause and its method is a reference, not copied code.
- [x] **Step 0 of the SDK-22 wave: the replica observation contract and its cases (Nov 20, 2026; committed, not pushed; no lane started).** The author approved step 0 from local `30ffae4` and held the lanes until the contract answers how a reader knows two ranks belong to the same start of a job, with four acceptance points: which observations are comparable, what the verdict means, what the fixtures prove, and what starts the latency clock.
  - *What was written.* `REPLICA_OBSERVATION_CONTRACT.md`: the `replica` key a rank adds on each scheduled step, the launch identity, the comparability checks, the four separate facts a reader reports (coverage, fingerprints, signatures, and D1's attribution later), the one rule that raises `replica_drift`, old captures, and when a verdict is due. `sdk/tests/fixtures/replica/`: 27 named cases in two files, 19 that raise nothing and 8 that raise exactly one incident, each with the result both engines must give. `sdk/tests/test_replica_contract.py` reads the rules a second time in code and checks every case against it.
  - *The restart question.* Each process makes its own attempt from its own clock (`_next_attempt`), so healthy ranks of one start have different attempts, and matching on attempt would leave a healthy run at insufficient coverage for good. The contract matches on `launch`: rank 0's attempt, shared through the process group's store in the exchange that already shares the run ID at `init`. A rank that cannot learn it records null, and the reader says the rows belong to no known start. The first draft preferred the launcher's restart count; that was dropped after reading `local_elastic_agent.py`: torchrun's count is `max_restarts` minus the restarts left, 0 on every fresh launch, so a job started again by hand under the same run ID would carry the same count twice and its old rows would pair with the new ones. The restart case's expected result: the earlier launch's step is complete, the new launch's step is insufficient with the missing rank named, and no drift is raised, though the two rows paired by step alone would read as drift (a test checks that they would).
  - *Three choices in it that are the author's to sign off.* (1) Launch comes from rank 0's attempt through the store, and from nothing else. (2) The projection is seeded from a parameter's name (`name-v1`), not its place in a list, so ranks that list parameters in another order agree; this changes the projection's values from today's, and the alternative was to record the place and verify it. (3) When one step holds both an explicit `record_replica_spread()` and the observations, the gathered spread decides the step whether it shows drift or not, and the observations are evidence only.
  - *Checked against the code.* Old records: the two legacy cases give the same verdict from the real `detect_replica_drift`. The spread's scale is the mean norm, never below `1e-12`, as `spread_from_fingerprints` has it. The key travels in `extra_json`, so the stream's schema and protocol number stay; a fingerprint that is not finite is written as a string, because the stream's encoder refuses a bare NaN. One observation survives a JSONL line, `record_to_row` and `row_to_record`, and a Parquet file. It was not sent through a live stream and a server.
  - *The latency clock.* It starts when the server has stored and acknowledged the batch with the last required observation, and stops when the status or report that carries the verdict is written; the bound is one status interval. The missing-rank proof uses a rank that is alive and training with its telemetry held back, since killing a rank would stall the training's own collectives.
  - *Size, an estimate.* One recorded capture names 290 parameters; with name, shape, dtype, three fingerprints and a signature for each, an observation is tens of kilobytes at every scheduled step. The writer lane measures it.
  - *Not done.* Nothing reads or writes the `replica` key. The lanes wait for the author's release.
- [x] **Step 0 revised after the author's review (Nov 20, 2026; committed, not pushed).** The author approved rank 0 as the launch authority and the name-seeded projection, narrowed the precedence of an explicit spread, and named two more faults in the contract and its reference test. Lanes 5 and 6 (readers; small fixes), which do not build against the contract, were released and started as sessions Z5 and Z6. Lanes 1 to 4 wait for the author.
  - *Launch is a nonce, not rank 0's attempt.* An attempt is wall-clock milliseconds with a counter that lives in one process (`_next_attempt`), so a job started again where the clock is behind can get the same number, and the old start's rows would pair with the new one's. Rank 0's process now makes 128 bits from the system's entropy once (`uuid.uuid4().hex`) and shares it beside the run ID under a key of its own, set before the run ID's: no new wait, the run ID's entry keeps its two items, and a rank on an older SDK still reads the run ID. The store exchange is in `tracker.py`, so the writer lane owns that file too. Case `two_launches_with_one_attempt_number`; with the first draft's form the same rows read as drift on `w`.
  - *`name-v1` is defined.* The seed is the first four bytes of the SHA-256 of the parameter's name (the `params` key, UTF-8, not normalised), big-endian, 32 bits; the weights are today's with that seed. Test vector on the page: `"layers.0.weight"` has seed 1749384365. Run: `_projection_weights` gives the same weights as a standard-library reading at seeds up to `0xFFFFFFFF`, with no overflow. `fold64-v1` is the writer lane's to define on the page to the same precision before it merges; a changed method gets a new name.
  - *An explicit spread decides only what it covers.* The reference looked a gathered spread up by step number alone, so a clean spread at step 99 of one launch silenced drift at step 99 of another. The record now says what a gathered spread covers (`replica_spread_scope`: launch and group), absent for a table the job passed in, an unreadable group, a rank with no launch, and every old record. A gathered spread decides a parameter only for the same run, launch, group and step, and only if its table holds that parameter. Any other keeps its own verdict and decides nothing else; when it and the observations both show drift on one step, both are listed, each with its source. Five cases, one for each way the scope can differ, and one for the double listing.
  - *Agreement needs a comparison.* With every parameter unread on every rank, the reference said fingerprints and signatures agree. Coverage now says only who sent a record; `agree` needs at least one parameter compared, and otherwise both facts read `not_compared` with why, in one spelling for both. A group of one rank is not compared. Such a step does not read as checked. Cases `every_parameter_unread`, `group_of_one`, `signature_method_differs`.
  - *Signatures.* Two different signatures under one method prove a difference; two equal ones do not prove equal bytes, since 64 bits can collide. The page said the second.
  - *Also made exact, since both engines must give the same list:* the order of steps (step, rows with no launch, launch, group) and of incidents (step, source, launch, group); groups that overlap without being equal, or a rank outside its own group, are `not_established`; an incident from observations names its launch and group; a reader's input is one run.
  - *How it was checked.* The nine new cases were first run against the reference as committed in `89bef4e`. Seven read wrongly there (four raised nothing, one raised once where two are due, two said agree with nothing compared); `signature_method_differs` differed only in how "not compared" is spelled; `two_launches_with_one_attempt_number` read rightly as written and reads as drift on `w` only with the first draft's launch form. At commit `511cff8` the fixtures held 36 cases and the test file 48 tests, all passing.
  - *Found after that commit: a fresh nonce can be read stale.* torchrun keeps one store across a restart of its workers, and the exchange's key is the same in every start. Run (PyTorch 2.15, torchrun, two workers on the loopback address, one restart; `stale_store_probe.py` in the session's scratchpad): the restarted rank 1 found the earlier start's entry before rank 0 had set the new one. The reference of `511cff8` then paired that rank's new row with the earlier start's and raised drift on `w`. Two rules were added: the writer reads the launch a second time, without waiting, before its first observation; and the reader allows a rank one attempt in a launch, the first keeping it and a later attempt's rows set aside as `not_established`. For the second rule the nonce is made at each `init`, not once in a process, and it is `secrets.token_hex(16)`, 128 bits (`uuid4` has 122). What the rules leave is in the contract's section 9: a second read that is still stale under a launch in which the earlier start's same rank never reported, and a store file left by a crash. The run ID travels in the same entry and is read the same way today: bug list, L25. In the probe's second start rank 0 printed nothing before the run was stopped; not looked into.
  - *Also after that commit.* A gathered spread with its scope now goes through the JSONL, row and Parquet round trip; the real `detect_replica_drift` is run on every record in the cases that holds a `replica_spread` (with a scope, without, beside observations), and gives each the verdict of its own record; today's weight function is checked far into a large tensor (element 5,000,000,000 at seed `0xFFFFFFFF`), where the sum inside wraps past 64 bits; two rules that had no case got one (`rank_outside_its_own_group`, `overlapping_groups_beside_a_third`); and the contract lists the words a result uses. 39 cases, 26 that raise nothing and 13 that raise; 53 tests pass. Not run: `ruff` (it is not in this environment). Still not done: nothing reads or writes the keys, and no observation was sent through a live stream and a server.

Six cases are pins, run against today's Python detectors. The other fourteen are targets for what is not built: four hold real records, and run on today's Python each reads differently from its target; the rest are stated in the page's words (calls, a status entry, a checkpoint's components) and are not something either engine can run as they stand.
  - *`session.step(skipped=...)`.* The record key is the one a loss scaler already writes, `step_skipped`, read from the code: the Python detectors already leave a skipped step out of `grad_spike`, `update_spike` and the update history, read only activations for `non_finite` there, and count skips for `loss_scale_trouble` (ten in a row raise it with no loss scale recorded; run here). The Rust detectors read the same column, seen by reading and not run. So the argument is mostly the SDK's: `skipped=False` writes `false`, `skipped=True` writes `true`, no call at a microstep ends no record, and an omitted argument is as today. One detector rule is new in both engines: a skipped step does not count toward `stalled_weights`, which does not read the key today.
  - *Unread gradients (decision 7), by what each kind is judged from.* `non_finite` stays `checked` and fires on anything that was read; `grad_spike` is `unavailable`, since its total leaves a parameter out, and a spike in the total as read still fires; `stalled_weights` is `checked` for the parameters read and names the unread one. The server does not read `grads_not_read` today, so this is new there.

  - *A rank with no peer (decision 24).* The server's `ranks_compared` lists a rank only when it was compared with another; `duplicate_batches` reads `not_configured` with the words "one rank observed, zero peers compared".
  - *C03.* The `rank_silent` or `job_stalled` incident of a rank whose capture had stopped keeps its kind and gains `capture_stopped` as the status entry holds it (a reason and how long ago; it holds no step) and `capture_last_step`, the last step the rank stored, and says TensorQuill cannot tell from that rank whether training itself stalled.
  - *Decision 9.* For the model component alone, when no entry is identified as the model's state, the finding is `not_identified` ("model state could not be identified"); `missing` is kept for B14's comparison.
  - *Left open on the page.* Whether a first step whose last rank is still on its way gets a short grace before the kind reads `unavailable`; whether the heartbeat should carry the step at which capture stopped.
  - *Run.* With both contract suites and `test_replica_contract.py`: 145 passed.

Unexplained; the test now reports how the driver ended.
  - *Not built.* Lane AC1's three small items, which move to the next wave on the author's earlier word. `compare.py` telling `first_divergence` its rows are resolved, which lane AC4 proposed and the author was not asked for.
  - *Not run.* Linux, PyTorch 2.6, NCCL, a GPU, a real FSDP or DDP job, CI on anything since `e531c36`.
  - *The author's instruction on the planning documents (Nov 21, 2026):* "commit just dont push md files", and on how, "just skip will decide later". They are committed here as before; how to push code without them is to be settled before the next push.

- [ ] **Wave AD planned and started (Nov 21, 2026; lane base `eb5a71b`, this entry on the branch `contracts/wave-ad`).** The author asked for every pending code change and bug in one wave, and approved the plan as a plan after four rounds of corrections. `main` stays at `eb5a71b` through the trial.
  - *The author's choices.* Scope: bugs, the agreed queue, reader gaps and measured costs, with Q1 to Q3 and B4 recorded as measurements with no incident, and a design page for K2. Both `activation_drift` and `moe_imbalance` change, by a contract signed first. Packaging follows the transfer of the repository to the organization. S5 stays out until its model and time limit are given.
  - *The author's corrections to the plan.* Rules are chosen from a development allowlist that leaves out Gate 1's six held-out cases and their source records, and every held-out check runs after the rules are frozen. RS10's stream behaviour is fixed by an end-to-end acceptance run before it is signed. Every residual of wave AC gets a disposition. An incremental `--watch` is held to a full recompute after each change to a run. The lane base and the signed contract are pinned by hash. Each lane is checked against its immediate base and the trial tree against `eb5a71b`. At the trial, the only differences in what Gate 1's arms are shown are those the author approved and recorded beforehand.
  - *Started on the author's word ("start all possible parallel sessions").* Six lanes cut from `eb5a71b`, each a session on Opus 5.5 at high effort in its own worktree: AD1 evidence readers, AD2 server ingest and watchdog, AD3 capture and probe, AD4 writer and identity, AD5 readers and MCP, AD6 driver, adapter and documents. Items that depend on the contract wait in each lane for the signed commit's hash. Stage 0 is this branch.
  - *Stage 0, measured (`experiments/rule-selection/RESULTS.md`).* Drift: a trailing baseline that never uses a module's first 50 records is quiet on all 28 healthy record sets of the allowlist (largest ratio 1.68 against a factor of 3) and fires on the three recorded GPU faults at step 254, as today; a higher factor on today's rule is quiet only because the largest healthy ratio here is 7.92, which grows with the length of training. Imbalance: not judging step 0 moves nanoMoE's incident to step 1; three flagged samples in a row misses the practice fixture's overload; excusing the state a router starts in for up to 10 samples is quiet on healthy nanoMoE, fires at step 7 without the balance loss, and reports every seeded collapse where today's rule does.
  - *Stage 0, on the author's second word.* The robust local trend of the sibling repository LLM-Training-Debugger, with actual step numbers, as a threshold-free measurement for Q1 and B4: it shows a sustained rise where there is one, a healthy run can rise more than a buggy one, it does not show B4's re-creation (that climb is over in three steps), and no capture on the allowlist has the six probes Q1 needs. The median-and-MAD score on TensorQuill's own captures: it separates the activation faults by a wide margin and does not separate the total gradient norm (a healthy Granite step scores 76, two seeded faults 31 and 54); a zero MAD happened in no real capture.
  - *RS10's acceptance run (`sdk/tests/test_rs10_acceptance.py`).* Two facts it established: a `DoPut` stream has one schema, so a batch refused for a key column is followed in its stream only by batches refused the same way; and a new stream's first answer names no offset, so the sender restarts from the offset it saved. Today the refused batch is sent again without end and the valid batch behind it is never stored.
  - *Written for signature, nothing built.* `DETECTOR_RULES_CONTRACT.md` with 36 cases, and `CROSS_RANK_ACTIVATION_DESIGN.md`. Held out of the contract: the per-parameter split of a pre-clipping total (L20), until lane AD3 has shown what the capture records when groups are clipped separately.
  - *Found while starting.* The Granite captures hold a router collapse seeded at step 450, so they are healthy for activations and seeded for routing. MPS is available on this Mac, which the bug list's C22 row did not have. The golden folder holds Gate 1's six held-out cases, so "the golden files" could not be a calibration set.
